## Scenario review 2026-10-06 (L3 core switch routing between IT and OT VLANs)

The app saw the symptoms (session IT/OT bypass, writes, switch hardening) but not the cause: the routing switch was invisible and the firewall was blamed. Owner decisions: LS-01 new check as proposed, LS-03 layer-2 evidence decides the carrier, Cisco switches only for now. **Fixed 2026-10-06**: all planted conditions detected; the false flat-segment, ip_conflict, LLDP and SVI unknown-device findings are gone.

| ID | Sev | Issue | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| LS-01 | Critical | L3 switch routing between IT and OT VLANs is invisible (SVIs / ip routing not parsed; no finding) | Fixed: `ip routing` + SVIs + ACLs parsed; the switch is an enforcement point (vendor cisco_l3_switch) with SVI levels from the firewall labels; check `switch_routes_between_zones` (critical IT ↔ L0-L2, high L3/DMZ ↔ L1-L2, medium otherwise) |
| LS-02 | High | Switch-routed flows reported as no permitting firewall rule; reachability denied | Fixed: the switch is a parallel path beside the firewall in reachability, the session evaluation and access paths; no false no-permitting-rule |
| LS-03 | High | Captured traffic credited to a firewall policy although routed by the switch (L2 source ignored) | Fixed: sessions keep the router MAC of their frames (network_sessions.carrier_mac, migration c8d9e0f1a2b3); evaluation, the access-path overlay and rule confirmation credit only the device that carried them |
| LS-04 | Med | Switch ACLs not parsed (wrong-direction ACL on an OT SVI not seen) | Fixed: switch ACLs as rules bound to SVIs (+ implicit deny); check `switch_acl_wrong_direction` |
| LS-05 | Med | Router MAC taken as the host's (same as MV-07) | Fixed with MV-07 |
| LS-06 | Med | Firewall interface address classified by VLAN inference (false flat_control_segment) | Fixed: MACs learned in several VLANs get no VLAN; firewall / switch interface addresses are never VLAN-inferred |
| LS-07 | Low | undocumented_lldp_device for the firewall's own interfaces | Fixed: H-15 exemption applied to undocumented_lldp_device (interface IPs and their MACs) |
| LS-08 | Low | Management SVI routed from every VLAN / two gateways in OT VLANs not reported | Fixed: checks `switch_mgmt_svi_unrestricted` and `switch_dual_gateway` |

## Audit 2026-10-06 (code audit of the mixed-vendor / L3-switch work; three independent reviewers, each finding re-verified by hand before fixing)

Scope: `l3_switch.py`, `fw_interfaces.py`, the MV changes in `firewall_parser.py`, the parallel enforcement point and carrier handling in `reachability.py` / `topology_diff.py` / `access_paths.py`, router-MAC handling in `pcap_parser.py` / `host_identity.py` / `mac_table_parser.py` / `device_classification.py`, the `alert_engine.py` changes, `mgmt_networks.is_jump_host`, the migration. Tests: tests/test_audit_mv_ls.py (16).

| ID | Sev | Finding | Where | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AU-01 | High | An ACL bound `in` on two SVIs came back from the Cisco ACL parser with the zone string `Vlan10,Vlan20`; the switch model matched zones by equality, so no ACE survived while the per-binding implicit deny was still written: every flow from those VLANs was "denied" by the switch. An ACL bound but not defined in the config produced the same deny-all | l3_switch.py `sync_switch` | **Fixed** - zones split on `,`; a binding with no parsed ACEs yields no rows and is reported in the ingest stats (`acls_not_found`) |
| AU-02 | High | `run_purdue_suggestions` (run checks, link confirmation, set-upstream) cleared `purdue_suggestion_source` on every interface, including the `svi_auto` marker of switch SVIs; `refresh_levels` then took them for operator labels and never re-derived them - relabelling a firewall interface left the SVI at the old level | topology_manager.py | **Fixed** - `svi_auto` rows are skipped by the suggestion engine (test_svi_levels_follow_relabelling_after_run_checks) |
| AU-03 | High (multi-site) | `refresh_levels`, `known_router_macs`, `known_networks`, `router_macs` and `switch_firewalls` were unscoped; the ingest paths (switch upload, PCAP upload) run outside `analysis_site`, so a second site took its SVI levels from the first site's firewall labels and a VRRP-style MAC known in one site made hosts of another site lose their MAC | l3_switch.py | **Fixed** - every helper filters on the current site (analysis site, else the bound ingest site) (test_switch_helpers_are_site_scoped) |
| AU-04 | Med | A switch named like a firewall of the site hit the `(site_id, name)` unique index inside the swallowed `try`, leaving the session in failed-flush state; the upload's VLANs, ports and hardening findings were lost with a 500 | l3_switch.py, switch_parser.py | **Fixed** - name lookup regardless of vendor, refused with `stats["l3"]["error"]`; the sync runs in a SAVEPOINT (test_switch_name_taken_by_a_firewall_does_not_break_the_upload) |
| AU-05 | Med | A generic (ASA / PAN / pfSense) upload under a FortiGate's name converted that FortiGate's Firewall row: vendor overwritten, its interfaces (with the operator's levels) and zone definitions deleted | fw_interfaces.py `sync_firewall` | **Fixed** - refused (`NameTakenByFortiGate`); the rules are stored, the interfaces skipped with `stats["interfaces_skipped"]` (test_generic_config_never_converts_a_fortigate_row) |
| AU-06 | Med | `router_macs` dropped any interface address owned by two Firewall rows as ambiguous - exactly an HA pair - so a cluster's router MAC never attributed a flow, and sessions ingested before the migration carry no carrier | l3_switch.py | **Fixed** - HA members are mapped to the cluster primary first (test_router_macs_treats_ha_members_as_one_device); the re-ingest requirement for old captures is documented |
| AU-07 | High | ASA `management-only` interfaces (the factory `Management0/0`) got implicit permits toward every lower interface: one false `rule_implicit_permit` per interface on most ASAs, and false "permitted" reachability through the management interface | fw_interfaces.py | **Fixed** - parsed, stored as `is_mgmt_only`, excluded as source and destination (test_asa_implicit_rules_edge_cases) |
| AU-08 | Med | The implicit model ignored outbound ACLs (`access-group X out interface dmz` still got an any/any implicit permit toward dmz), `access-group X global` (ends in an implicit deny), and `same-security-traffic permit inter-interface` (equal levels permitted, modelled as denied) | fw_interfaces.py `implicit_rules` | **Fixed** - no implicit row toward an interface with an outbound ACL (its ACE rows decide), none at all with a global ACL, equal-level pairs with same-security-traffic |
| AU-09 | Med | `policy_id` is `String(32)`: `implicit:<nameif>><nameif>` truncated at 32 collided between pairs (the review's `seen` set then dropped all but the first), and `<acl>:<seq>` truncation made `LongName:12` and `LongName:123` one id. The switch's `implicit:acldeny:PLC_PROTECT:Vlan50:in` (38 chars) was truncated in practice | fw_interfaces.py, firewall_parser.py, l3_switch.py | **Fixed** - implicit ids hash long pairs (`implicit_id`), ACE ids keep the sequence and hash a long ACL name (`_ace_id`), switch deny rows are `implicit:acldeny:<n>`; the review dedups on the interface pair (test_ace_ids_fit_and_stay_distinct) |
| AU-10 | Med | MV-04 made a Cisco ACE's identity its position; inserting an ACE above re-keyed every finding below it (acknowledgements lost), and a running-config paste vs `show access-list` output numbered the same ACE differently | alert_engine.py `_disabled_rule_group_key` | **Fixed** - finding identity of Cisco rows is `<acl>:<sha1 of the ACE text>` (the remark left out); the positional id stays for display and paths (test_cisco_finding_identity_survives_insertion) |
| AU-11 | Low | `switch_mgmt_svi_unrestricted`: any ACL on the management SVI suppressed it (an `in` ACL filters traffic FROM the VLAN), and an `in` ACL ending in `permit ip any any` on another SVI counted as a filter | l3_switch.py | **Fixed** - only an `out` ACL on the SVI suppresses; an `in` ACL counts when it does not end in a permit-any. |
| AU-12 | Low | An ACL-only re-upload of a device whose interfaces were uploaded before wrote the rules with `firewall_id NULL` (detached from the enforcement point), and `FirewallDevice.vendor` was not updated to `cisco_asa` on that path | firewall_parser.py | **Fixed** (test_acl_only_reupload_keeps_rules_attached) |
| AU-13 | Low | pfSense: an interface without `<enable/>` is disabled; it was stored "up" whenever it had an address, and fed the `opt*` macros | fw_interfaces.py | **Fixed** (test_pfsense_disabled_interface) |
| AU-14 | Low | PAN-OS multi-vsys: zone names repeat per vsys and overwrote each other; loopback / tunnel units were not interfaces (zone members pointed at nothing); the set-format parser skipped aggregate / loopback / tunnel | fw_interfaces.py | **Fixed** - zones keyed `<vsys>/<zone>` (stored with `vdom`), loopback / tunnel / vlan units parsed in both formats (test_panos_multi_vsys_zones_and_loopback) |
| AU-15 | Med | `is_jump_host` substring-matched the first matching source description: a jump host inside a declared management subnet was NOT a jump host (MV-10 lost), a subnet labelled "jump host segment" made every host one | mgmt_networks.py | **Fixed** - dedicated `jump_nets` (baseline role jump_host, trusted hosts of that role) and address membership (test_jump_host_membership_not_description_match) |
| AU-16 | Med | LS-05 merge of a MAC-only row into an IP-only row also ran for a routed binding the router detection missed (single remote host behind a router, proxy-ARP dumps): the router's switch-port row (VLAN, port, a manual classification) was folded into a remote host and deleted | host_identity.py `reconcile` | **Fixed** - no merge when the MAC carries several addresses in the capture or the MAC-only row is classified by hand (test_mac_only_row_not_folded_into_a_routed_binding) |
| AU-17 | Med | `carriers` was "last frame wins" per host pair: with two gateways (LS S6) or asymmetric routing the carrier depended on packet order, and a pair re-seen on one segment kept a stale router MAC | pcap_parser.py | **Fixed** - frames counted per router MAC (None included), the most frequent one wins deterministically; `carrier_mac` is also carried over when sessions are folded (host merge, NAT reconcile) (test_carrier_kept_on_session_fold) |
| AU-18 | Med | `multi_network_macs` made a MAC a router from one stray frame when its own address was never ARPed (the A-22 guard of `detect_gateway_macs` was missing); nested known networks (FG port1 /16 beside SVI Vlan10 /24) could count one network twice | l3_switch.py | **Fixed** - own address falls back to the first-seen one, two non-own addresses in two most-specific networks are required; added `routed_by` (two distinct routed sources: frames whose addresses lie in different known networks) which is what the MV capture needs |
| AU-19 | Med | Once any session carried a router MAC, every config-linked rule's traffic confirmation took the O(flows) scoped scan instead of the O(1) port lookup | alert_engine.py `_TrafficIndex.confirmed` | **Fixed** - cheap level / port check first, carrier check only on a hit |
| AU-20 | Low | Overlay: a switch-carried flow with no row through the switch fell back to all candidates and was credited to a firewall row; unexplained flows were split by carrier (two rows, two tag keys, for one flow). The topology-diff fallback (`_first_match_global`) ignored the carrier | access_paths.py, topology_diff.py | **Fixed** - no candidate through the carrier → unexplained; unmatched keyed by the 4-tuple; the fallback is limited to the carrier's rules |
| AU-21 | Low | ACL bindings were stored in `FirewallInterface.allowaccess` ("in:PLC_PROTECT"), which the Mgmt & Engineering page lists as admin-access protocols; `/topology/state` never showed them | l3_switch.py | **Fixed** - bindings derived from the stored rules (`bindings_of`); `allowaccess` stays empty for SVIs |
| AU-22 | Low | `_observed` (evidence sessions of a routed SVI) applied `limit(5000)` before the per-network filter, without an ORDER BY | l3_switch.py | **Fixed** - ordered, streamed, filtered |
| AU-23 | Low | `_carrier` did not exclude IPv6 link-local sources (a router's own RA / NS frames counted as "carried by itself"); `known_router_macs` knew only the interface IPs that have a host row with the MAC, so a FortiGate sub-interface absent from the ARP table was treated as a routed address | pcap_parser.py, l3_switch.py | **Fixed** - link-local skipped; every interface address of the owning device is its own |
| AU-24 | Low | Clearing a host's VLAN for a multi-VLAN MAC left its earlier `vlan_inferred` classification in place | mac_table_parser.py | **Fixed** |
| AU-25 | Low | Hygiene: `ctx.carrier_fw_id` undeclared on `_Context`, unused `Finding` import, tautological `self.traffic is not None`, convoluted vsys test in the PAN set parser, stale comments (deletion scope in `ingest_firewall_config`, "ACL bindings shown on the page"), MV-04 doc said the remark becomes the rule name (it is appended to the raw line; the ACL name stays the policy name for syslog hits) | various | **Fixed** |

Considered and left as designed: `switch_dual_gateway` reports OT networks (L0-L2) only - a second gateway in site operations (L3) is the medium `switch_routes_between_zones` finding; MV-10 exempts any L4+ source to a declared jump host (an internal IT admin reaching the jump host is the same sanctioned pattern as a remote vendor); clearing an SVI's level re-derives it (the operator sets a level to override, clears it to follow the firewall again); platforms where `ip routing` is the default and absent from the running-config are not modelled (the switch needs the line, or an SVI level set by hand - documented); `carrier_mac` is set only by PCAP ingest - captures uploaded before the switch ARP table (or before this version) carry no carrier until re-ingested (documented).

## Scenario review 2026-10-06 (multi-VDOM FortiGate: VDOMs it / ot / root in series over an inter-VDOM link)

The app resolved objects and policy ids per VDOM (N-24) but judged a flow that crosses two VDOMs by the most permissive one, and treated the inter-VDOM link as a zone. Owner decisions: VDOMs as enforcement points in series (VD-01/03), VPN sources at L5 (VD-05), VD-02a held open (closed 2026-10-08 by the capture point, see below). **Fixed 2026-10-06** (all but VD-02a): all planted conditions detected.

| ID | Sev | Issue | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| VD-01 | Critical | A flow crossing two VDOMs is judged by the most permissive VDOM (it permits + ot denies = permitted) | Fixed: per-VDOM enforcement points (that VDOM's policies), two VDOMs joined by an inter-VDOM link in series on every (level of A, level of B) boundary; chain reviews count VDOMs ("across 2 VDOMs of FG-VD") |
| VD-02 | High | Router MAC not recognised on single-talker taps / for the VPN pool: duplicate hosts, ip_conflict, false no-permitting-rule and unused-policy | VD-02b fixed: SSL-VPN tunnel pools count as known networks for router detection. **VD-02a fixed 2026-10-08** by the PCAP capture point (section *VD-02a: capture point of a PCAP upload*): the upload names the firewall / interface / segment and a single routed frame identifies the firewall MAC |
| VD-03 | High | Inter-VDOM link interfaces modelled as zones (false dmz_bypass, rows at every level, labelling offered) | Fixed: `vdom-link` interfaces are internal - level set = the peer VDOM's labelled levels (reachability and the twin resolver), never a leg or a zone; own section on the FW Topology page, no label accepted, no suggestion, no link proposal; policy twins skip link legs |
| VD-04 | Med | Config hardening checks read the merged VDOM view; same policy id in two VDOMs seen once (VPN policy lost → false config_vpn_no_routing_policy) | Fixed: VPN policies for the config checks come from the per-VDOM views |
| VD-05 | Med | VPN pool sources have no level: false session_mgmt_from_non_admin to the jump host, chain_ot_upward_skip at L1/L2 for ssl.it rows | Fixed: `ssl.<vdom>` tunnel interfaces L5 / outside automatically (`ssl_vpn_auto`, operator label wins); tunnel-pool hosts `remote_vpn_user` L5 (`vpn_pool`) ahead of every other mechanism, never moved by protocol inference (true findings were previously hidden by a pool host classified as L2 SCADA) |
| VD-06 | Med | config_possible_dual_homed for a host that appears in both VDOMs' policies because the flow crosses both | Fixed: zone keywords match whole name tokens, link interfaces skipped, per VDOM |
| VD-07 | Low | Management VDOM root evaluated as a forwarding VDOM | Fixed by VD-01: a VDOM without labelled interfaces is not an enforcement point |
| VD-08 | Low | Policy labels `it/40` vs `40 (vdom it)` | Fixed: config-check titles read `40 (vdom it)`; `it/40` stays the finding key |
| VD-09 | Low | config_unused_interface_not_disabled for a VLAN trunk parent (port4; also LS port3) | Fixed: VLAN trunk parents (and links) are never "unused" |

## Audit 2026-10-06 (full project review; six independent reviewers - API/routes, findings engine, reachability/NAT/access paths, firewall parsers, PCAP/switch/identity parsers, report/UI/migrations - every finding re-verified by hand before fixing)

Scope: the whole codebase. Groups: A = API / routes / middleware, B = alert engine / explain / chain findings, C = reachability / NAT / access paths, D = firewall and config parsers, E = PCAP / ARP / switch / host identity, F = report, UI, migrations, hygiene. A-01 (API auth) and H-01 stay deferred by decision; VD-02a stays open. Tests: `tests/test_audit_full.py` (19) plus three existing tests whose premise the fixes changed (D9 hostname precedence, B4 "no traffic = incomplete", B5 zone-doc auto-resolve). Full suite 2412 passed (2413 with the known-flaky health test deselected from the run); reachability hops carry a new `alternatives` key (C1-C3); schema drift 0; PostgreSQL offline render clean; UI smoke 16/16 and 7/7; `iasc_monitor.db` untouched.

| ID | Sev | Finding | Where | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | High | `DELETE /sites/{id}` purge left rows of the deleted site behind (access-path tags and hops, VPN user groups, management networks, firewall groups) and failed on the FirewallGroup foreign keys | routes.py `_purge_site_data` | **Fixed** - explicit deletes in dependency order, group references nulled first, plus a safety net over every `SiteScoped` mapper not yet handled |
| A2 | High | `DELETE /hosts/{id}` failed with a foreign-key error once the host had address history | routes.py `delete_host` | **Fixed** - history rows deleted first |
| A3 | Med | The body-size limit middleware sat outside CORS, so an oversized pre-flight / rejected upload came back without CORS headers (opaque error in the browser) | main.py | **Fixed** - limit registered before CORS so CORS is outermost |
| A4 | Med | `_record_ha` (HA cluster detection after a FortiGate upload) queried without the site scope: a peer of the same name in another site was paired | routes.py `_record_ha` | **Fixed** - runs under `analysis_site` |
| A5 | Med | `POST /reviews/run` without a site: the per-site loop re-used ORM objects across `_review_one_site` calls (stale identity map, one site's expunge detaching the next site's rows; DetachedInstanceError on the summary) | routes.py `run_periodic_review` | **Fixed** - plan `(id, name, info)` captured first, `expunge_all()` between sites |
| A6 | Med | Device / firewall name, device type, role and attestation author query parameters had no length limit (a 1 MB name reached the parsers and the finding titles) | routes.py ingest / classify / attest routes | **Fixed** - `max_length` 64 / 255 |
| A7 | Low | `/management/...` accepted a `finding_id` of another site (404 only when the finding did not exist at all) | routes.py `_mgmt_site` | **Fixed** - 404 when the finding's site differs |
| A8 | Low | Docstring of the firewall-config upload described `purdue_zone_from/to` as ordered | routes.py | **Fixed** - "in either order" |
| A9 | Low | `/topology` counted the group-marker host and VIP pseudo-hosts as hosts | routes.py `/topology` | **Fixed** - `real_host_clause()` |
| A10 | Low | `/site-devices` body typed `site: str` while the UI sends an int | routes.py | **Fixed** - `str \| int` |
| B1 | High | Firewall-group drift compared the first expanded row of a policy only: a member whose policy differed in a second address / service member was reported "in sync" | firewall_groups.py `_rules` / `drift` | **Fixed** - row bodies compared as sets, `rows_primary` / `rows_member` in the evidence |
| B2 | High | `ha_system_error` matched the words "error"/"fail" anywhere in an event (including a policy name or a URL) and keyed one finding per hour regardless of what failed | alert_engine.py `check_ha_system_error` | **Fixed** - phrase-based regex on `logdesc` / `msg` / `reason` / `status` only; `kind` in the dedup key and evidence |
| B4 | High | `review_unused_policies` with no sessions and no rule hits at all marked every permit "unused" (a data gap presented as a finding storm) | alert_engine.py | **Fixed** - review marked incomplete ("no observed sessions and no rule hits") instead |
| B5 | Med | `zone_doc_1_1..1_6` never auto-resolved although they are re-derived from the current hosts / zones every run | alert_engine.py `_AUTO_RESOLVE_CHECKS` | **Fixed** |
| B6 | Med | Within one run, a finding re-detected by two checks (or two evidence rows) was created, then found and "re-detected" again - double counts in `findings_created` and a second `updated_at` bump; escalation could lower the severity back | alert_engine.py `_make_finding` | **Fixed** - run-scoped `_RUN_FINDINGS_KEY` cache: second sight only escalates |
| B7 | Med | The explanation pop-up said "auto-closes when no longer detected" for five checks that are never auto-closed (denied_trend, ntp_sync, single_path_critical_device, no_path_redundancy_verified, firmware_not_documented) | explain.py | **Fixed** - `_NEVER_AUTO_CLOSED`; severity reason for L2 bypass and DMZ-source cases |
| B8 | Low | Chain-finding severity reasons said "High" while the finding was medium (and vice versa) | chain_findings.py | **Fixed** - label from the computed severity |
| B9 | Low | `check_it_ot_bypass` subnet heuristic (no level on either host, inferred from the /24) raised critical; the docstring claimed otherwise | alert_engine.py | **Fixed** - medium with `severity_reason` |
| B10 | Low | `traffic_findings._details()` loaded every ProtocolDetail row of the site at once | traffic_findings.py | **Fixed** - keyset generator, 2000 rows a batch |
| B11 | Low | `last_matched` flush inside a nested transaction rolled the outer SAVEPOINT back on an IntegrityError | topology_diff.py | **Fixed** - nested SAVEPOINT when already nested |
| B12 | Low | `review_mgmt_access_not_restricted` counted unknown-level hosts across sites | alert_engine.py | **Fixed** - site-scoped count |
| C1 | High | Two VDOMs of one FortiGate in series were evaluated as one enforcement point when a flow entered through a VDOM that had no labelled leg of its own (transit VDOM); the chain stopped at the first VDOM | reachability.py `_add_vdoms` | **Fixed** - BFS over the inter-VDOM link graph builds a series chain through transit VDOMs (`series_group`) |
| C2 | High | A VDOM reachable both directly (its own labelled interface) and through a link was evaluated by whichever enforcement point was built last; the alternatives were not OR'd | reachability.py | **Fixed** - `hop_alternatives()`: EPs sharing a `series_group` AND'd, groups OR'd, boxes AND'd; verdict = union over alternatives |
| C3 | High | Shared-level case (both VDOMs own an interface at the same level): the intra-VDOM route was not considered, so a permitted flow read "denied" through the link chain | reachability.py | **Fixed** - intra-VDOM EP is an alternative of the chain (`$S/rev/vdom_cases.py` cases 1-4 verified) |
| C4 | Med | The NAT retry filter in access paths kept a hop when ANY enforcement point permitted, even one that is in series with a denying one | access_paths.py | **Fixed** - `_hop_alternatives_permitted(hop)` built on the `alternatives` lists |
| C5 | Med | SNAT / published-address NAT entries were keyed `(firewall, policy id)`: policy ids repeat per VDOM (N-24), so one VDOM's pool applied to another's policy | nat_paths.py `NatIndex.build` / `apply_snat`, nat_sessions.py `_Ctx` | **Fixed** - keyed `(firewall, vdom, policy id)` |
| C6 | Med | `nat_sessions.reconcile` searched every session for the twin of every SNAT'd session (O(n²); 40 s at 20 k sessions) | nat_sessions.py | **Fixed** - sessions indexed by `(dst host, port)`, SNAT lookups memoised |
| C7 | Med | The per-context evaluation cache is keyed by concrete host addresses and grew without bound when one context served every flow of a big capture | reachability.py | **Fixed** - bounded (`_EVAL_CACHE_MAX` 20 000, oldest half dropped) |
| C8 | Low | A single firewall with extra legs lost the explicit `(zone_from, zone_to)` boundary when the leg set did not include it | reachability.py `_add` | **Fixed** - union |
| C9 | Low | `parse_address_spec("a.b.c.d 255.255.255.255")` (ASA / pfSense host netmask) was read as the Cisco "match everything" wildcard → `0.0.0.0/0` | rule_match.py | **Fixed** - /32 unless the address is 0.0.0.0 |
| D1 | High | Re-uploading a FortiGate config replaced the rule rows and lost `owner` / `conduit` written by the reviewed-policies import and `last_matched` stamped by the engine | forti_config_parser.py | **Fixed** - snapshot per (vdom, policy id) and re-applied (`rules_carried`); the config's own comment still wins for owner |
| D2 | High | An ASA / IOS object-group with no members, or a PAN-OS group whose members are all cyclic, expanded to nothing and the rule's src × dst product was empty: the rule vanished silently | firewall_parser.py `_memo_store` | **Fixed** - `unresolved:<name>` token, `empty_object_groups` counter and a warning |
| D3 | High | The FortiGate config checks judged one representative row per policy - the first expanded row - so a policy with `srcaddr "host" "all"` passed the any-any check when the host row came first | forti_config_parser.py `_config_findings` | **Fixed** - representative row is "any" in a field when any row is; every row kept in `_rows` |
| D4 | Med | `_extract_vpn_settings` compared `str(srcintf)` with "ssl.": a policy with two source interfaces was never a VPN policy | forti_config_parser.py | **Fixed** - `_vpn_names` |
| D5 / F1 | Med | `too-large:<group name>` and long policy names / ports exceeded `VARCHAR` lengths (SQLite ignores them, PostgreSQL raised) | models.py | **Fixed** - every mapped class clips string values to the column length at flush (`_clip_strings` listener) - also covers D10 / F3 (syslog event fields, FortiOS policy ids) |
| D6 | Med | `config_overly_broad_subnet` findings of one policy (src and dst) collapsed into one Finding row (same source_ref) | forti_config_parser.py | **Fixed** - `ref_key` `pid:field:subnet` |
| D7 | Med | `ingest_approved_hosts` stored MACs / IPs as typed (`AA-BB-..`), so the approved-host comparison against normalised Host rows never matched; multicast rows were accepted | baseline_parser.py | **Fixed** - `_host_identity` (A-23) normalisation, `skipped_bad_identity` |
| D8 | Med | FortiOS `set subnet a.b.c.d/nn` (one token, CIDR form) was stored as `a.b.c.d/nn/32` | forti_config_parser.py | **Fixed** - `ip_network` |
| D9 | Med | The upload route passed the file-name stem as the firewall name, so the parser never fell back to `set hostname`: two uploads of one firewall under different file names made two Firewall rows | routes.py / forti_config_parser.py | **Fixed** - config hostname before the file name (`fallback_name`) |
| D11 | Med | pfSense / PAN-OS XML parsed with `xml.etree` (entity expansion) | firewall_parser.py, fw_interfaces.py | **Fixed** - `defusedxml.ElementTree.fromstring` (dependency added) |
| D12 | Low | `parse_syslog_any` swallowed a vendor parser's exception as "no vendor matched" | syslog_vendors.py | **Fixed** - logged with the vendor and the line |
| E-H1 | High (multi-site) | Switch hardening findings were raised and auto-closed across sites: a switch of the same name in site B closed site A's findings | switch_hardening.py | **Fixed** - runs under `analysis_site(scope_site_id)` |
| E-H2 | High | Addresses a MAC claimed only in ARP / NDP (a router's interface address, a host that only answered ARP) never reached the identity resolver, so the renumbering / conflict they imply was missed | pcap_parser.py | **Fixed** - merged into the bindings |
| E-M1 | Med | The prescan address tables grew with the frame count on a synthetic capture (random MACs) | pcap_parser.py `_prescan` | **Fixed** - capped (65 536 MACs, 4 096 addresses per MAC), `prescan_macs_dropped` / `prescan_ips_dropped` in stats + warning |
| E-M2 | Med | ARP table rows for multicast (224.0.0.x), the subnet broadcast and junk addresses became hosts | arp_parser.py | **Fixed** - group MACs / non-host IPs dropped |
| E-M3 | Med | PROFINET DCP identify-all / RT multicast frames (dst 01:0e:cf:..) created a Host per group MAC | pcap_parser.py | **Fixed** - recorded sender → group marker (B-14a pattern), `profinet_group_packets`, `dst_group` in the detail |
| E-M4 | Med | LS-05 "router-like" test counted addresses of every family: a dual-stack host (IPv4 + fe80 + global IPv6) was never folded into its IP row | host_identity.py | **Fixed** - same family and scope only |
| E-M5 | Med | HP port-list ranges expanded unbounded (`1-999999999`) | switch_parser.py | **Fixed** - range ≤ 1 024 wide, port ≤ 9 999, list ≤ 4 096 |
| E-L1 | Low | `VpnContext.build` read every site's tunnels / pools in ingest-time callers | vpn.py | **Fixed** - filtered by the bound ingest site |
| E-L3 | Low | Dead `_prescan_mac_ips` | pcap_parser.py | **Fixed** - removed |
| F2 | Med | Deleting a firewall group from the UI did not carry the selected site (`SITE_OP_RE` missed `/topology/firewall-groups/{id}`) | ui/index.html | **Fixed** |
| F4 | Low | GIN indexes on `findings.detail` / `protocol_details.fields` existed in a migration but not on the models (schema drift on PostgreSQL) and served no query | migrations | **Fixed** - dropped (`d9e0f1a2b3c4`) |
| F5 | Low | `ot_upward_skip` access-path flag had no check mapping in the report (no SR / checklist shown) | generate_report.py | **Fixed** |
| F7 | Low | Stale docstrings (Baseline claimed a `baseline_id` on FirewallRule; `findings_category_reference` counted checks that no longer match) | models.py, routes.py | **Fixed** |
| F8 | Med | `generate_report.py` on a multi-site database without `--site-id` / `--all-sites` merged every site's findings into one report | generate_report.py | **Fixed** - refused with the site list (exit 2) |
| F9 | Med | Removing a routing switch (`l3_switch._remove`) left `PcapCapture.firewall_id / interface_id / suggested_interface_id` and `FirewallDevice.firewall_id` pointing at the deleted rows (FK error on PostgreSQL) | l3_switch.py | **Fixed** - nulled |

Considered and left as designed: F6 (report per-check limit) - the count and the "… N more" line were already present; the C-group notes on the `ALL_TCP` token, the `nat_exempt` skip and the O(n²) compose step (bounded by the per-site path cap); B12's port-set differences between the two management checks (documented); a finding's title is not refreshed on de-escalation (the next run rewrites it); E-L4 pcapng option blocks; F10 informational.

## Real-config review 2026-10-07 (FortiGate 100F, FortiOS 7.4.11, single VDOM, 29 policies)

First production config through the app. The report it produced (99 findings, 27 pages) was misleading in places and missed the exposures a manual review found; DR-01..DR-12 fix that. Re-run of this config only (fresh database, config upload + review run): 85 open findings, 26 pages, every policy present, no known false positives; the report now states that 29 level-dependent checks could not run until interface levels are assigned. Three existing expectations updated (e2e snapshot DR-05, rule-twins port list DR-04). Full suite 2423 passed; schema drift 0; PostgreSQL offline render clean; `iasc_monitor.db` untouched.

| ID | Sev | Finding | Where | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DR-01 | High | Coverage table listed IT/OT bypass, chain, DMZ-bypass, egress and remote-access checks as "no findings" (reads as a pass) when no interface had a Purdue level | generate_report.py | **Fixed** - new input "Purdue levels on firewall interfaces" (operator / derived levels; the automatic L5 of a VPN tunnel does not count); 29 checks are "no data" without it; executive summary says so in a paragraph |
| DR-02 | High | Policies whose destination is only FQDN objects produced no rule rows - 11 of 29 policies (all internet egress: MQTT brokers, jump host, proxies, AWS IoT, NTP, iLO → proxy with service ALL) were invisible to every analysis; 41 `fqdn_in_policy` findings instead (one per object) | forti_config_parser.py, rule_match.py | **Fixed** - FQDN values kept as `fqdn:` / `wildcard-fqdn:` rows; `is_external_addr_token` treats them as external internet unless under an internal suffix (.local, .corp, ...); one `fqdn_in_policy` finding per policy listing its objects |
| DR-03 | High | Dial-up IPsec was not remote access (only `ssl.*` names were): the vendor VPN tunnel had no level, its pool no `remote_vpn_user` class, its user groups were not in the VPN group review, so vendor Modbus straight to the controllers could never raise `vpn_direct_to_ot` | forti_config_parser.py, vpn.py, segments.py | **Fixed** - a `type dynamic` phase1-interface with mode-cfg / EAP / XAUTH / user group is remote access: tunnel L5 / outside automatically, new column `firewall_interfaces.remote_access` ("ssl" / "ipsec_dialup", migration e0f1a2b3c4d5), `remote_tunnels()` / `zone_is_remote()` used by VpnContext, group discovery, the VPN-to-OT re-judge and segment exposure. Site-to-site and spoke tunnels unchanged |
| DR-04 | Med | `rule_insecure_protocol` (and the cleartext / IT-service / management port checks) ignored TCP vs UDP: an ICMP+UDP policy "permitted Telnet, FTP, SMTP, HTTP, POP3, IMAP" | alert_engine.py `_ports_matched` | **Fixed** - single-transport services matched only for a rule of that transport |
| DR-05 | Med | Settings left at the FortiOS default are omitted from backups; the checks read absence as "not configured" (lockout, idle timeout) and read the wrong keys (`admin-idle-timeout`, `maintainer-admin`, `min-length` instead of `admintimeout`, `admin-maintainer`, `minimum-length`) | forti_config_parser.py | **Fixed** - real keys (old ones still read), defaults applied (lockout 3, admintimeout 5, minimum-length 8, admin-maintainer enable); syslog TCP read from `set mode reliable` |
| DR-06 | Med | VLAN sub-interfaces exported without `set type vlan` were typed physical; `config_unused_interface_not_disabled` fired for bond0 (trunk), its LACP members and the DHCP WAN links | forti_config_parser.py | **Fixed** - type inferred from `vlanid` + parent; in use = aggregate / redundant member, tunnel parent, policy / zone / route / phase1 reference, DHCP / PPPoE addressing |
| DR-07 | Low | Rule counts were expanded rows: "1830 of 1830 permit rules have no owner", "1830 active rules" in the inventory | forti_config_parser.py, generate_report.py | **Fixed** - counted per policy, row count shown beside it |
| DR-08 | Med | Local-in policies were not read: accept-ALL entries on both WAN links (from a management address group) and on the switch-management VLAN were invisible | forti_config_parser.py | **Fixed** - new check `config_local_in_permits_all` (5.8; high when outside interface and source all) |
| DR-09 | Med | IPsec authentication not checked: site-to-site tunnels accepting any peer certificate; dial-up on a shared pre-shared key | forti_config_parser.py | **Fixed** - new checks `config_ipsec_peer_unrestricted` (medium) and `config_ipsec_dialup_psk` (low with EAP / XAUTH / user group, else medium), both 4.2 |
| DR-10 | Low | The syslog finding did not say where the logs go: here a server inside a network the firewall protects | forti_config_parser.py | **Fixed** - server and the protected interface / network in the title and evidence |
| DR-11 | Low | A custom service named for one transport but defined as the other (`UDP_3900` = tcp 3900) went unnoticed | forti_config_parser.py | **Fixed** - new check `config_service_protocol_mismatch` (3.3, low) with the policies that use it; RPF check skips FortiOS system tunnels (naf / l2t / ssl) |
| DR-12 | Low | Report cover showed "Site Name: (not specified)" unless `--site` was passed | generate_report.py | **Fixed** - defaults to the site's name |

Still needs operator input for this site (not code): interface Purdue levels, the management-network declaration (vlan_20), an approved-hosts baseline, attestations 1.1-1.6, and a capture or syslog export for the session / real-time checks.

## Docker build synced to the tested set 2026-10-07 (SQLite)

Guide: docs/docker-install.md (install, first use, verification, backup / restore, upgrade, offline install, debugging, troubleshooting, verification record).

| ID | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|
| DK-01 | The image installed the old pins (fastapi 0.115, sqlalchemy 2.0.35, scapy 2.5, uvicorn[standard] 0.30, ...) - never the versions the suite ran on (fastapi 0.141.1, starlette 1.0.0, sqlalchemy 2.1.1, scapy 2.7.0, plain uvicorn 0.46.0, ...) | **Fixed** - `requirements.txt` pins the direct dependencies to the tested versions; `requirements-lock.txt` is the complete tested set, installed by the image with `--no-deps`; `requirements-dev-lock.txt` for the test tools; `install.sh` uses the lock too |
| DK-02 | Base pinned to Python 3.11.10 while testing ran on 3.11.15 | **Fixed** - `python:3.11-slim-bookworm` (cp311 wheels), digest pinning documented |
| DK-03 | Uploads spooled to `/tmp` in the container layer; no `VOLUME`, so a plain `docker run` kept the database in the container | **Fixed** - `TMPDIR=/data/tmp` (cleared at start), `VOLUME ["/data"]` |
| DK-04 | No backup procedure (a file copy of a WAL database can be inconsistent) | **Fixed** - `backup_db.py` (SQLite online backup + integrity check, `--keep N`) |
| DK-05 | No way to debug or run the suite in the shipped image | **Fixed** - `dev` build target + `docker-compose.dev.yml` (bind mounts, `--reload`, `./data`, no restart) |
| DK-06 | `# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1` fetched the Dockerfile frontend from Docker Hub at every build (fails offline / behind restrictive registries); unwritable bind-mounted `/data` gave an obscure crash | **Fixed** - built-in BuildKit frontend; entrypoint checks `/data` and says how to fix ownership; optional build secret for a TLS-inspecting proxy's CA |
| DK-07 | Found while verifying: scenario tests classified one host row per address; with the open VD-02a duplicate (a routed frame creating a second row with the firewall's MAC) the row picked depended on `last_seen` ordering, i.e. on the wall-clock time against the fixed capture timestamps | **Fixed (tests)** - every row of the address is classified; VD-02a itself fixed 2026-10-08 (capture point) |

Verification (record in the guide, section 15): image packages identical to the lock, `pip check` clean; suite inside the `dev` image 2419 passed / 4 skipped (need the operator's legacy DB), host 2423 passed; empty-volume start migrates to head; a real FortiGate config + reviews + PDF in the container = host result; 200,000-packet PCAP; UI smoke 16/16 + 7/7 against the runtime image; persistence across restart, down/up and a Docker daemon restart; backups; dev override with reload. Built on a stand-in base (same Python 3.11.15, no third-party packages) because the sandbox cannot reach Docker Hub; the official base pull is the one step not exercised here.

## Audit 2026-10-08 (multi-firewall access control → Purdue levels; four independent reviewers, every finding re-run by hand; no code changed)

Full report with evidence and the recommended fix order: docs/multi-firewall-purdue-audit.md. Verdict: sound for a linear Purdue stack (one firewall / one declared series pair / one VDOM chain per level boundary, every interface labelled); not yet effective where two firewalls share a level pair but not a wire (cell / area / per-plant firewalls, a direct 4↔3 beside a DMZ chain, a 3-leg DMZ firewall beside a 2-leg one), and label-dependent without safeguards.

| ID | Sev | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| MP-01 | High | Distinct firewalls on one level pair were AND-ed; placement never selected enforcement points → false "denied" via an uninvolved firewall's implicit deny, access-path rows and `chain_it_ot_bypass` findings lost, false `session_no_permitting_rule`, flows attributed "seen" to firewalls they never crossed | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** Boxes on one boundary are alternatives (OR) unless declared in series: paired group, confirmed upstream chain (`topology_confirmed`), or confirmed inside↔outside link (`reachability._Context._assign_series_keys`, `hop_alternatives`). Host queries drop boxes whose labelled interface networks hold fewer of the flow's addresses than another alternative, and the carrier MAC selects between boxes (`_select_alternatives`; hop `placement`, EP `not_on_path`). `enforcement_points[*]` gain `arrangement` + `alternatives[{devices, basis}]`, hops gain `arrangement`. Access-path rows come one per alternative; `apply_overlay` credits a flow to the accepting row whose firewall holds its endpoints. UI: "FW-A \| FW-B [parallel]" / "FW-A + FW-B [series]" on the enforcement-point line, hop chip, "not on path" marker. Tests `tests/test_mp01_parallel.py` (7); `test_ha_cluster_is_one_enforcement_point` and `test_permit_plus_unloaded_device_on_same_boundary_*` updated to the OR semantics. MP-02 (box reused on a later boundary) remains open. |
| MP-02 | High | `_uses_box_twice` dropped a whole path when a multi-leg box reappeared on a later boundary instead of removing that box's EP | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `_path_between_levels` now attaches per-hop `excludes` (physical boxes crossed earlier on the path) to each `_Path`; `_hop_eps` / `_evaluate_hop` / `_evaluate_hop_nat` / `_combine_path_scoped_nat` / `_path_nat_annotation` honour them (cache keys include the exclusion). A path is dropped only when a hop has no box left. Hops report `already_crossed` (names), shown in the UI hop detail. A 3-leg FW-C (4/3.5/3) beside FW-A (4/3.5) → FW-B (3.5/3): 4→3 is now permitted via A→B and the rows `[FW-A, FW-B]`, `[FW-C, FW-B]` exist. Tests in `tests/test_mp01_parallel.py` (3 more). |
| MP-03 | High | An unlabelled firewall built no EP, a "nearest" flat edge was inferred in its place, a flow it denies was reported permitted; the inferred hop was not shown; `unlabelled_firewalls` stayed empty | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `_Context.unplaced_firewalls` (`_find_unplaced`): every firewall / device with enabled rules and no enforcement point, with `reason`, `rule_count`, `labelled_levels`, `unlabelled_interfaces`; returned by `GET /reachability`, `/reachability/path`, `/access-paths` (`unplaced_firewalls`), shown as a banner on the Reachability and Access Review pages, in the report's executive summary and permitted-path section; `no_policy` reasons name them. `_flat_downgrade`: with an unplaced firewall any verdict over an inferred hop is `unknown` (`inferred_hop_note` on paths and cells, "Why unknown" in the UI); a guessed hop still never yields `denied`. `_infer_flat_edges` only joins levels that have hosts, a boundary or a labelled interface (no more baseless 4→0 cells). `_path_string` prints inferred hops (`4 → [3↔4] → ~ 2 (inferred, no firewall)`). Deliberate deviation from the audit's "never better than unknown": with every firewall placed, a nearest-guess hop keeps the firewall hop's permit (A-25), now visibly flagged, so an L2 segment behind an L3 firewall still gets an answer. Tests `tests/test_mp03_unplaced.py` (6); `test_reachability_two_firewall_chain_through_dmz` updated (empty L2 → `no_policy`). |
| MP-04 | High | Paired (series) group never consulted by reachability; mismatched member labels dropped a member from the path (and a stray 3.5 turned on site-wide `dmz_bypass`); `cross_fw_asymmetric_permit` ignored a paired group with no confirmed link | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** Reachability consults the group since MP-01 (`series_key`). `declare_paired` refuses members placed on different level pairs (422 naming both, with the far-side labelling convention); `firewall_groups.paired_group_warnings` (level_mismatch / unplaced_member / unlinked) is returned as `paired_group_warnings` by `/reachability`, `/reachability/path`, `/access-paths` and shown as banners on the Reachability and Access Review pages. `_run_cross_fw_checks` compares paired members with no confirmed link over their shared-network transit interfaces, else over whole rulesets (`_GroupIface`), reported as `paired_unlinked`. docs/firewall-groups.md updated. Tests `tests/test_mp04_paired.py` (7). |
| MP-05 | High | `_RuleZoneResolver` still split zone names on whitespace (C-05 fixed only reachability): multi-word zone → rule unevaluated, no twin findings | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `alert_engine._split_zone_value` splits on commas only (each element stripped of quotes/brackets, case kept), the same list rule as `fortios_zones.zone_tokens`; a policy `port1 (L4) → "OT Zone" (L1)` now resolves `({4}, True) / ({1}, True)` and raises `rule_it_ot_bypass`. Test `test_mp05_rule_twins_resolve_space_zone_names_whole` in `tests/test_audit_c_fortios.py`. |
| MP-06 | Med | Network-scoped sources not placed by level: impossible rows (L3 / L3.5 / L2 "10.4.0.0/24 → L1") and 3× bypass findings per path | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `access_paths._scope_level` places a scope like a host: a single host's level, else the most specific labelled interface network containing the whole scope. `_src_elsewhere` (enumeration) and `chain_findings._foreign_source` (chain reviews, dead ends) use it, so a row whose source lives at another level is not produced. The 4-firewall `/24` probe: 4 rows and 1 `chain_it_ot_bypass` (was 10 rows, 3 findings). Tests `tests/test_mp06_07_10_19.py`. |
| MP-07 | Med | Prefix / dead-end rows shown and reported as real paths with wrong-level flags (`dmz_bypass` rows with no finding) | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `_enumerate` classifies every row (`classify_reach`) and sets `row["reach"]` = reaches / prefix / dead_end; prefix and dead-end rows keep only flags that are not judged by level (`permit_any`, `insecure_protocol`, `direction_unverified`, NAT…) - the level-judged ones move to `flags_at_reached_level` - and `report_include_default` is False for them. `counts.by_reach` added; UI shows a "prefix" / "dead end" badge. The review screen no longer shows `dmz_bypass` where the findings engine raises none (NT: 3 rows, MV: 4, VP: 90, MF: 19 re-marked; findings unchanged). Tests `tests/test_mp06_07_10_19.py`. |
| MP-08 | Med | Truncation removed IT → OT rows first (matrix order) and the chain reviews never recorded it | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `compute_reachability` enumerates cells downhill first, deepest first (5→0, 5→1, …, 4→0, …), then upward, so a truncated access-path list loses IT → OT rows last. `chain_findings._note_truncated` calls `_record_truncation` (every chain review shows "access-path enumeration" in `last_review_info()["truncated"]`) and the new `review_access_path_truncation` raises one informational `access_path_enumeration_truncated` finding per site (budget, rows listed; auto-resolves when a run completes). With the audit's budget of 45 the probe now keeps 15 + 15 chain findings instead of 0. Tests in `tests/test_mp08_12_24.py`. |
| MP-09 | Med | A known-but-unlabelled interface / zone member in a rule side meant "any leg", unflagged | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** Reachability loads the names / aliases / labels of interfaces that exist without a level (`_Context._iface_unlabelled`). A side naming one is "another leg" on a multi-leg box (its labelled legs decide; only unlabelled → matches nothing on that crossing) and uncertain (`_SIDE_UNKNOWN`) on a partly labelled explicit boundary; a wholly unlabelled explicit boundary keeps D-11 (both ways, `direction_unverified`). Skipped permits are listed per enforcement point as `rules_skipped_unlabelled` (UI: "Permits on unlabelled interfaces, not judged here"), unresolved notes name the interface, and `enforcement_points[*].unlabelled_interfaces` lists them. The twin resolver marks such sides incomplete and `names_unlabelled_interface` in evidence. Tests in `tests/test_mp09_14_15_resolvers.py`. |
| MP-10 | Med | Specific denies before a permit-any not subtracted across hops; `denied_services` dropped by `_union_verdicts` | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `_Verdict.denies` carries a device's full-address specific denies as (protos, ports) pieces; `_combine_verdicts` subtracts them from the path's permitted pieces (`_subtract_denies`: a deny applies when its protocols cover the piece's - a narrower deny stays text in `denied_services`); `_union_verdicts` keeps the denies common to every alternative (text and pieces). Enforcement-point evaluations expose `denied_pieces`, and the access-path enumeration subtracts them per stage (`_walk_stages_nat`). FW-A permits 443,502; FW-B denies 502 then permits any: cell 4→2 = partial [tcp/443] denied [tcp/502], host path tcp/502 denied, the row lists tcp/443 only (no Modbus bypass). Tests `tests/test_mp06_07_10_19.py`. |
| MP-11 | Med | Bypass severity by destination band only; L3 "IT side" for bypass but OT for egress / NAT; internet → PLC = SCADA → PLC | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `chain_findings.bypass_severity`: (source band, destination band) table - internet→L0/L1 critical, internet→L2 / enterprise→L0/L1 high, enterprise→L2 / DMZ→L0/L1 / site→L0/L1 medium, DMZ→L2 / site→L2 low (one step up when seen); the finding text and `severity_reason` name the source band ("from site operations (L3) - a server that should talk to the control zone only through a conduit"), evidence carries `source_band`; the explain entry no longer calls every source "a compromised IT host". `is_it_side` itself is unchanged (it defines which rows are candidates; the band decides how bad). MF: three L3 / L3.5-sourced chains drop critical→high; enterprise / internet sources keep theirs. |
| MP-12 | Med | Device boundary vs interface labels merged into phantom legs; no conflict warning; topology page boundaries differed from the engine | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `_Context._add`: a boundary level that lies *between* labelled legs is a phantom and is dropped (4→3 device with 4 / 2 interfaces → legs [2, 4]; 4→3.5 with 4 / 3 → no DMZ leg, so no site-wide `dmz_bypass`); a boundary level *beyond* the labelled range is kept as the unlabelled far leg (C8). Every disagreement is reported as `label_conflicts` (`/reachability`, `/reachability/path`, `/access-paths`; banners on Reachability and Access Review). `topology_manager.firewall_level_boundaries` now returns the engine's boundaries, and `_site_has_dmz` is decided from them. Tests in `tests/test_mp08_12_24.py`. |
| MP-13 | Med | `peer_link` suggestion assumes a series pair (±1); wrong for a boundary chain → false bypass row + finding | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** A confirmed link is one wire: `run_purdue_suggestions` suggests the peer's OWN level on both ends; the ±1 far-side convention applies only between members of a declared paired group. INNER's uplink on OUTER's L3 segment is suggested L3, so its 3↔2 boundary survives. Tests updated (`test_purdue_suggestion_peer_link*`, `test_peer_link_suggestions_around_dmz`). |
| MP-14 | Med | Alias-vs-zone precedence and name-map overwrite differed between the twin resolver and reachability (C-09 applied to reachability only) | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `_RuleZoneResolver` now keeps exact-name and alias/zone_label maps apart (`exact_by_fw` / `labels_by_fw`, sets of levels so a shared label yields both levels) and resolves in reachability's order: FortiOS zone definition → exact interface name → inter-VDOM link → alias / zone_label → legacy FirewallZone. An alias equal to a zone name no longer shadows the zone; a label equal to another interface's name no longer overwrites it. Parity tests in `tests/test_mp09_14_15_resolvers.py`. |
| MP-15 | Med | Upload boundary `purdue_zone_from/to` never fed the rule twins; an unlabelled 4/2 firewall with permit-all raised nothing anywhere | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `_RuleZoneResolver.boundary_by_fw` (FirewallDevice boundary, else Firewall ingress/egress) places a side on a firewall with NO labelled interface: an interface's recorded `side` picks the end (inside → low, outside → high), otherwise both ends; `any` sides take both. `_emit_twin` steps such findings one severity down, suffixes the title "[levels from upload boundary, direction unverified]" and sets `level_source: "boundary"`. Once any interface is labelled the boundary is ignored. Tests in `tests/test_mp09_14_15_resolvers.py`. |
| MP-16 | Med | Same-level crossing through one firewall's legs reported "no boundary traversal" even when denied | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `reachability._same_level_path`: when the two hosts sit on two different labelled interface networks of one firewall at the same level, that firewall's policies between those legs (rules whose src / dst zone names the ingress / egress interface, or any) are evaluated - `compute_path` returns permitted / partial / denied with one hop `[3↔3 FW port2→port3]` and `same_level_crossing`; only hosts with no firewall leg between them stay `intra_level` ("not evaluated"). UI shows the crossing. |
| MP-17 | Med | Host query returns the most permissive level path as *the* path | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `compute_path` evaluates every level path, prefers - among equally permissive ones - the path whose firewalls hold the queried addresses, and returns `paths_evaluated`, `alternatives` (each path's verdict, firewalls, placement evidence, chosen) and `path_note` ("most permissive of N level paths … an upper bound"); the UI lists the other paths. `_path_string` prints boundaries after an early deny instead of calling them inferred. |
| MP-18 | Med | Same-level uplinks read as "definitely connected" and suggested as a series pair | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `detect_subnet_overlaps` proposals carry `arrangement` (parallel / series / unknown) + `arrangement_note` - two interfaces at the same level or both "outside" are parallel firewalls on a shared segment; `firewall_groups._linked` (and so `suggest_paired`) accepts only links / segments whose ends differ in level or side, so two cell firewalls are never steered into a series pair. |
| MP-19 | Med | `dead_end_permit` cannot name the blocker for network scopes; no dead end for `dst = all` | **Fixed 2026-10-08 (first half).** `chain_findings._representative` picks one address for a scope (the host itself, else a classified host inside it at the row's level, else the first usable address of a single network) and `_blocking_point` traces it (`blocked_at.traced_src / traced_dst`; the title says "(traced a → b)"). A `dst = all` row still terminates at the level it reaches (an "any" destination legitimately includes that level's hosts) - no dead end is claimed for it. Tests `tests/test_mp06_07_10_19.py`. |
| MP-20 | Low | Links / segments / upstream had no effect on the path model and nothing said so | **Fixed 2026-10-08 (by MP-01 + docs).** Paired groups, confirmed upstream chains and confirmed inside↔outside links now put boxes in series (MP-01); README "What shapes the path model" says what each declared relation drives (path model vs cross-firewall comparison vs suggestions). |
| MP-21 | Low | Composition two legs only; a /24 pivot composes nothing with no note | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `_compose_conduits` iterates to three legs (a composed row that ends above OT is the next pass's first leg; `pivots` lists every pivot, `legs` every leg; tag keys of two-leg rows unchanged); leg pairs that overlap on a network rather than one host are reported as `composition_notes` (API + Access Review notice: "declare a hop"). MV / VD / MF gain three-leg composed rows. |
| MP-22 | Low | Chain evidence without per-hop scope; `dead_end_permit.device_name` = last permitter | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** Every chain entry carries the policy's `src`, `dst`, `service`, `any_policy` and `narrowest` (the scoping hop); chain_it_ot_bypass names the change point in text and evidence (`change_point`); `dead_end_permit.device_name` is the blocking firewall when traced. |
| MP-23 | Low | VPN policies triple-reported; dial-up text says "SSL-VPN user" | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `VpnContext.is_vpn_rule`; the level twins (rule_it_ot_bypass, rule_mgmt_wrong_zone, rule_ot_it_dependency) skip VPN-sourced policies (`permit_rows(skip_vpn=True)`) - the protocol twins (insecure protocol, cleartext credentials, conduit drift) still judge them, since a cleartext FTP over the VPN is its own finding; "any remote-access (SSL-VPN / dial-up IPsec) user". Dial-up IPsec FTP/HTTP `rule_insecure_protocol` findings stay. |
| MP-25 | Low | Smaller items | **Partly fixed 2026-10-08.** `_union_verdicts([partial, unknown])` is marked `uncertain` (cell `uncertain: true` - the service list is a lower bound); labels on an HA secondary only are explained in `unplaced_firewalls` ("interface levels are on the HA secondary … label the primary"); a tunnel that stops being remote access loses its automatic L5; `carrier_fw_id` chooses between firewalls since MP-01. Still open: SNAT pool far-side placement, routing-switch OR hiding a firewall's policy (LS-02 design), one-host flat evidence, `subnet_level` order dependence, per-firewall-set roll-up of chain findings. |
| MP-24 | Low | Per-firewall "rules not judged" counts reached only the log | **Fixed 2026-10-08.** `alert_engine.rules_not_judged(db, site_id)`: per firewall, the enabled permit policies with a side the twins cannot resolve (not set / unlabelled interface / unknown name / partly resolved), with the policies and reasons; `GET /reviews/rules-not-judged`; table on Reports & Diagnostics; "Rules not judged" paragraph + table in the report's Check Coverage. Tests in `tests/test_mp08_12_24.py`. |

Confirmed correct: series chain rows and dead-end detection, scope / service intersection across hops, AND / OR truth tables, "exactly this leg" on multi-leg boxes, NAT with the original source kept, carrier filter, zone-before-CIDR precedence in both resolvers, mixed-level zones, suggestions never auto-promoted, site scoping of the resolver.

## VD-02a: capture point of a PCAP upload (2026-10-08)

Owner decision: the PCAP upload asks where the capture was taken - a dropdown of the site's uploaded firewalls, then the interface / port (or "not sure / several ports"), or the CIDRs of a segment that is no firewall port. No firewall = soft fail: the capture is still ingested, flagged *unassociated*, and can be associated later.

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| Capture point | `POST /ingest/pcap` takes `firewall_id`, `interface_id` (`any` = firewall only) and `capture_networks`; every upload creates a `PcapCapture` row (`capture_id`, `association`, `capture_point`, `capture_point_notes`, `capture_point_suggestion`, `gateway_macs_by_capture_point` in the response). Validated against the site (404 unknown id, 422 other site / interface without a network / bad CIDR) before the file is read. `/ingest/pcap-fw` with `firewall_id` uses the firewall as capture point too. |
| Rules (`app/parsers/capture_point.py`) | With the firewall known: (a) a MAC claiming a firewall interface address (ARP / NDP) is the firewall's; (b1) a MAC of the firewall's vendor (OUI) carrying an address of its networks that another MAC carries too is the routed copy; (b2) a MAC sourcing addresses of two or more of its networks is the firewall. With the local segment known (interface + VLAN sub-interfaces, or the CIDRs): (c) a MAC sending from / receiving for an off-segment address is the gateway. The firewall's interface addresses become its own addresses. A loaded switch ARP table still overrides. |
| Soft fail | No capture point: a note says host identity rests on the router heuristics; the capture lists as unassociated (`GET /ingest/pcap/captures`, Upload page banner). |
| Associate & reprocess | `POST /ingest/pcap/{id}/associate` (and `/confirm`) re-attributes the stored rows of the capture's sessions (the raw file is not kept): a host row carrying the identified firewall MAC for an address that is not the firewall's is merged into the real row of that address (`merge_hosts` - sessions, observations, findings move), or loses the MAC; classification re-runs. |
| Learned MACs | MACs identified by an associated capture are stored on it (`pcap_captures.gateway_macs`) and count as routers for every later upload of the site, associated or not. |
| Wrong interface | `capture_point_fit`: the share of addresses (ARP / NDP claims, else frame sources of non-firewall MACs) inside the named networks; below 50 % a note warns that a wrong capture point makes remote hosts lose their MAC. Firewall-only uploads get a suggested interface. |
| Findings | `unauthorized_industrial_write` dedupes per capture *file* (source file, else capture id), so re-uploading a file refreshes instead of duplicating now that every upload is a capture. |
| UI | Upload page: *Capture point* fieldset (firewall, interface, segment), result summary with the identified MACs and notes, and *Captures of this site* with per-capture associate controls. |
| Schema | Migration `6b7c8d9e0f1a`: `pcap_captures.capture_networks`, `gateway_macs`, `reprocessed_at`, `association`. |

Tests: `test_pcap_capture_point_routes`. Re-run of the multi-VDOM scenario: a capture-only upload keeps the duplicate jump-host row (`ip_conflict`); associated with the firewall, one row per address; associating the capture-only upload afterwards merges the duplicate and the next review closes the `ip_conflict`.

## Scenario review 2026-10-08 (PCAP only, flat OT network)

One capture of a flat /24 (PLCs, HMIs, EWS, historian, office PC, vendor laptop, a rogue Raspberry Pi, router to the Internet) and no other data. Missed at first: the flat segment itself (FO-01), the horizontal sweep (FO-04) and the rogue device (FO-03); refused handshakes became critical bypass findings (FO-04). Issues FO-01 to FO-12. **FO-01 to FO-04 fixed 2026-10-08** (capture segment, roll-up of cross-level flows, unidentified industrial clients without an inventory, host sweeps and connection attempts; migration `7c8d9e0f1a2b`); a blocked SYN is no longer a session. **FO-05 to FO-12 fixed 2026-10-08**: unclassified hosts on an OT capture segment are OT-adjacent for egress and SMBv1; new check `session_remote_access_app`; cleartext logins recorded per session (`network_sessions.cleartext_auth`, migration `8d9e0f1a2b3c`) and reported for any destination, port-only rule medium; "unfiltered" wording with no firewall loaded; titles / severities follow the current run; the capture gateway is classified `router`; short captures roll up the "no NTP" devices; the upload names the likely engineering workstation. All planted conditions detected.

## Scenario review 2026-10-09 (PCAP only, isolated OT network: no firewall, unmanaged switches, no Internet)

One 80-minute capture of an air-gapped plant: three subnets stacked on one layer-2 network with no router, a multi-homed HMI, a contractor laptop, a consumer Wi-Fi AP serving DHCP and a phone behind it. At first 4 of 12 planted conditions were detected (5 + 1 partly after classification). Issues IO-01 to IO-13 (project doc *isolated-ot-tabletop*). **All fixed 2026-10-09**, no migration:

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| IO-01 | `detect_gateway_macs` ignores the addresses a MAC itself claims in ARP: a multi-homed host is no router. |
| IO-02 | New check `multi_homed_host` (segments.py): one MAC with addresses in two or more subnets; high when the other devices' levels differ or a subnet holds L0-L1 devices. |
| IO-03 | The capture segment records layer-2 domains (`segment.domains`: subnets joined by MACs using both, or by direct frames); `flat_control_segment` works per domain, counts unclassified members, high with "(one layer-2 segment, N subnets, no router)". |
| IO-04 | DHCP replies parsed (`segment.dhcp_servers`); new check `rogue_dhcp_server`; `wireless_in_ot_zone` also reports consumer Wi-Fi / router OUIs in captures; the unknown-device fallback reports consumer / mobile devices. |
| IO-05 | A consumer-OUI gateway is never classified `router` (`capture_gateway`) and stays subject to the device checks. |
| IO-06 | PROFINET DCP Set requests are `write` (factory reset `control`) and reported by `unauthorized_industrial_write`. |
| IO-07 | A UDP flow without a reply is a connection attempt (`unanswered`), except one-way protocols (syslog, traps, NetFlow / IPFIX, sFlow, cyclic IO, multicast / discovery, DHCP, PTP); `session_ot_internet_egress` reports attempts as "tried to reach ... (no answer seen)", medium / low. |
| IO-08 | New check `dead_default_gateway`: an address 2+ hosts (or one host 3+ times) ARP for and nobody claims. |
| IO-09 | `ntp_sync`: one finding when a capture of at least `IACS_NTP_MIN_CAPTURE_SECONDS` with industrial traffic shows no NTP / PTP at all. |
| IO-10 | Every address claimed in ARP becomes a host. |
| IO-11 | The upload note reports where program downloads / writes came from (flagged unidentified) and asks to confirm before approving; no "likely engineering workstation". |
| IO-12 | Unknown-device fallback findings are one per MAC, listing its addresses. |
| IO-13 | `ot_it_dependency` skips services on OT hosts (L0-L3.5). |

140 checks. Tests: `tests/test_io_isolated.py` (synthetic capture), `test_review_dead_default_gateways`. Re-run of the scenario: every planted condition detected in both passes (details in the project doc).

## Feature 2026-10-09: Classify control and Purdue lane display

|---|---|
| Classify control | Host Topology panel and Inventory panel: suggested device types with their default Purdue level, a confidence and the reasons (industrial protocols as client / server, writes and program downloads, OPC UA, historian ports, vendor OUI: industrial, PC / laptop, consumer gear or device, DHCP server, capture gateway), with warnings where classifying is the wrong fix (unknown laptop, consumer equipment). Device type picklist grouped by level, level pre-filled with the type's default and editable, "Apply to all N addresses of this MAC", "Back to automatic", "Run reviews now". |
| API | `GET /hosts/{id}/classification-suggestion` (read only); `PATCH /hosts/{id}/classification?apply_to_same_mac=true` classifies every address of the MAC (response `updated_ids`); `router` may be set without a level; `DELETE /hosts/{id}/classification` removes a manual classification (optionally for the whole MAC) and re-runs automatic classification. |
| Lanes | One card per MAC per lane listing its addresses ("multi-homed · N addresses", "also in L?" when its addresses sit in different lanes until classified); cards grouped by VLAN, else by capture segment ("one layer-2 segment, N subnets"); public addresses in an "Outside the plant" lane; readable device type and protocol names (port name when the protocol was not decoded); status line counts devices and addresses. |
| `/topology` | Nodes gain `segment`, `external`, `device_label`, `classification_source`; edges gain `protocol_label`. |
| Finding text | The unknown-device fallback now points to "Inventory or Host Topology: Classify". |

The standalone `ui/topology.html` page is unchanged (replaced 2026-10-10 by the console's standalone mode). Tests: `test_host_classification`, `test_host_classification_suggestion`, `test_topology_and_classify_control`; UI smoke checks the classify box and counts topology addresses across merged cards.

## Scenario review 2026-10-09 (routed OT: layer-3 core switch with no ACLs, Internet-only firewall; SPAN capture)

The scenario is a single site with VLANs for workstations, HMIs, PLCs, VoIP, CCTV and network management, all routed by a Cisco core switch with no ACLs, behind a FortiGate that only NATs to the Internet. The capture is a SPAN of the core: 802.1Q-tagged, with each routed packet seen twice.

Planted conditions detected: pass A (capture only) 7 of 12; pass B (plus switch and firewall configs) 8 + 2 partly; pass C (plus classification) 11 + 1 partly. Issues RS-01 to RS-12 are in the project doc *routed-ot-tabletop*, grouped G1-G4.

**Group 1 fixed 2026-10-09** (no migration):

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| RS-07 | The 802.1Q VLAN of each (source MAC, address) is kept. A host seen in one VLAN gets `vlan` with `vlan_source = "pcap_8021q"`; a MAC table or the operator still wins. The capture segment lists `vlans`. |
| RS-01 | Layer-2 evidence counts per frame: one frame going MAC to MAC between two subnets, neither MAC a router. Subnets seen in different VLANs never form one segment. The false "one layer-2 segment, N subnets, no router" on a SPAN of a router is gone. |
| RS-11 | The routed copy of a packet (same IPv4 packet within 10 ms, other MACs / VLAN) is not counted or decoded twice (`span_duplicates`). |
| RS-02 | An address that never sent a frame, never claimed itself in ARP / NDP, has no MAC and is left with no session is not a host (`silent_addresses_not_hosts`). The attempts and the sweep finding keep it. |

Re-run: hosts 285 → 36, packets 7 632 → 5 054. The isolated-OT tabletop findings are unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_rs_span.py`.

**Group 2 fixed 2026-10-09** (no migration):

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| RS-03 | An Internet flow carried by a routing switch is judged by the switch and the site's firewalls in series. Unclassified public addresses take L5 in the session evaluation and the access-path overlay. The false `unused_policy` is gone. |
| RS-04 | A routing switch's implicit route is not "direction unverified", so L1 / L2 → switch → firewall → L5 rows are kept, and with the capture they are seen. `path_ot_internet_egress` reports L1 and L2 (critical). A switch-only chain is not Internet egress. `chain_ot_upward_skip` leaves out switch-only chains and chains ending at L5. |
| RS-12 | A segment the capture shows routed to an OT segment by a routing switch or an unknown router is OT-adjacent "via router" (egress medium). Firewall and switch interface networks count as segments. |

Re-run: pass C reports egress from L1 / L2 as critical and seen; pass A / B report the workstation egress via CORE-SW01. All 12 planted conditions are detected in pass C.

**Subnet zones (new G3) 2026-10-09** (migration `9e0f1a2b3c4d`). Owner: VoIP / CCTV / building systems are L4; mismatches are flagged.

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| Zones | New table `subnet_zones` and API `GET / POST /subnets`, `DELETE /subnets/{id}`. A *Subnets & Zones* page lists every subnet with evidence and a suggested level / zone; the operator declares or accepts them. |
| Classification | Hosts with only weak evidence take the declared level (`subnet_declared`); unlabelled firewall / switch interfaces on the subnet take it (`zone_declared`) and the switch checks follow. |
| Findings | `misplaced_device` "Device does not match its zone" for a manual / protocol-classified device whose level differs from its zone (high across OT / DMZ / IT, medium two or more OT levels apart). `switch_routes_between_zones` also reports an L0-L2 SVI routed to SVIs of unknown level (high). |
| Upload | The note "N subnet(s) have no zone yet" has an Assign zones button; the nav badge counts them. |

Tests: `tests/test_subnet_zones.py`; UI smoke `zones`.

**External communications (G5) 2026-10-09** (migration `1f2a3b4c5d6e`). Owner: build the view, with the optional IP-to-organisation data.

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| Names | Capture ingest records DNS answers, TLS server names (SNI) and HTTP Host headers per address (`observed_names`, `app/parsers/name_sniff.py`); nothing is looked up. |
| View | `GET /external-comms`: one row per inside host and destination (registrable domain of the observed name, else the address) for every address outside the site's known networks and internal ranges; category, answered / tried only, volume, first / last seen, the router it left through, the first matching rule per device in path order, review status; `unknown` = no name, no recognised service, not reviewed. *External comms* page with a nav badge for unknown flows. |

| Findings | `session_ot_internet_egress` titled and keyed per inside host and destination name when a name is known. |

A classified engineering workstation (L3 or below) whose session would be a level skip is engineering access: nothing when it is an approved engineering source, else new check `engineering_access_unapproved` (8.4, medium). Its industrial writes are "Engineering access to approve" (high) instead of "Unauthorized" (critical); full review runs re-word open write findings when the source's classification changes. |

141 checks. Tests: `tests/test_rs_g4.py`; `tests/test_external_comms.py` updated; UI smoke `extcomms` checks the OT markers. Re-run: every planted condition detected; the isolated-OT tabletop findings are unchanged.

Transits are left out of `switch_routes_between_zones` and `switch_mgmt_svi_unrestricted`. |

## Feature 2026-10-10: foundational requirement and security level on every check

Owner request: map the findings' SRs to the FR and SL, store them in the catalogue, and let the PDF report and the Findings page filter by target level.

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| Mapping | New `app/security_levels.py`: the 62443-3-3 SR / RE → SL-C table (Annex B) and the FR of each SR. Each check and finding gets `fr` (the FRs of its SRs, in the order they are mentioned) and `security_level` (the lowest SL at which one of its SRs applies). A string with no 62443-3-3 SR (62443-2-1 programme items: `zone_doc_1_6`, `config_rules_missing_owner`, `remote_access_app`) has no level and is kept at every target level. Both values are derived from the finding's own SR text, so the inline `config_*` checks get them too. No migration. |
| Catalogue | `check_catalogue()` entries carry `fr`, `sl` and `sl_by_sr`. `/findings/category-reference` rows carry `fr` and `security_level`. |
| API | `?target_sl=` (1-4, `SL2`, `SL-T2`) and `?fr=` (FR1-FR7, 1-7, IAC…RA) on `GET /findings`, `/findings/summary` and `/findings/export`; 422 on bad values. Rows and CSV carry `fr` / `security_level`. The summary adds `by_security_level` and `by_fr`. Filtering classifies the distinct SR strings in scope and matches them with `IN`, so paging stays in SQL. |
| Findings page | *Target SL* and *FR* filters; an SL badge on each row (FR and SL in the side panel); *Foundational requirement* / *Security level* group-by options; an "Open by security level" card. |
| PDF report | `target_sl` on `GET /reports/compliance.pdf`, `--target-sl` on the CLI, and a *Target security level* option on the Reports page. The cover names the target. Findings, the severity summary and coverage leave out checks above it. The executive summary adds a findings table of FR × SL. Each check heading shows FR and SL. Coverage gets an SL column. The control mapping gets FR and SL columns and marks checks above the target "> SL-T". |
| Docs | The project doc *62443-check-reference* gains FR and SL columns; README section "Foundational requirements and security levels". |

All 146 catalogue entries match the hand-built table shared with the owner. The table is from the standard as known to the author: check it against the licensed copy. Tests: `tests/test_security_levels.py` (49); `test_cov_routes` key sets extended; UI smoke `findings` (filters, badges, grouping) and `system` (report link with `target_sl`).

**Severity order 2026-10-10** (owner: the PDF and the findings are sorted Critical, High, Medium, Low):

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| Findings | `GET /findings` and `/findings/export` sort by severity (critical, high, medium, low, info), newest first within a severity; `sort=newest` keeps the old order. The Findings page has a *Sort* control (Severity by default). |
| PDF | Within a checklist section, checks with the most severe findings come first, and each check lists its findings critical → info (so the per-check limit keeps the most severe). The remediation priorities were already severity-ordered. |

Tests: `tests/test_findings_sort.py` (6); UI smoke `findings` checks the list order.

Computed in `routes._inventory_evidence`, no migration. |
