## Interface label as host evidence 2026-10-10 (SF-8a, owner: build)

A host on the network of a firewall or routing-switch interface the operator labelled, with no evidence of its own, takes that interface's Purdue level. Before, the label served only the firewall-side checks (rule twins, access paths, chains, reachability); hosts behind a labelled interface that the capture showed only as servers (SCADA-SRV, AD, the historian, WSUS) or that spoke nothing industrial (the rogue laptop) had no level until classified by hand.

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| Mechanism | `subnet_zones.labelled_networks` / `apply`: operator labels only (`purdue_level` set, `purdue_suggestion_source` None; inter-VDOM links and tunnels excluded; automatic levels - SVI, edge, zone - do not count, an SVI's level may itself come from its hosts). Runs at the end of every `classify_hosts` and after every interface label change (`set_interface_labels`). Source `interface_label`, lowest in the classification precedence. |
| Precedence | Takes only hosts with no evidence (`None`, `oui_hint`, the ICS-client marker `protocol_client_hint`, an earlier label); a manual classification, a declared subnet zone, a protocol role, a VLAN name, an LLDP role or a baseline role wins. Relabelling moves the hosts; clearing the label clears them (an ICS client goes back to its marker). |
| Not an identity | No device type is set. An ICS client that only has the label's level stays an `unknown_device`; on a flat-segment finding such hosts count as unclassified members, not controllers; the IO-11 upload note still calls them unidentified. |
| Subnets & Zones | A network behind an operator-labelled interface is suggested at that level ("behind FW port2 labelled L3: unclassified hosts take this level (interface label)"). |
| UI / docs | Inventory side panel explains the source; README (packet captures) lists where a host's level comes from. |

Tests: `tests/test_interface_label_hosts.py` (3). SF tabletop re-run: pass B goes from 9 + 4 partly to the full session-level picture (the historian's Modbus to PLC-03 critical, HMI → AD, the SCADA server's egress "seen", every rule twin "[matching traffic observed]", the WSUS egress no longer reported as OT egress); passes C-E unchanged apart from the laptop now reading "(L1)" instead of "(unclassified, on OT segment …)". Only the device-type checks (`engineering_access_unapproved`, `flat_control_segment`, `mgmt_access_from_wrong_zone`) still wait for classification. Routed-OT, isolated-OT and flat-OT tabletop findings unchanged.

## Zones and conduits 2026-10-11 (IEC 62443-3-2 documentation view)

Owner: "62443 zone objects can be in different Purdue levels and IP addresses. The zone and conduit view needs to be unique and not affect findings." Design and worked example: project doc *iec-zones-conduits*.

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| Model | New tables `iec_zones` (name, SL-T 1-4, description, owner), `iec_zone_members` (kind host / subnet / interface / fw_zone, reference by name so a config re-upload keeps it; unique per site - an asset belongs to one zone) and `iec_conduits` (one per zone pair, name, SL-T, description); migration `3b4c5d6e7f8a`. Independent of `subnet_zones` (Subnets & Zones still sets levels for the checks). |
| Resolution | `app/iec_zones.py`: a host's zone = a host member (MAC or address), else the most specific network among subnet members, interface networks (VLAN sub-interfaces included) and FortiGate zone member interfaces; a firewall's own interface address is not a member; networks claimed by two zones listed as overlaps; members that no longer resolve are shown as not found, not dropped. |
| Conduits | Per zone pair and direction: the enabled permit policies whose sides map to the zones (interface / FortiGate zone membership, zone networks on the interface, concrete addresses; an unzoned outside / L5 interface is the Internet) and the observed sessions (count, services, samples, last seen). Documented conduits list both; zone pairs with policies or traffic and no documented conduit are listed (including assets in no zone and the Internet); notes for a conduit with nothing behind it and for a conduit SL-T below the higher zone's. |
| API | `GET/POST /iec-zones`, `PATCH/DELETE /iec-zones/{id}`, `POST /iec-zones/{id}/members` (409 when the asset is in another zone unless `move`), `DELETE /iec-zones/members/{id}`, `GET /iec-zones/candidates`, `POST /iec-zones/import` (`subnet_zones` / `fortigate`), `GET/POST /iec-conduits`, `PATCH/DELETE /iec-conduits/{id}`, `GET /iec-zones/export?table=zones|assets|conduits` (CSV). Site purge removes the three tables. |
| UI | New page *Zones & Conduits* (Compliance): zones table (SL-T, Purdue levels spanned, members by kind, hosts), zone editor (fields, members with resolution, add member with pickers for interfaces / VLANs / FortiGate zones / subnets / hosts, hosts and why each is in the zone), hosts in no zone with quick add, documented conduits with per-direction policies and traffic, communication without a documented conduit, imports and three CSV exports. |
| Not a finding source | The findings engine, classification and report never import the module (`test_engine_does_not_import_the_zone_module`); `test_zones_never_change_findings` runs the same review with and without zones and compares findings and host classification; UI smoke `iec` checks the findings summary is unchanged. |

Tests: `tests/test_iec_zones.py` (7), route table in `test_cov_routes`; UI smoke `iec`. ## SL-C and the inventory zone column 2026-10-11

Owner: "build the SL-C and the zone fix." Correction recorded: the 2026-10-11 zones release message said the Asset Inventory CSV `zone` column was already stored; it was not (the column was read and dropped). Fixed here.

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| SL-C | `hosts.sl_c` / `sl_c_source`, `firewalls.sl_c` / `sl_c_source` (migration `4c5d6e7f8a9b`): IEC 62443-4-2 capability security level 1-4 (NULL = not assessed) and its source. `PATCH /assets/{id}?sl_c=1-4|none&sl_c_source=`, `PATCH /firewalls/{id}/sl-c`; `GET /assets` and the inventory CSV export carry both; the Asset Inventory CSV imports `sl_c` / `sl_c_source` (bad values counted in `invalid_sl_c`). Inventory page: SL-C column and editor fields. |

The bundled sample inventory now seeds four zones (ICS_CONTROL, SUPERVISORY, ENGINEERING, CORPORATE). |
| Not a finding source | `test_sl_c_never_changes_findings` (every host and firewall SL-C 1: findings identical); `test_engine_does_not_import_the_zone_module` still passes. |

## Documentation status on findings and the `undocumented_conduit` check 2026-10-11

Owner, asked how the zone flag should affect the cross-zone / bypass / upward findings: "option 2, the 4" - every finding with two ends says whether its path is documented (option 2), and a zone pair that communicates with no documented conduit is a finding (option 4). Design: project doc *iec-zones-conduits*.

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| Documentation status (option 2) | `zone_judgement.documentation` on every finding whose evidence has two ends: `intra_zone` (both ends in one zone), `documented` (a conduit is documented between the two zones), `undocumented` (two zones, no conduit), `partial` (the other end is in no zone), `external` (the other end is outside the site). Applies to every two-ended check, not only the level-based ones. Findings page: `· no conduit` on the zone badge (amber), the status in the panel line, a **Conduit** filter (`?conduit_doc=` on `/findings` and `/findings/export`); CSV column `zone_documentation`; PDF Device column marks *in zone* / the conduit name / *no conduit*. Severities unchanged. |
| `undocumented_conduit` (option 4) | `app/zone_findings.py` (outside `app/parsers`), run by `run_periodic_reviews` as `review_undocumented_conduits`, auto-resolved; workbook row 1.4, SR 5.1; one finding per pair of documented zones with a permitting policy or observed traffic and no documented conduit (dedup `rule_key` `zones:<a>:<b>`). Medium; high when traffic was observed and a zone holds L0-L2 assets; low when only a policy permits it and nothing was seen. Evidence: zones with SL-T, per-direction policies / sessions / services (`paths`). Closes when the conduit is documented or the path is gone; re-raised when the conduit is deleted. Pairs with assets in no zone or the Internet are not findings. Explanation entry, check reference row, report input "IEC 62443-3-2 zones". |
| Guard | `test_engine_does_not_import_the_zone_module` now states the rule exactly: parsers and explanations never name `iec_zones`; `alert_engine.py` references `zone_findings` only to run its reviews; `generate_report.py` names `iec_zones` only in `collect_zone_data`. `test_zones_never_change_findings` / `test_sl_c_never_changes_findings` compare every finding except the zone-documentation checks. The e2e snapshot gains `undocumented_conduit` (the sample inventory seeds four zones that communicate). |

SF tabletop re-run: four `undocumented_conduit` findings (Control cell ↔ Enterprise services high, Site operations ↔ Enterprise services / Vendor access medium, Enterprise services ↔ Vendor access low); of the 101 other open findings 23 are on a documented conduit, 11 have no documented conduit (the historian's Modbus, the HMIs' DNS / LDAP / SMB to AD, the IPS / AV findings on policy 4), 5 are external, 1 partial, 61 one-ended.

Tests: `tests/test_iec_zones.py` (`test_undocumented_conduit_check`; option 2 statuses and `conduit_doc` filter + CSV in `test_device_findings_judged_against_zone_sl_t`). UI smoke `iec`. Full suite 2549 passed, 1 skipped; UI smoke 19/19 and 7/7.

## SF-5 and SF-6 judged with the IEC zones 2026-10-11

Owner: "fix sf-5 and sf-6", after agreeing that SF-5 is the blocked-attempts check and SF-6 a conduit issue (one policy serving two conduits), not a multi-zone membership. Details: project docs *segmented-firewall-tabletop* (round 4) and *iec-zones-conduits*.

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| SF-5 `blocked_cross_zone_attempts` | `app/zone_findings.py:review_blocked_cross_zone_attempts` (row 8.8, SR 5.1 / SR 6.2; auto-resolved; dedup `blocked:<ip>`): per source host, targets in another segment (IEC zone, else firewall interface network) whose attempts were all dropped and no session got through; Internet targets and firewall interface addresses excluded. Per target the conduit status (undocumented / outside the conduit's services / documented / unzoned). Severity low; medium for management / file-sharing ports, an undocumented path or an unknown source; high for an unknown source on management ports. |
| SF-5 `conduit_service_blocked` | Targets the documented conduit covers (service and source) go to an info finding (row 1.4, SR 5.1; dedup `conduitblocked:<ip>`): sanctioned traffic the firewall drops. |
| SF-5 unknown device | `_unidentified_ics_clients`: a host with no device type and no inventory evidence whose level comes only from the interface label / declared subnet, on an L0-L2 network, that opens sessions or attempts to another firewall-interface network or the Internet is reported "Unidentified device on the control network reaching across the firewall" (high with management ports or Internet attempts; evidence `across_firewall`). |
| SF-6 labels | `fortios_zones.zone_list` (ordered, case kept); rule-twin titles name every interface with its level, sorted by level ("port1 (L1) + port2 (L3)", evidence `src_zone_parts` / `dst_zone_parts`); Access Review rows carry `zone_parts` and the UI / scope label uses them. |
| SF-6 zone judgement | `FindingJudge` resolves policy sides per interface (an interface that is not a member resolves to the zone of its network) and treats a multi-interface side as one conduit per zone pair: documentation the worst pair, SL-T the strictest, `conduits` listed. |
| SF-6 `policy_spans_multiple_conduits` | `review_policies_spanning_conduits` (row 3.1, SR 5.1 / SR 5.2; low, medium when a zone holds L0-L2 assets; dedup `spans:<fw>:<vdom>:<pid>`; auto-resolved): a policy carrying two or more zone pairs - split it per conduit. |

All 14 planted conditions detected.

|---|---|

Hosts go into the selected site (refused with a message when "All sites" is selected and several exist). |
| 2. Preview with suggestions | `POST /ingest/baseline/asset-inventory/preview` (read only): every column with up to 3 sample values and a suggested field - `saved` (the site's saved mapping), `exact` (the field name), `alias` (known names: `ip address`, `mac address`, `host name`, `device type`, `asset type`, `purdue level`, `vlan id`, `security zone` ...), `values` (a column of IP / MAC addresses) or `guess` (header words); the last two are marked "check". One field per column; per column *Ignore* or any field. `app/parsers/inventory_mapping.py` (pure functions). |

Listed over every row: rows that will be imported, rows without a usable IP / MAC (with row numbers), invalid Purdue levels, invalid SL-C, device type values the checks do not know (with counts), values read as an application type in other case / spacing (`PLC` → `plc`, `Engineering Workstation` → `engineering_workstation`, now also stored that way on import). |

Saved mappings per site | Table `inventory_import_mappings` (site, name, `column_map`; migration `8a9b0c1d2e3f`); `save_mapping_as` on the import stores the mapping used (ignored columns kept as ignored); `GET /ingest/baseline/asset-inventory/mappings`, `DELETE …/mappings/{id}`. Site purge removes them. |
| 5. Ignored columns reported | Every import's stats carry `mapping_source` (`names` / `supplied` / `saved:<name>`), `columns_mapped`, `columns_ignored`, `mapping_problems` (e.g. no IP / MAC column), `device_type_normalised` / `device_type_unrecognised` (+ values). The Upload page shows them in a summary with a link to *Inventory → Import CSV*. An upload without a mapping still matches by field name and alias only (never a guess). |

| `rule_dns_external` | `alert_engine.review_rule_dns_external` (rule twin; row 9.11, SR 5.1 RE2 / SR 7.6; FR5 + FR7, SL 1): one finding per enabled permit policy with a row whose destination is external - a public address or network (`_external_dst_token`: `8.8.8.8/32`, `1.1.1.0/24`, ranges, IPv6), a public `fqdn:` / `wildcard-fqdn:` or an `internet-service:` object (DR-02) - on tcp / udp 53 (ICMP rows of the policy are ignored; `any` / private / unresolved destinations are not external - the `any`-destination egress stays with `path_ot_internet_egress`). Not level-dependent. Severity high when the source zone holds L0-L3 (owner rule: no OT device reaches the Internet directly), low when the source is enterprise only (L4 / L5), medium for DMZ or an unknown level; one step up when matching sessions were observed. Evidence: `external_destinations`, `transports`, `src_ot_levels`, `severity_reason`, the standard rule-twin fields; dedup per logical policy (T3-19), auto-resolved, VPN policies included. In `_RULE_TWIN_CHECKS`, `_TWIN_CHECK_NAME`, the auto-resolve map, `CHECK_TYPE_REFERENCE`, explanation entry, check reference (153 checks), README table. |
| `config_dns_external` | `_config_findings` reports every external resolver: primary and secondary each judged (`resolvers` with role / server / kind internal · external · fortiguard; `external_resolvers`); with neither set the FortiOS default applies - FortiGuard DNS 96.45.45.45 / 96.45.46.46 (`fortiguard_default`, DR-05: defaults are absent from a backup) - which the row names explicitly and the old check never saw. Title names each external server ("Firewall DNS uses external resolvers: primary 8.8.8.8, secondary 1.1.1.1"). The bundled sample config (primary 10.0.4.10, secondary 8.8.8.8) now raises it - the e2e snapshot gained the check. |

Tests: `tests/test_rule_dns_external.py` (3: the owner's policy 306 ingested from a FortiGate config - one finding, both resolvers, tcp + udp, 8 rows, high; an enterprise policy low; internal resolver and `all` destination not reported; no duplicate on a re-run; unknown levels medium and an FQDN destination; the resolver check with both external, internal + external, nothing configured = FortiGuard, both internal = nothing); `test_cov_alert_engine::test_review_rule_dns_external` (coverage map); e2e snapshot updated. ## Cleartext and management protocols split 2026-10-12

Owner: "rewrite the insecure protocol rule to be Cleartext and make a different rule for management protocol. Cleartext management protocol will be in both findings".

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| `rule_cleartext_protocol` / `cleartext_protocol` | Renamed from `rule_insecure_protocol` / `insecure_protocol` (functions `review_rule_cleartext_protocols` / `review_cleartext_protocols`); same port list (FTP, FTP-data, Telnet, SMTP, TFTP, HTTP, POP3, IMAP, SNMP v1/v2c, SNMP trap), same mapping (row 3.5, SR 7.7 / SR 4.1, medium; rule twin critical when traffic is observed). Titles say "Cleartext protocol ...". `smbv1_legacy` still comes from the PCAP review. |
| `rule_mgmt_protocol` (new) | `review_rule_mgmt_protocols` (rule twin): permit rule across a zone boundary whose ports overlap a management protocol - SSH 22, Telnet 23, SNMP 161 / 162, RDP 3389, VNC 5900 / 5901, WinRM 5985 / 5986, HTTPS-alt 8443 - whatever the levels or declared management networks (`rule_mgmt_wrong_zone` still judges those). Rows 5.1, 5.2; SR 1.3, SR 5.1 RE1, SR 4.1 (FR1 + FR4 + FR5, SL 1). Medium; high when a cleartext management protocol (Telnet, SNMP) is permitted; critical when matching traffic is observed. Evidence adds `cleartext_mgmt_ports`. One finding per logical rule, auto-resolved, in `_RULE_TWIN_CHECKS` / `_TWIN_CHECK_NAME` / the auto-resolve map; the rule twins share `_review_rule_zone_crossing_ports`. |
| `mgmt_protocol` (new) | `review_mgmt_protocols` (PCAP): the same ports in a cross-level session; medium, high for Telnet / SNMP; auto-resolved; in `run_periodic_reviews`. |
| Both findings | Telnet / SNMP v1/v2c are in both port lists (`_CLEARTEXT_MGMT_PORTS`), so a policy or session using them gets a cleartext and a management finding, each naming the other in `related_checks`. HTTP / HTTPS / 8080 are not management ports here (general web traffic; HTTP is already cleartext). |
| Access Review | A `reviewed_ok` / `exception` classification also acknowledges `rule_mgmt_protocol` on the path's policies (`AUTO_POLICY_CHECKS`). The Access Review flag `insecure_protocol` (cleartext or management protocol incl. SSH) is unchanged; in the PDF its references now point at `rule_cleartext_protocol`. |
| Docs | Check reference (155 checks), README check table and rule-twin example, explanation entry, access-path-review.md, "rules not judged" text (report + console). |

Tests: `tests/test_mgmt_protocol_checks.py` (16: catalogue, registration, port lists, the split per port - Telnet / SNMP both, SSH / RDP management only, FTP / HTTP cleartext only, HTTPS neither - one finding per rule with every port, intra-zone not judged, escalation to critical, PCAP Telnet both / SSH management only, a periodic run raising both with `related_checks` and auto-resolving both); `test_cov_alert_engine` (coverage map + `test_review_mgmt_protocols`, twin assertions); renamed checks in the existing tests; Access Review acknowledgement counts (+1 per policy that also permits a management port), twin SELECT budget 24 -> 25 (one more twin, still constant in rule count), e2e snapshot gains `rule_mgmt_protocol`. Full suite 2579 passed, 11 skipped, e2e snapshot then fixed and re-run (2 passed). No migration, no UI change beyond the text. Not packaged.

## Access Review: one note for many rows 2026-10-12

Owner: "for the access review UI. need a means to flag multiple rules at the same time with the same classification or in report or false positive notes".

Bulk classification and bulk report inclusion already existed (tick rows or a rolled-up line, or "select all matching the filters"; the bar above the tables). Missing was the note. The bulk bar gains **Note** and **By** fields and a **Set note** button:

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| Note with Apply | A typed note is saved with the classification on every selected row (one `POST /access-paths/classify`, `note` per item). Reviewed OK / Exception therefore put it on the acknowledged automatic findings of the rows' policies ("Accepted reviewed OK in Access Review: <note>") - the way to record "false positive, why" for many policies at once. |
| Set note | Saves the note alone (classification unchanged); Enter in the box does the same. An empty note clears it. |
| Replace guard | When selected rows already carry a different note, a confirm names how many ("1 of the 4 selected rows already have a different note. Replace it?"); declining saves nothing. The box clears after a save. |
| By | Shares the remembered "Classified by" name with the row panels (`classified_by` on every item). |

No API change (the classify route already took `note`). Smoke: `ui_smoke_index.py` `ar_bulk_note` (inside the `accessreview` section: Apply with a note and the replace confirm, persisted note / classification / classified_by, cleared box, a declined confirm saves nothing, Set note alone keeps the classification; the note carries an XSS payload). Checked by hand in the browser on the seeded demo (4 rows: Reviewed OK + note, the policy-20 findings `rule_it_ot_bypass`, `rule_mgmt_protocol`, `rule_mgmt_wrong_zone` acknowledged with the note; Set note; no script ran). The Playwright smoke was not run here (Playwright is not in the dev lock files on this machine).

## Firewall Devices rules: classify Source / Destination from the hover 2026-10-12

Owner: "on the Firewall devices Rules page. The Source and Destination values should have mouse over that allow for classifying the value(s) to a Purdue level and zone".

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| Interactive hover | The Source / Destination pop-up (`hostPopShow`) can now be used: it stays open while the pointer moves from the cell into it (250 ms grace, `zonePopHideSoon`), Escape closes it, and scrolling inside it no longer closes it. It ends with **Classify level / zone…** when the cell holds an IPv4 value. The zone-name hover is unchanged (a tooltip). Single rows and expanded member rows now have the hover too (before: policy lines only). |
| Dialog (`fwClassifyOpen`) | Lists the cell's IPv4 values (bare IP -> /32, a CIDR normalised to its network) with a checkbox each (all ticked), what is declared for it now (`GET /subnets`) and the inventory hosts behind it (every host on a /32 - duplicate-MAC hosts included - with level and classification source); values a declaration cannot hold (any, IPv6, FQDN, range, object names) are listed as not classifiable. One **Level** + **Zone** (the Subnets & Zones presets; a preset fills its level) + optional note, **Save** posts one `POST /subnets` per ticked value for the rule's own site (works with "All sites" selected). |
| After saving | "Declared now" and the inventory refresh; the line counts the hosts now at the level and names those at another level with the reason (manual classification, protocol role, a narrower declaration, network device) - neighbouring OT levels are a normal mix, larger gaps become `misplaced_device` at the next review run (existing behaviour). **Run reviews now** runs the reviews for that site. Closing re-renders the rules and puts focus back on the same cell. |
| Keyboard | Focus on the cell shows the pop-up; Enter opens the dialog directly (without expanding the policy line); the dialog is a native `<dialog>` (Esc, backdrop). |

Why declarations: hosts have no zone of their own - level + zone live in Subnets & Zones, which already applies a level to hosts with weak evidence and reports disagreements; a host is declared as a /32 (most specific wins). No API change, no migration. Smoke: `ui_smoke_index.py` `fw_classify` (inside the `fw` section: hover -> pointer into the pop-up -> button; dialog values; L2 + an XSS zone name stored as declarations; "Declared now" updated; Run reviews offered; Enter opens without expanding, Escape closes; the declarations are deleted again and every host's level is back - checked by hand against the demo API: declare / delete restores level and source). ## Shadow Rules: one card per policy pair 2026-10-12

Owner: "shadow Rules ui page. condense the rows like what was done for the Firewall devices rules".

The shadow analysis works on expanded rule rows (one per source x destination x service), so an address-group policy produced one card per row pair - an 8-row policy duplicated by a broader one gave 8 cards. The page now groups them (UI only, `shadowGroups` / `shadowGroupCard`):

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| Grouping | One card per firewall device + VDOM + shadowed policy + shadowing policy (the sorted set of policies for a union shadow) + type + action conflict + union flag. A shadowed side without a policy id stays one card per pair. |
| Card | Type / "can never fire" / action-conflict / union badges, device, "policy B shadowed by policy A", a row-pair count chip; a summary line "N of the M rows of policy B ..." from the loaded rule list - "All M rows" / "The only row" when every row of the policy is shadowed, then "the whole policy is dead / a duplicate: remove it, or move it above policy A if it was the intent"; the overlap dimensions when the same for every pair; two condensed boxes (distinct sources, destinations, services, zones; lowest order with the row range; latest last-matched); *Show the N row pairs* opens the original per-pair cards (lazy). |
| Toggle | *One card per policy pair* (ticked by default, remembered in the browser: `iasc_shadow_policy_lines`) - untick for the previous one card per row pair. The status line adds the card count. |

No API change. Checked by hand on the demo plus a test site (policies 1 "WORKSTATIONS -> OT-NET ALL", 2 "WORKSTATIONS -> PLCS TELNET+HTTP", 3 deny WS-1 -> PLC-1 HTTP): 10 row pairs -> 3 cards ("All 8 rows of policy 2 duplicate policy 1 ...", policy 3 by 1 and by 2 with action conflict); expand shows 8 pairs; untick shows 10 cards. Smoke `_shadow_checks`: cards account for every row pair, expand count, untick = one card per pair, last-matched count on visible boxes. Playwright not run here.

## Logging / monitoring readiness and FortiGuard DNS / NTP severity 2026-10-12

Owner: "for 7 do option 2; for 8 do as suggested" (7 = logging / monitoring config, option 2: count every log destination + one monitoring-readiness finding per firewall, and move the forwarding check to row 6.3; 8 = FortiGuard DNS / NTP tiered severity).

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| Log destinations | `_log_destinations` reads every destination: `log syslogd` / `syslogd2-4 setting`, `log fortianalyzer` / `fortianalyzer2-3` / `fortianalyzer-cloud setting`, `log fortiguard setting` (FortiGate Cloud), `log disk` / `memory setting`, and each destination's `filter` (`forward-traffic`). The sections, plus `log setting` and `log eventfilter`, are now normalised settings sections. Syslog defaults (UDP, no encryption) are judged; FortiAnalyzer / cloud defaults are not (a backup omits them, DR-05). |
| `config_no_syslog_forwarding` | Fires only when no off-box destination is enabled (any syslog 1-4, FortiAnalyzer 1-3 / Cloud, FortiGate Cloud counts). Was syslog 1 only, which reported a FortiAnalyzer-logging firewall as "no forwarding". Row 6.5 -> **6.3** ("Logs forwarded to SIEM/log server with adequate retention"), SR 6.2 -> SR 6.1, SR 6.2, SR 2.9. Evidence lists every destination. |
| `config_syslog_unencrypted` | Judged per enabled off-box destination; one finding naming each weak one and why (no encryption / UDP / reliable disabled). The first destination's old detail keys are kept. |
| `config_monitoring_readiness` (new, 156 checks) | One finding per firewall (rows 6.1, 6.2; SR 2.8, SR 6.1, SR 6.2): implicit-deny hits not logged (`fwpolicy-implicit-log`), local-in denies not logged (`local-in-deny-unicast`) - both off by default -, event-filter categories switched off (event / system / user / vpn / ha), traffic logs not forwarded (every off-box filter `forward-traffic disable`), enabled permit policies logging security events only (`logtraffic utm`, the FortiOS default; `disable` stays `config_logging_disabled`), logs uploaded to an Internet service (FortiGate Cloud / FortiAnalyzer Cloud). Medium; low when only the cloud note; none when clean. |
| FortiGuard NTP / DNS | `config_ntp_fortiguard`: **info** on a firewall with a WAN leg (static default route or interface `role wan`), **low** without. `config_dns_external` when every external resolver is FortiGuard: info / low the same way, at least **low** when enabled permit policies use FQDN objects, **medium** when those rules sit on a firewall whose boundary is into OT (`purdue_zone_to` <= 2); a third-party public resolver stays **medium**. Both carry `severity_reason` / `has_wan_leg`. Catalogue default for `config_ntp_fortiguard` low -> info. Note: a custom NTP server on a public address is still not detected (README limitation 9, unchanged). |
| Re-ingest | An existing config finding now also refreshes its checklist row and SR (so the 6.5 -> 6.3 move applies on the next upload). |

Tests: `tests/test_monitoring_readiness.py` (17: catalogue; each destination kind counts as forwarding; no destination = row 6.3; encryption per destination incl. FAZ explicit weak settings; readiness gaps incl. event categories and filters; clean config = no finding; default-utm policy gap; cloud only = low; FortiGuard NTP info / low; FortiGuard DNS info / low with FQDN / medium into OT; third-party DNS medium); e2e snapshot gains `config_monitoring_readiness` (the sample config has no log section); `test_rule_dns_external`, `test_audit_full` unchanged and passing.

## Report: PASS / NOT EVALUATED and Configuration Evidence 2026-10-12

Owner: "In the 62443 pdf does it print out tests that passed and the evidence?" -> proposal 1 (passed / not evaluated from recorded evaluations) + 2 (configuration evidence appendix per firewall): "do 1 and 2, then run the full test suite".

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| `check_evaluations` (migration `9b0c1d2e3f4a`) | New SiteScoped table: site, check_name, scope, source, evaluated_at, complete, note, evidence JSON; unique (site, check, scope). Purged with the site. |
| Periodic reviews | `run_periodic_reviews` records, for every review of a **site-bound** run, each check it evaluates (`review_checks_map()` = the auto-resolve map + `_REVIEW_EXTRA_CHECKS` for denied trends, single path, path redundancy, firmware documentation, NTP sync): `complete` False with "review failed" or the incomplete reason. Unscoped legacy runs record nothing. |
| FortiGate config | An upload with `generate_findings` records `__fortigate_config__` per firewall (`fw:<id>`) with the evidence (`_config_evidence`: sections of setting / value / checks, using the same settings the checks read; an omitted setting is "not set (FortiOS default)"). `FORTIGATE_CONFIG_CHECKS` lists the 58 checks that upload evaluates. |
| Coverage (PDF) | `app/check_evaluations.coverage_states`: **PASS** - periodic: the review ran completely after the latest data (`data_changed_at`: config uploads, device updates, captures upload / reprocess, Subnets & Zones, baselines, IEC zones); FortiGate config checks: every FortiGate's latest config evaluated (others named as not covered); real-time checks: log events present; industrial writes: decoded data present. **NOT EVALUATED** with the reason (never run since the data, incomplete / failed, new data after the run, config uploaded without findings). Untracked checks (cross-FW, switch, management-network rechecks) keep "no findings". The executive summary counts pass / not evaluated. |
| Configuration Evidence (PDF) | New section before the control mapping: per FortiGate, the examined settings grouped (logging and monitoring; time and name resolution; administrative access; availability; remote access; security profiles; maintenance and updates) with PASS or FINDING + the checks with an open / acknowledged finding on that firewall (`source_ref` `config:<fw>:` / `fqdn_policy:<fw>:`). |

Limitations (documented): live log events and host sightings do not mark a pass out of date (continuous; the scheduled reviews cover them); switch outputs and interface-label edits carry no timestamp.

Tests: `tests/test_check_evaluations.py` (12: evidence recorded at upload, none without findings generation, every mapped review records, unscoped run records nothing, failed review incomplete, PASS after a run and NOT EVALUATED after new data, config checks PASS per config / not evaluated after an upload without findings, helpers, PDF coverage + evidence text + render, no section without a FortiGate, site purge); `test_report` (unused_policy NOT EVALUATED without a run, real-time PASS with events); `test_cov_alert_engine::test_review_checks_map`; migration site-table list.

## Asset Inventory: host VLAN from the firewall interface 2026-10-12

Owner: "for the asset inventory? what does the Vlan tag not show based on the uploaded config." -> "yes, do the VLAN from firewall interface".

A host's VLAN came only from a switch MAC table (`mac_table`), the /24 guess from MAC-table hosts (`subnet_inference`), an 802.1Q capture (`pcap_8021q`) or the inventory CSV (`manual`). The FortiGate import stored `set vlanid` on the interface (`FirewallInterface.vlan_id`) but nothing copied it to the hosts, so with a firewall config and untagged captures the column stayed empty.

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| `assign_vlans_from_interfaces` (device_classification) | Runs inside `classify_hosts` (config upload, capture upload, `POST /classify`) before the VLAN-name mechanisms: a host takes the VLAN id of the most specific firewall / routing-switch interface network holding its address (`vlan_source = "fw_interface"`). Replaces only an empty value, its own earlier value and the /24 guess; MAC table, 802.1Q and inventory values are kept. Interfaces of the same network that disagree on the VLAN give none; a value no interface supports any more is cleared. Site-scoped (interfaces of the host's site only). |
| `/assets` | Rows with `vlan_source = "fw_interface"` gain `vlan_origin` ("FW-V vlan_20 (Process Control), 10.90.20.0/24"). |
| Console | Asset Inventory VLAN cell: "from FW-V vlan_20" under the id (tooltip: the full origin); asset detail: "(firewall interface FW-V vlan_20 …)". |

Tests: `tests/test_vlan_from_interface.py` (10: hosts take the VLAN; MAC table / 802.1Q / manual kept; /24 guess replaced; cleared when unsupported; disagreeing interfaces give none; other site ignored; runs inside classify_hosts; `/assets` origin). Classification / MAC table / assets / e2e / subnet-zone tests: 78 passed. No migration.

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| `app/review_trigger.py` | `request(site_id, what)` queues one `run_periodic_reviews` pass for the site in a background thread and returns at once; uploads that arrive during a run set a pending flag and are reviewed by ONE more run after it; a run skipped because the site's review lock is held (scheduler, `POST /reviews/run`) is retried every 15 s for up to ~10 minutes, then left to the schedule. `status()` keeps the last run per site (started / finished, ok, new findings, which uploads). `IACS_REVIEW_ON_UPLOAD` (default true) switches it off. |
| Upload routes | `_reviews_after_upload(what)` decorator on: `/ingest/pcap`, `/ingest/pcap-fw`, `/ingest/pcap/{id}/confirm`, `/ingest/pcap/{id}/associate`, `/ingest/arp`, `/ingest/firewall`, `/ingest/fortigate-config`, `/ingest/switch`, `/ingest/mac-table`, `/ingest/forti-report`, `/ingest/baseline/comms-matrix`, `/approved-hosts`, `/asset-inventory`, `/reviewed-policies`, `/baselines/{name}/activate`. A successful (dict) response queues the review of its `site_id` and carries `reviews` ("queued" / "queued after the running review" / "off"). Not: live log lines (`/ingest/forti-event`, `/forti-syslog`, `/syslog` - real-time checks + the schedule cover them) and the inventory preview. |
| Tests | Suite default `IACS_REVIEW_ON_UPLOAD=false` (conftest, like the scheduler); `tests/test_review_on_upload.py` (10: off switch, no site, coalescing of two uploads during a run into one more, a failing run does not kill the worker, retry behind a busy lock, config / pcap uploads queue their site's review, a failed upload queues nothing, live syslog does not queue, every upload route decorated and the live / preview routes not). End to end on a fresh server: a config upload answered "queued" and the periodic findings (incl. `rule_mgmt_protocol`) appeared without a manual run. |

## Firewall Devices NAT: one line per policy 2026-10-12

Owner: "firewall devices NAT page should have the same consolidation as the firewall devices rules page".

NAT rows are stored per expanded address (a source-NAT policy with an 8-host group = 8 rows; the owner's site-a-rout01: 63 rows, 16 policies). The NAT drill-down now groups them like the rules drill-down: `natGroups` keys (type, firewall device, VDOM, policy id - or the VIP name when there is no policy - , state); a group of several rows is one line (`tr.rule-policy`, `data-members`) showing the original addresses (`fwHostCell`: host hover + Classify level / zone), "→ mapped", the distinct port mappings, protocols, ingress interfaces, policy, state and "N translations"; click / Enter expands the member rows (the previous row layout, `tr.rule-member`). Same *One line per policy* setting as the rules page (`iasc_fw_policy_lines`, checkbox `#fw-nat-policy`); unticked = one row per translation, as before.

Checked by hand on a test config (VIP port forward + SNAT policy 302 over a 3-host group: 4 rows -> 2 lines, expand -> 3 members, host hover with Classify, untick -> 4 rows). Smoke `fw_nat`: lines = expected groups, a policy line expands to its member count, then the existing per-row checks with the tickbox unticked. No API change.

## Host Topology: drag a host between levels 2026-10-13

Owner: "on the host topology can we drop and drag between the layers, such as unclassified to Layer3, etc".

Host cards on the Host Topology (console and `/ui/topology`) are draggable; a level lane highlights when a drop is allowed. Dropping opens an inline confirmation bar (`#topo-move`: "Move 10.0.1.1 to L3 Site Operations? Device type stays ..." - Move / Cancel, Escape cancels; no `confirm()`), then `PATCH /hosts/{id}/classification?purdue_level=<lane>&apply_to_same_mac=true`: a manual level for every address of the card's MAC, each keeping its device type (an untyped host stays untyped - choose a type in Classify). Dropping a hand-classified host on Unclassified offers "Return to automatic" (`DELETE`, same MAC). Not drop targets: the card's own lane, the outside (public addresses) lane, Unclassified for an automatically classified host, and lanes that are not drawn (empty L5 / L3.5 - use Classify). Keyboard users keep the Classify control. API: `device_type` on the PATCH is now optional when `purdue_level` is given (400 when both are missing). Tests: `test_host_classification_level_only` (test_cov_routes; level only keeps the type, survives /classify, 400 without either, an untyped host gets no type); related files 94 passed. Smoke: topology section drags an unclassified host to L3, confirms, drags it back to Unclassified. Checked in the browser on a demo DB (drop to L3 -> manual L3, router type kept, both addresses of the MAC moved; back to Unclassified -> automatic again; same-lane and automatic-to-Unclassified drops refused).

Such a device gets one card per subnet (`.host-node.multi-homed`: red background and border, `mh-peer` badge "multi-homed · same device as 10.100.159.38 (L4)"), and `drawTopoEdges` joins its cards with a red dashed link labelled by `mhLinkLabel`: "DMZ bypass" when one address is L4 / L5 and the other L3 or below, "bypass Lx↔Ly" for other different levels, "multi-homed" when a level is missing or equal; legend entry. Dragging a multi-homed card classifies only that card's addresses (no `apply_to_same_mac`); the Classify panel's "apply to all addresses of this MAC" is off by default when an address of the MAC is on another subnet. ## Multi-homed device on one capture segment 2026-10-13

Owner (on .22): the card "Addresses (one network card) 10.100.159.38, 192.168.88.52 / MAC 28:cf:e9:18:b5:ed" "is not show as two IP address to move".

Cause: `topoSubnetKey` used a host's capture segment as its subnet. When a capture shows both subnets on one layer-2 segment (the multi-homed device answers on both), every address gets the same segment label `192.168.88.0/24 + 10.100.159.0/24` (segments.py joins the member subnets), so the device was not seen as multi-homed and stayed one card. Now the key is the declared zone CIDR, else the segment member subnet that holds the address (`ipInCidr`), else the /24. Checked in the browser with the owner's two addresses on a shared segment at L4: two red, separately draggable cards, each naming the other address. Smoke: topology section checks the keys and the multi-homed detection for a shared segment.

## auth_failure: FortiOS 7.x admin login events 2026-10-13

Owner: "yes, fix the auth failure check" (gap found with the Plant T test set in `../project-docs/test-data/fortigate-plant-t/`: 6 failed admin logins, no finding).

FortiOS 7.x logs an admin login as `type=event subtype=system action="login"` with the outcome in `status=` (logid 0100032001 success / 0100032002 failed); `check_auth_failure` only knew `subtype` login / authentication with the outcome in `action=`. New `_login_outcome(ev)` (alert_engine.py) returns failed / success for both shapes (status= from `raw_fields`, else the admin-login logid); `check_auth_failure` uses it for `auth_failure` and for the success path (`auth_from_non_admin_host`, which therefore now also sees FortiOS 7.x successful logins). Other system events (config changes etc.) are not logins. Tests: `tests/test_fortios7_admin_login.py` (failed -> finding, six failures in one hour -> one finding, outcome from status or logid, success is not a failure, a config event is not a login, the legacy shape still counts); with the alert-engine / syslog / e2e files 192 passed. The Plant T syslog now gives one `auth_failure` (admin from 10.40.1.66, 6 occurrences).

## Asset Inventory: IEC 62443 zone 2026-10-13

Owner: "asset inventory needs a zone parameter" -> "IEC 62443 zone" (the zone on Zones & Conduits, the same one the inventory CSV `zone` column fills).

API: `GET /assets` rows carry `iec_zones` ([{id, name, sl_t, basis}] - the address's host-member zones, else the zone of its subnet / interface / FortiGate-zone member; `iec_zones.host_zone_index`, one ZoneModel per site with zones) and `iec_zone` (first name). `PATCH /assets/{id}?iec_zone=<name>` (`iec_zones.set_host_zone`): the host's existing host-member placements (MAC / address) are removed and it is added to that zone, created when missing (via `assign_from_inventory`); empty removes its own membership, so an inherited subnet zone shows again; the response has `iec_zone_change` (created / added / already / removed); 422 for a host without a site or identity. `GET /assets/export` gains a `zone` column (several zones joined with " | "; re-imports through the CSV mapping). Console: Zone column (tooltip: SL-T and basis, "several zones" badge), Zone filter (zones of the listed assets + "No zone"), and an editable zone name in the asset panel (datalist of the site's zones; a note when the zone is inherited). Zones stay documentation: no other finding changes. Tests: `tests/test_asset_iec_zone.py` (rows and basis, create / already / move, host member overrides and clearing falls back to the subnet zone, MAC-less host, export column, no zones); related files 156 passed. Smoke: assets section (Zone column, field, set "Smoke Asset Zone", zone created, filter). Checked in the browser on the demo DB.

## Audit of the .23 changes 2026-10-14

Owner: "audit code". Scope: the working tree against the .22 package (every file changed or added since; backend diff 1.6k lines, console 1k lines), read line by line, plus a static pass for unused imports. Also checked and found correct: the site scope applies to column-entity queries (so `_page_approved` only sees the reviewed site's hosts - overlapping addresses in another site cannot silence an unknown device), every new DOM is built with `el()` / `textContent`, every new route validates `site_id` and scopes its reads, the new tables are purged with the site, the migration test covers the new tables, and the `.22 -> head` upgrade path renders offline.

| # | Severity | Finding | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Medium | `add_conduit_rules` matched the firewall with `ILIKE`, so a name typed as `FW%` or `FW_C` attached rules to whichever firewall matched the pattern | exact, case-insensitive match on `name` or `device_name` (`func.lower(...) ==`); test `test_firewall_name_is_matched_exactly_not_as_a_pattern` |
| 2 | Medium | a policy number that exists in several VDOMs, attached without a `vdom`, was stored with vdom "" and then shown as "not in the configuration" | refused with "give the vdom" naming the VDOMs |
| 3 | Medium | `recheckFindings` reported "Findings re-checked." even when `/reviews/run` was skipped because the scheduler / another run held the site's review lock (`by_review.skipped_concurrent_run`) | the skip is detected, a note is shown in the findings section (`recheck-skipped`) and the save message says the findings were not re-checked yet |
| 4 | Low | `findingsSection` registered a new load guard (`VIEW_LOADS` entry) per render, unbounded over a long session | one guard per object (`linked-findings:<params>`) |
| 5 | Low | the Findings page's own Target SL filter still offered "SL 1-4" while every other SL list is SL0-SL4 with labels | same labelled list (SL0 keeps only findings with no 62443-3-3 requirement, as the report) |
| 6 | Low | a zone membership set from the Inventory page / API was recorded as created by "asset inventory" (the CSV path's label) | "inventory page" |
| 7 | Low | `tests/test_cov_routes.py` drove the new fixtures through pytest's private `fixture.__wrapped__` | the seeded API is a `contextmanager` (`api_ctx`) in `test_finding_links.py` / `test_report_checks.py`, as in `test_sl0_conduit_rules.py` |

Noted, not changed: `GET /assets` and the three list pages each build a `ZoneModel` (and `/findings/link-counts` a `LinkIndex`) per request - fine at the current sizes, worth caching per site if the inventory grows past a few thousand hosts; the drawer's Escape closes it even from inside a text field (a draft note is lost); `check_group` treats every `config_*` check as FortiGate, which is true today. Tests after the fixes: 168 passed (conduit rules, finding links, report checks, UI static, IEC zones, asset zone, routes).

## Interface levels apply to every host on the subnet; menu order 2026-10-14

Owner: "In the FW builder page, when a subnet is defined to a Purdue level. The Host Topology hosts should reflect the Purdue level. Unless the host has been changed in the Host Topology to a certain level. Move the FW Topology Builder left hand card option above the Host Topology option."

Before (SF-8a), a labelled interface's level reached only hosts with "weak" evidence (none, OUI hint, ICS-client hint): a host levelled by its protocol, VLAN name or LLDP role kept that level. Now (`subnet_zones.apply`, `_LABEL_KEEPS`) every host on the interface's network takes the label's level, keeping its device type (a PLC found by Modbus stays a PLC, at the labelled level); only a manual classification (Host Topology / Inventory Classify, baseline) and a Subnets & Zones declaration stay above it, plus the identities that are not devices of the subnet (remote VPN users, the capture gateway, routers). The label is applied when an interface is labelled in the FW Topology Builder (`set_interface_labels` -> `subnet_zones.apply`) and at the end of every classification run. Consequence handled: `flat_control_segment` judges a host levelled by its interface by the level its device type implies (`segments._lvl`), so an engineering workstation or historian on an L1-labelled subnet is still an L3 device on the controllers' segment (the sample-data snapshot is unchanged). Removing a label still returns the host to "no level" until the next classification run restores its own evidence. Inventory "Classified by" text no longer claims a labelled host has no device type. Menu: FW Topology Builder is the first item of the Topology group, Host Topology right under it. Tests: `test_interface_label_hosts` updated (PLC / HMI follow the label with their type), e2e snapshot, io_isolated; classification-related files passed.

## Findings workbook: xlsx export and import 2026-10-14

Owner: "For the compliance report I would like an xlsx export of the findings which will be the base of an import feature to update the finding notes, status and Name. The Import of the compliance xls will update the findings so a clean report can be outputted."

`app/findings_workbook.py` (new dependency `openpyxl==3.1.5` + `et_xmlfile==2.0.0`, pinned in requirements.txt / requirements-lock.txt). `GET /findings/export.xlsx?site_id=` writes the sheet *Findings* - one row per finding: Finding ID, Site, Check, Severity, then the shaded editable columns **Status** (data-validation list: Open / Acknowledged / Closed / False positive), **Name**, **New note**, **False-positive reason**, then the read-only context (title, device, SR, row, category, created, last seen, occurrences, acknowledged by / at, closed at, notes history, excluded-from-report flag); open + acknowledged + false positives, closed with `include_closed`, findings of excluded checks with `include_excluded`; a *How to use* sheet. `POST /findings/import.xlsx?site_id=&dry_run=&name=`: rows matched on the Finding ID (must belong to the site), and every row whose status / new note / reason differs goes through `apply_finding_status` - the status rules refactored out of `PATCH /findings/{id}/status` and shared with it (false positive needs a reason, acknowledging needs a name - the row's Name, else the `name` parameter -, re-opening over an open recurrence refused, notes appended with a stamp, never overwritten); refusals are reported per row and the other rows go through; a note already in the history is not appended again, so importing the same file twice changes nothing; `dry_run` (default) rolls back and reports. Console: Reports & Diagnostics card *Findings workbook (xlsx)* - include closed / include excluded, download link, file + your name, *Preview changes* (table of changes, errors) then *Apply* (only for the previewed file); the other pages reload on their next visit. Tests: `tests/test_findings_workbook.py` (contents incl. closed / excluded flags and the How-to sheet; round trip: preview writes nothing, apply updates status / name / appended notes, re-import no-op; per-row errors: fp without reason, unknown status, other site's id, unknown id; not a workbook -> 422); `test_cov_routes.test_findings_workbook`; lifecycle / UI static tests with the refactored status route passed.

## Stored firewall config and the config lines behind a finding 2026-10-14

Owner: "think about the uploaded firewall config. Firewall devices need a means to see the uploaded config to check findings. Where it is possible, can the findings have a snip of the firewall config that is causing the finding?" Decisions: secrets masked before the text is stored; the latest upload only; console only (not the PDF).

Until now an upload kept only the filename, the per-rule `raw_line` and the Configuration Evidence settings. New table `firewall_config_texts` (migration `ce3f4a5b6c7d`, one row per Firewall, `site_id`, filename, upload time, size, line count, sha256, masked count, zlib-compressed text) written by both config upload routes after the parser committed (`_store_config_text`; a failure never fails the upload; `stats.config_stored`). `app/config_store.py`: `mask_secrets` replaces passwords, pre-shared keys, SNMP community strings (FortiOS `set name` under `system snmp community`), RADIUS / TACACS keys, private-key blocks, PAN-OS / pfSense `<password>` / `<phash>` / `<pre-shared-key>` elements and FortiOS `ENC …` values with `***` - line numbers unchanged - and the text is written masked, so the clear values are never in the database. `GET /firewalls/{id}/config?site_id=` (metadata + text; `download=true` = text file; 404 for another site's firewall or a config uploaded before this release). `GET /findings/{id}/config-snippet`: `snippet_for_finding` finds the firewall from the finding (`firewall_id`, a rule row, the device name), then the block the finding names - `firewall policy` edit <policy_id> (VDOM-aware, "vdom/id" split, shadow_rule's shadowed policy), local-in policy, VIP, IPsec phase1, service, interface, admin, SSL-VPN portal - else the section the device-level check reads (`CHECK_SECTIONS`, e.g. `config_no_syslog_forwarding` -> `log syslogd setting`), else the rule row's `raw_line` (Cisco / PAN-OS / pfSense); at most 120 lines (`truncated`); `available: false` with a reason when the finding names no firewall, no text is stored, or the policy is no longer in the stored config (re-upload removed it) - never a wrong block; a note when the config was uploaded after the finding was last seen. Console: *Config* button on each Firewall Devices card opens the drawer (`openConfigDrawer`): file / upload time / lines / masked count, folding top-level `config … end` sections (closed by default above 4000 lines), find with match count (Enter = next match), go to line, download link, line numbers; *Configuration lines* block (`configSnippetBlock`) on the Findings page panel and in the linked-findings editors, loaded when opened, with *Open in config* opening the drawer on the highlighted lines. Site purge deletes the texts. Tests: `tests/test_config_store.py` (masking FortiOS / Cisco / XML / PEM with stable line counts; block finder incl. VDOMs; stored masked + served + download + other site 404; latest upload replaces; snippets for policy / interface / admin / IPsec phase1 / section; unavailable cases; Cisco upload stored and rule-line snippet; forced site delete removes the text), `test_cov_routes.test_firewall_config_text`, migration table list. Smoke: `fw_config`, `findings_config` (written, not run - Playwright not installed).

Owner: "Asset Inventory need a [way] to not include the host in the asset Inventory but not from being seen on other pages"; "Treat the exclusion as a decision (recommended)". `Host.inventory_excluded` (bool, default false) + `inventory_excluded_reason` / `_by` / `_at` (migration `df4a5b6c7d8e`, new head). `PATCH /assets/{id}?inventory_excluded=true&inventory_excluded_reason=&inventory_excluded_by=` marks the host (false clears the three fields). `/assets` and `/assets/export` leave excluded hosts out by default; `include_excluded=true`, `inventory=excluded` (only them) or `inventory=all` show them; rows carry the four fields and the CSV the `inventory_excluded` / `inventory_excluded_reason` columns. Console: `inventoryExcludeBox` on the asset panel (Inventory page and the asset drawer): reason + your name (remembered) + *Exclude from the inventory*, or the exclusion record with *Include in the inventory again*; the list loads with `include_excluded=true` and filters client-side - the Inventory filter gained *Excluded - not inventory assets* and *Everything incl. excluded* (default "All (except excluded)"); "excluded" badge in the Inventory column; the panel's Inventory row says "excluded - not an inventory asset (reason)"; Host Topology cards carry a "not an inventory asset" mark; the asset drawer lookups pass `include_excluded`. Tests: `tests/test_inventory_exclusion.py` (PATCH / list default, include_excluded, inventory=excluded / all / unlisted, topology + hosts unchanged, export columns, include again clears, 422 on a bad filter value; unknown_device before / after the exclusion under `analysis_site`). ## Evidence-matrix batch C: PCAP evidence - default credentials, weak TLS / SSH, banners, device identities, transient devices 2026-10-15

Owner: "do batch c". PCAP parser (`app/parsers/pcap_parser.py`): `_default_credentials` compares cleartext logins against well-known default pairs in the stream - FTP / POP3 (`USER` tracked per session, then `PASS`), SMTP `AUTH PLAIN`, HTTP Basic - and adds the kind `default credentials (<proto>)` to the session's `cleartext_auth`; the user / password values are never stored (same rule as `_cleartext_auth`); `_snmp_community` reports `SNMPv1/v2c community (default)` for `public` / `private`. `_weak_transport` recognises a TLS ServerHello negotiating SSLv3 / TLS 1.0 / TLS 1.1 and an SSH-1 banner; per session and version one `ProtocolDetail` row (protocol `tls` / `ssh`, `fields.weak`, `server_ip` / `server_port`) is written at the end of the ingest (`weak_transport_sessions` in the stats). Service banners of the server side - HTTP `Server:` headers and the SSH-2 banner on port 22 - become `ObservedName` rows (`http_server`, `ssh_banner`). Device identities: `enip_cip_analyzer._parse_identity_reply` decodes the CIP Identity item of a ListIdentity reply (vendor id, device type, product code, revision, serial, product name) and `s7comm_analyzer._parse_szl_response` the read-SZL responses 0x0011 (order number, firmware from index 6 as V<a>.<b>.<c> - the field layout follows Wireshark's szl decoding) and 0x001C (PLC / module name); both return `decoded["identity"]`, which `_add_detail` hands to `_apply_identity`: the server host's `model`, `firmware_version`, `serial_number` and `hostname` are filled when empty (never overwritten) and an `ObservedName` (`cip_identity` / `s7_szl`) records the sighting (`device_identities` in the stats) - this also feeds `firmware_not_documented` and the inventory. Reviews (`alert_engine`, registered in `run_periodic_reviews` and `_AUTO_RESOLVE_CHECKS`): `review_transient_devices` -> `transient_device_in_ot_zone` (8.6, SR 7.8 / 5.1, 2-1 NET 1.1): hosts at L0-L2 whose OUI vendor is laptop / PC / USB-Ethernet hardware (`_TRANSIENT_OUI_WORDS`: Dell, Lenovo, HP, ASUS, Acer, Framework, LG, MSI, Gigabyte, Razer, ASIX, Belkin, TP-Link, D-Link, StarTech, Plugable, Cable Matters, Anker, UGREEN, Realtek, Fujitsu Client, Toshiba, Panasonic, VAIO) and no identity (not manual, not in the active baseline's approved hosts, not approved / excluded on the Inventory page, not firewall infrastructure); one finding per MAC, high when it sent industrial requests or used management ports, else medium; `review_default_credentials` -> `default_credentials_observed` (5.4, SR 1.5 / 4.1, high, one per client -> server:port); `review_weak_tls` -> `weak_tls_observed` (3.5, SR 4.3 / 4.1, one per server:port, medium; high when a client from another Purdue level used it). 179 checks. Registered in `CHECK_TYPE_REFERENCE`, `explain.py` (close texts), `docs/62443-check-reference.md`. Tests: `tests/test_batch_c_pcap.py` (default pairs and stream detection incl. a non-default user, HTTP Basic and SMTP decoding, TLS / SSH recognition, SNMP default vs other community; CIP ListIdentity reply and request; S7 SZL 0x0011 / 0x001C; a crafted capture landing the kinds on the sessions, the weak-version detail rows, the banners and identities on the hosts, and both reviews reading them), `test_cov_alert_engine.test_review_transient_devices` (vendor, level, manual / approved / excluded exemptions, severity with ICS traffic) and `test_review_default_credentials_and_weak_tls` (kinds, cross-level severity, idempotent). Full suite 2752 passed, 11 skipped.

## Audit of the unreleased work (batches A-C, config store, builder settings, inventory exclusion) 2026-10-15

Owner: "audit the project". Four independent reviews (FortiOS checks, PCAP evidence, SIS review / config store / routes / UI, catalogue consistency); every item below was verified in the code before the fix.

FortiOS checks (`forti_config_parser.py`): `_tokens` no longer re-splits values on whitespace - the tokenizer keeps quoted names whole, so a group "SSL VPN Users" (or a member "vendor one") is now matched (before: `config_vpn_user_no_mfa` was silently never raised for any name with a space); `config_vendor_rule_permanent` looks policies up by the VDOM-qualified id in multi-VDOM configs (before: every SSL-VPN policy of a VDOM config was reported as permanent); remote-access groups include XAuth dial-up `authusrgrp` and the `users` of SSL-VPN policies; a RADIUS / TACACS+ server counts as an admin MFA means only when an admin uses `remote-auth` (a VPN RADIUS server alone no longer flags every local admin); SNMP communities of a switched-off agent (`system snmp sysinfo status disable`) are dead config; NTP custom servers are judged whenever configured (FortiOS defaults `ntpsync` to enable); HA remote link monitoring (`pingserver-monitor-interface`) counts as monitoring; `config_sslvpn_weak_tls` and the SSL-VPN authentication-rule path skip a disabled SSL-VPN; `config_no_dos_policy_ot` is judged only for firewalls with an OT leg (labelled L0-L2 interfaces or an into-OT upload boundary) instead of on every upload; `config_icmp_across_boundary` ignores `any` sides and says "between" interfaces / zones; `ntpserver` / `authentication-rule` sub-tables are shape-guarded; a variable named `_tokens` shadowing the helper (FortiToken table) renamed. PCAP evidence (`pcap_parser.py`, analyzers): the CIP / S7 identity is applied to the host that SENT the reply (a sender id carried through `_add_detail` / the stream; before, a port heuristic put an RSLinx scan's reply - 44818 -> 44818 - on the scanner), only for `direction == "response"`; the SSH-1 kind needs the server side (port 22) and "SSH-1.99" (SSH-2 with v1 compatibility) is not weak; the HTTP `Server:` banner is read from the header block only; the FTP / POP3 user name is kept as "is a default user" + a SHA-256 digest, never verbatim, and `_is_default_pair` flags only default users (user == password alone, "anonymous", "ftp" no longer count); S7 SZL 0x0011 version fields follow the Siemens layout (Ausbg = 'V' + major, Ausbe = minor.patch -> "V3.2.6") and 0x001C indexes 2 (module name) / 5 (serial, filled into the inventory) / 7 (type); userdata responses carry `direction`. Reviews (`alert_engine.py`): `review_sis_isolation` skips family-wide (`all`) destinations - every Internet-access rule had raised a high finding -, judges a rule's source by resolving each token (`_wide_source_tokens`: narrow only when contained in an allowed single host; unresolvable tokens stay wide), keys rule findings on the logical rule (VDOM + policy id, else the rule's addresses) instead of the row id, uses /64 for IPv6 networks, reads the hosts once and ignores inventory-excluded hosts; `_TRANSIENT_OUI_WORDS` lost Realtek, Panasonic, Toshiba, TP-Link and D-Link (NIC / industrial vendors) and matches on word boundaries. Config store: multi-VDOM backups declare the VDOMs first (`edit root / next`) - `find_block` now takes the `config vdom` block that holds content (`_vdom_blocks`; before, every snippet and settings block on a multi-VDOM FortiGate said "not in the stored configuration"); `mask_secrets` covers Cisco `username ... algorithm-type ... secret 9`, `snmp-server host ... <community>`, SNMPv3 `auth` / `priv` passwords, IKEv2 keyring `pre-shared-key local|remote|address ... key`, `key-string` (while `key 1` of a key chain is kept), OSPF / NTP / BGP / HSRP-VRRP / PPP authentication keys, `failover key`, `server-private ... key`, `ldap-login-password`, `wpa-psk`; XML tags by suffix (`<prv>`, `<rocommunity>`, `<ldap_bindpw>`, `<snmp-community-string>`, `<shared_key>`, legacy hashes); FortiOS `passwd-policy` / `password-expire` names are no longer masked; a finding whose device name matches several firewalls (HA peers) takes the one whose stored config holds the policy. Routes: a repeated `inventory_excluded=true` keeps the recorded reason / author / time. Console: `openConfigDrawer` ignores a slower earlier load (`configDrawerSeq`) and, on configs over 4000 lines, builds a section's lines only when it is opened (`ensureSection`; find / go-to-line open and render on demand). Report plumbing: `_CHECK_INPUTS` for the four new reviews (no data -> "no data", not PASS), `transient_device_in_ot_zone` in `_LEVEL_CHECKS`, `config_vpn_user_no_mfa` in `CHECK_SECTIONS` (direct-user variant). Catalogue: README check table regenerated from `CHECK_TYPE_REFERENCE` (179 rows; it had 67 of 55 claimed), the two missing reference rows (`engineering_access_unapproved`, `access_path_enumeration_truncated`) added, `docs/IACS_Monitor_62443_check_reference.xlsx` "Checks" / summary sheets regenerated (141 -> 179), `docs/finding-explanations.md` count. Noted, not changed: `cross_fw_same_permit_both_sides` is catalogued in the report / routes tables and documented as not raised; `default_credentials_observed` / `weak_tls_observed` fall in the report group "network" like `cleartext_credentials`; the finding routes take a finding id without a site (A-01). Tests: new cases in `test_batch_a_checks` (RADIUS-only, SNMP agent off, ping-server monitor), `test_batch_b_checks` (names with spaces, `authusrgrp`, SSL-VPN off; the DoS test uploads with an into-OT boundary), `test_batch_c_pcap` (Siemens version layout, 0x001C indexes, client SSH banner, SSH-1.99, body "Server:", 44818 -> 44818 identity on the sender, hashed user), `test_config_store` (Cisco / XML / FortiOS masking forms, VDOM declaration stub), `test_inventory_exclusion` (repeated exclude); `test_cov_e2e` uploads once more after labelling so label-dependent config checks are in the snapshot.

## Network Topology page 2026-10-16

Owner: "a second page based on flat network that used the swimming lanes from the vlan and/or subnets. So the host would not be defined by Purdue model but by functions, like PLC's. This is to show flat networks that do not have a mature posture but Layer3 routing through a firewall" - "I would prefer a separate page, under the Host Topology page". `app/parsers/subnet_topology.py` `build(db, site_id)`: lanes from the firewall / routing-switch interface networks (most specific first; interface name, alias, VLAN, label), then the capture segments no firewall covers (`segments.capture_networks`, same layer-2 domain label), then switch VLANs for hosts with a VLAN but no network, inferred /24 (/64) lanes and an *external* lane; each host carries its function group (`FUNCTION_GROUPS`: controller, hmi, engineering, server, infrastructure, it, remote, unknown - from the device type), level, source, model / firmware, SIS flag, exclusion; per lane the function counts, `mixed` (controllers share it with other functions) and the Subnets & Zones declaration covering it. `separators`: for every pair of lanes with observed sessions (capped at 50 000 rows) what sits between them - `firewall` when both lanes are interface networks of one firewall (the enabled policies whose src / dst zone names either interface or its FortiOS zone, with the permit count), `routed_switch` when that device is a Cisco routing switch, `flat` when both lanes share a capture layer-2 domain, `external`, else `unknown`. Route `GET /topology/subnets?site_id=` (view only). Console page *Network Topology* (nav sub-item under Host Topology): lane cards with header chips, function-coloured host cards (click / Enter opens the asset drawer; changes reload the page), *show inferred / external lanes* toggle, the connections panel with legend, *Declare…* opening a drawer that posts a Subnets & Zones declaration for the lane's subnet. Tests: `tests/test_subnet_topology.py` (function groups; lanes, interfaces, counts, mixed flag, host order, inferred and external lanes, lane order, firewall separator with policy ids / permit count / protocols, unknown and external separators; declaration on the lane; an empty site), `test_cov_routes.test_subnet_topology`. Smoke: `nettopo` section (lanes, cards, separators, toggle, drawer, declaration; written, not run). Docs: README route + Web UI row, user manual (page table, 4.7).

Same day, owner: "does the new page show pcap communications?" -> "do 2 for this page. create another subpage on host topology that addresses #3". **Option 2** - `build` now returns per host its observed `flows` (up to `MAX_FLOWS_PER_HOST` = 40, largest sessions first: peer id / ip / lane, direction to / from, protocol, port, transport, packets, cleartext-login kind, `same_lane`, `between` = the lane pair's separator kind) and per lane `intra_sessions` / `intra_protocols`; `flows_truncated` counts the dropped ones. Console: a `↔ n` badge on each host card with conversations (or the F key on a focused card) expands the list in the card - peer, protocol / port, "same lane" or "via firewall / routing switch / flat / nothing known between", cleartext logins in red. **Option 3** - *Lane Matrix* page (second nav sub-item under Host Topology): the same route rendered as lanes x lanes - each cell the session count between the two lanes coloured by separator kind (firewall blue, routing switch orange, flat red, nothing known grey, Internet), the diagonal the sessions inside the lane, hover for protocols and permit / policy counts, a toggle for inferred / external lanes, a legend. No host detail by design. Tests: `test_subnet_topology.test_per_host_flows_and_intra_lane_sessions`; smoke `nettopo` (flow badge) and `lanematrix` (rows, cells, kinds vs pairs, toggle). Docs: README (route, Web UI rows), manual.

Egress lane (owner: "how can an egress lane be added to these new pages?" -> "private will be a sub-group within the egress lane"). `subnet_topology._egress` runs `external_comms.flows` under the site's analysis scope and groups the rows by destination: name (best capture name, else the key), organisations, category, `private` (the corporate side beyond the firewall: External comms treats RFC 1918 addresses as inside by design, so these are built here from the sessions to hosts with a private address outside every known network and no MAC - seen only behind a router; reviews via `review_for` keyed by the address), addresses, aggregate review `status` (approved / remove / mixed / unreviewed), sessions / attempts, the inside `sources` (host, lane, level, ports, per-source status, `ot` = External comms' OT judgement or an OT lane), `ot_sources`, `left_via`; `by_lane` per source lane (sessions, attempts, `ot`, OT sources, top destinations, categories, Internet / private counts); `wan_interfaces` (firewall interfaces with role wan, routing switches excluded); a summary. Public addresses are no longer placed as lane hosts (the former "External / Internet" lane is gone; they are destinations). Console: the lane *Egress / outside the site* after the host lanes - header with the WAN leg(s), destination / Internet / private counts, approved count and an *OT sources* chip in red; sub-groups *Internet* and *Beyond the firewall (private addresses)*; one card per destination (name, category, organisation, status chip, red border when an OT host reaches it) with a `↔ n` badge listing the sources (OT in red, lane, ports, status); clicking a card opens the review drawer (sources table with Approve / Remove per source, *Approve for every host* / *Mark for removal*, note, name) posting to `/external-comms/review`, so the records are the External comms ones (an approval never closes an OT egress finding - owner's rule 7); *show egress lane* toggle. Lane Matrix: an *Egress / outside the site* column with sessions + attempts per lane, red (`egress-ot`) when the lane or a source host is OT, blue when the lane is a firewall interface network and a WAN leg is known, grey otherwise, hover with counts / top destinations / categories; legend entry. Tests: `test_subnet_topology.test_egress_lane_destinations_sources_and_review` (Internet and private destinations, order, sources with lanes and OT, summary, by_lane, no WAN leg, a review posted through the External comms route shows on the lane) and the earlier tests adjusted (no external lane / separator). Smoke: `nettopo` (destination cards, review drawer, toggle), `lanematrix` (egress column, OT cells).
