## Firewall Ports page 2026-10-17

Owner: "If the firewall has vLAN or subnets mapped to a port it is reflected. the purpose is I would like to see a visible picture of firewall and what subnets are associated with what ports or which ports have vlan tags with what subnets" -> "can communications between the subnets be shown based on the rules, where a mouse over the port lane will show the communication to other port lanes. the valuable information is the network services such as tcp/22" -> mock-ups shown -> "build". `app/parsers/port_topology.py` `build(db, site_id)`: for every firewall / routing switch of the site the ports in config order - physical interfaces, aggregates as one port naming their members (membership is not stored on `FirewallInterface`, so it is read from the stored config text, `_aggregate_members`), VLAN sub-interfaces placed on their parent port (`parent_interface_id`, else the `name.tag` prefix; an unknown parent becomes a port of its own); loopbacks / tunnels / vdom-links are not ports. Per port: `kind_label` (untagged · access, trunk · n VLANs, access + n VLANs, aggregate · a + b, WAN · egress leg, HA heartbeat, dedicated management, unused), status / role / HA / mgmt flags and the `subnets` riding on it (interface, VLAN tag, address, network, alias, FortiOS zone - omitted when the zone row merely repeats the interface name, the interface level or the covering Subnets & Zones declaration, hosts on the network - inventory-excluded hosts not counted). `links`: the enabled permit rules expanded to interface pairs (a FortiOS zone name maps to its member interfaces; alias names resolve too), per pair the services as `proto/port` (`rule_match.parse_ports` / `format_ports`) or `ALL`, each with a kind - cleartext / mgmt / cleartext-mgmt from the engine's `_CLEARTEXT_PROTO_PORTS` and `_MGMT_PROTO_PORTS`, any, icmp, ok - the policy numbers and how many captured sessions between hosts of the two interfaces used that service (`ALL` counts every session), `into_ot` when the destination interface is L0-L2 and the source is not, a warning count. Deny and disabled policies are not drawn. Route `GET /topology/ports?site_id=` (view only). Console page *Firewall Ports* (third nav sub-item under Host Topology): one panel per firewall; a port card per port with the kind line and one chip per subnet (VLAN tag, network, level badge, zone, alias, host count; click opens the lane on Network Topology); hover / focus a port and every other port of that firewall lights up - green border = policies into the hovered port, orange = out of it, both = both ways, faded = no permit policy - with the services as chips under the direction line (cleartext / management red, ALL amber, ICMP grey; inter-VLAN permits on the same trunk under the hovered port as "between its own VLANs"); click pins the policy table (direction, peer, services, policy buttons opening the policy row on Firewall Devices, sessions seen per service, notes); *show disabled ports* and *table view* toggles; legend. Network Topology lane headers now read `fgt-plant-t / port2.20 · VLAN 20 on port2 (alias)` (`parent` on lane interfaces). Tests: `tests/test_port_topology.py` (helpers; config order, aggregate members, trunk / access / WAN / disabled kinds, zone, alias, host counts, permits only, zone expansion, services and kinds, policy numbers, captured sessions, intra-trunk link; `into_ot` from interface labels; declaration on an unlabelled VLAN subnet; empty site and site scoping), `test_cov_routes.test_port_topology`. Smoke: `fwports` (panels, ports, chips, hover peers and service chips, pin table, disabled toggle, table view, chip -> Network Topology lane; written, not run). Verified in the browser on the seeded demo plus the Plant T config: port2 shows `access + 1 VLAN` with `vlan_22`, hover lights port1 / port3 / port4 / port5 with their services (`tcp/3389` red, `ALL` amber), the pinned table lists 8 policies / 3 warnings and policy 23 opens on Firewall Devices. Full suite 2770 passed, 11 skipped (14 min). Docs: README (route + Web UI row), user manual (page table, 4.7), CLAUDE.md.

## Audit of the topology pages (Network Topology, Lane Matrix, Firewall Ports) 2026-10-17

Owner: "code audit". Four independent reviewers (subnet-topology builder, port-topology builder, the three console pages, docs / tests / generator); every confirmed finding was re-checked and fixed the same day. **Builders.** (1) `subnet_topology.build` ran the routing-switch lookup and `segments.capture_networks` outside the site's analysis scope: another site's capture segments appeared as lanes and a routing switch in any non-default site was drawn as a firewall - `build` / `port_topology.build` now wrap `_build` in `analysis_site(db, site_id)` and derive the switch set from the site's own firewalls. (2) Policies naming several interfaces (`srcintf "port2" "port3"` is stored as `port2,port3`), a zone, `any` or no interface (ASA inbound ACLs) never matched: Network Topology said "no permit policy" between lanes while sessions flowed, Firewall Ports dropped the link - both builders split the side on "," and treat `any` / empty as every interface of that firewall (Firewall Ports marks such links `any_side`, the pinned table notes it). (3) Zones came from the legacy `FirewallZone` rows matched by firewall *name*: PAN-OS / ASA (which write only `FirewallZoneDef`) produced no links, and a second firewall whose device name equals the first's name stole its zones. New `port_topology.zone_memberships` (zone definitions keyed by firewall id, VDOM-qualified names included; a firewall without definitions - older uploads, the demo seed - falls back to the legacy rows of its own site, only for interfaces it has); both builders use it. (4) A FortiGate address object persists as a MAC-less twin of a captured host; Network Topology drew both cards and counted both - twins fold into the real host (sessions on either row land on one card). (5) The session budget (50 000) was spent on egress sessions and `truncated` stayed False - the query now takes sessions with both ends in a lane and reports `truncated` from the rows fetched; Firewall Ports orders by packets and returns `sessions_truncated` (status line). (6) The WAN leg's public network, tunnels and loopbacks made empty lanes - skipped. (7) A network shared by a firewall and a routing switch took the kind of whichever row came first - the firewall decides. (8) Firewall Ports flagged cleartext / management only on single-port services: `tcp/23,80,443` was "ok" - `_svc_kind` walks the parsed ranges (`cleartext-mgmt` / `cleartext` / `mgmt`, `wide` for spans of 1024+ ports), and "seen in captures" matches sessions inside multi-port services. (9) ASA sub-interfaces are named by `nameif` with the physical name in the alias, so each became its own "trunk · 1 VLAN": the parent is taken from the `name.tag` prefix of the name or alias; a routing switch's SVI is a port of its own (`SVI · VLAN n`, not a trunk). (10) Hosts on a network two firewalls share counted on one of them only - counted on every interface of the most specific network. Dead code and unused imports removed. **Console.** Network Topology lanes and the Firewall Ports page did not scroll at widths >= 960 px (content below the fold unreachable); the policy button on Firewall Ports did nothing when Firewall Devices had not been loaded yet and the selected rule row had no visible style; the chip -> lane jump guessed a 1.2 s load and had no highlight style (now awaits the load; `.nt-lane.selected`); a switch-VLAN lane read "VLAN 30 · VLAN 30"; the chip tooltip lacked a separator; the pinned port's picture was lost on any hover / blur / table-view toggle (restored on leave and after re-render); the old site's pinned table survived a reload / site switch; a declaration from Network Topology did not invalidate Lane Matrix / Firewall Ports; Lane Matrix cells are now focusable with an accessible label; ports carry `role=button`. **Tests / docs.** Smoke `s_lanematrix` used `keys` before assigning it (every run would have failed); `s_fwports` picks a chip whose subnet is a lane. README / CLAUDE.md / the route docstring still claimed an "external lane" and "Internet" separators that the egress lane replaced; CLAUDE.md smoke count (22 sections, three not yet run), migrations count (48), Layout lines for the two builders; the check reference's count line explains the supplemental row (179 + 1 = 180 rows). An empty `iasc_monitor.db` stub (0 tables) appeared in the repo during the audit (an audit script opened the default URL) and was deleted; the operator's database is not in this checkout. New tests: `test_port_topology` (multi-interface / any / multi-port services, zone definitions and the name clash, shared network counts + ASA sub-interface parent, SVIs and site scope) and `test_subnet_topology` (site scope, multi-interface and any policies, WAN lane, twins; session budget). Not changed (design, for the owner): `into_ot` ignores L3 destinations; two different firewalls between two lanes still report "unknown"; operator-declared subnets without firewall / capture coverage are not lanes; the `private` sub-group uses "not globally routable" rather than External comms' internal ranges; `/topology/subnets` runs External comms' flow analysis per request (slowest GET on a large site). Full suite 2776 passed, 11 skipped (13 min).

## Report: one write-up per check, references instead of SR / FR / SL / checklist numbers 2026-10-17

Owner: "The same findings need to be grouped. each finding needs a generic write up of the finding concern, why its a finding and remediation advice. for fortinet firewalls add in the sample config settings in the remediation" -> sample page reviewed -> "from the report, remove the check list number, SR, FR, and SL. add in the 62443 standard or best practice reference, such as NIST or CIS. fortinet filled from the finding where possible (as in the DNS case); once per check all the firewalls and affected subnets/vlans or host placed into one finding per site; Fortinet for right now." New `app/writeup.py`: `SR_TITLES` (62443-3-3 SR 1.1 - 7.8 titles), `NIST_BY_FR` (SP 800-53 controls per foundational requirement, cited as the SP 800-82r3 OT overlay), `OTHER_62443` (2-1 NET / SPE, 3-2 ZCR references), `CIS` (CIS Fortinet FortiGate Benchmark topic per configuration check - topics, not item numbers), `CONCERN` (one-sentence concern where the first sentence of the explanation is not one), `FORTI` (a FortiOS CLI example per FortiGate configuration / rule check, `{placeholders}` such as policy id / name, source / destination interface, account, service, current value filled from the finding's evidence by `forti_example`, `<...>` where the finding does not say; several interfaces render as `"a" "b"`), `references(check, sr_text)`, `concern`, `writeup`. ## MFA coverage: local accounts, LDAP users, FortiManager 2026-10-18

Owner: "does the application understand that fortinet access ftm is an MFA" / "does the app detect if the FortiGate built-in authentication server" / "I'm looking to make sure the MFA findings covers local and fortinet manager server is used" -> "build it with your recommended severities, 3 as a finding". (1) `config_admin_no_two_factor` now fires for every enabled local administrator without `two-factor` and without `remote-auth`, whether or not the firewall has tokens or a RADIUS / TACACS+ server (the old gate hid password-only admins on firewalls with no MFA means at all); **high** when the account holds `super_admin`, medium otherwise; evidence `auth_source` ("local (built-in user database)"), `mfa_kind`, `mfa_available`. `config_no_central_auth` stays the firewall-level finding. (2) `config_vpn_user_no_mfa` treats a local user of `type ldap` without `two-factor` and an LDAP server named directly as a group member as password-only (an LDAP password is one factor); RADIUS / TACACS+ servers and members keep counting as covered (they can enforce MFA server-side); evidence `auth_sources` per flagged user, `mfa_kind`; `remote_members` lists only the covered members. FortiToken Mobile is recognised as before (`set two-factor fortitoken` with an `FTKMOB…` serial), as are hardware tokens, FortiToken Cloud, e-mail and SMS. (3) New check `config_fortimanager_admin_access` (SR 1.1, SR 1.13; row 9.4; **info**, **low** when the `fmg` address is public or a public name): a FortiManager-managed device can be administered through the FortiManager tunnel with FortiManager accounts, so the MFA that protects that path lives on FortiManager; the finding names the address, stays open while `type fortimanager` is set (acknowledge once MFA on FortiManager is confirmed) and closes on a re-upload without central management. Registered in `CHECK_TYPE_REFERENCE`, `FORTIGATE_CONFIG_CHECKS`, explain (why / fix / close), write-up (concern, CIS topic, FortiOS example with the FortiManager-side `two-factor-auth` and `fmg-source-ip`), `CHECK_SECTIONS` (snippet: central-management + admins), check reference (180 + 1 rows), README table (180 entries), workbook regenerated. Tests: `tests/test_mfa_coverage.py` (admins always listed, super_admin high, no gate; LDAP user and LDAP-server member flagged, token user and RADIUS member covered; FortiManager info / low / closes), batch A / B expectations and the e2e snapshot updated (the sample config's local admins are now listed). Full suite 2791 passed, 11 skipped.

## Audit of the report, MFA and report-details work 2026-10-18

Owner: "audit the code" -> "build". Four independent reviewers (report generator + write-ups, MFA parser + report-details backend, the console exercised in a browser, docs / tests consistency); every confirmed finding re-checked and fixed. **Report generator.** (1) The all-sites roll-up in the public layout printed a blank cover and a "Placeholder for table of contents" page (the public page set has no cover drawing and no TOC pass) - `build_rollup_story(data, layout)` uses the public cover and no Contents for `public`. (2) A blank page between Engagement Particulars and 4 Findings (two page breaks) - removed. (3) `_set_layout` mutates module-level styles while renders run in the FastAPI threadpool, so two overlapping requests with different layouts could swap colours - `render_pdf` now holds a render lock. (4) FortiOS examples are `Preformatted` and never wrapped, so long lines overflowed the column / frame - `maxLineLength` 84 in the write-up block, 104 in the analysis entries. (5) Multi-interface values were rendered `vlan_20" "vlan_22` inside comments - the quoted form is used only where the template quotes the placeholder, a plain `a, b` list elsewhere. (6) The LRQA roll-up repeated "4 Findings" / "5 Analysis" per site and could collide with "6 Appendix" - per-site numbering (`i.1 Findings`, `i.2 Analysis`, entries `i.2.n`, appendix numbered after the sites). Placeholder sources widened (`zone`, `addresses`, `idle_timeout_seconds`, `portals`, `ot_interfaces_without_dos_policy`, wireless `interface`). **MFA / backend.** A remote-auth admin whose remote group resolves to an LDAP server or password-only users is now flagged (consistent with the VPN rule; RADIUS / TACACS+ groups stay covered; unknown groups are not judged). The FortiManager "public name" heuristic treated every internal FQDN as public - names are reported, not judged (`fortimanager_public: null`, info); only IP literals decide low. `/reports/details` rows: non-dict entries no longer consume slots, values are scalars only with control characters removed, `side` normalised, 50 client POCs + 50 assessors kept separately (the console caps each list at 50 and says so). **Console.** Enter on a Firewall Ports table row kept the pin but lost keyboard focus (focus restored); the egress review drawer did not show the new status after Approve / Remove (re-opened on the fresh destination); the Report details card showed the previous site's values when no site was selected (cleared) and kept a red message colour after a reload; a failed Firewall Ports load could leave the previous site's pinned table on screen (hidden before the fetch); dead `external` lane / separator code removed; remove buttons labelled for screen readers, a new row focuses its first field. **Docs / tests.** Workbook regenerated after the MFA description change; README route row and CLI table gained `layout`; the README / manual report descriptions no longer describe the FR x SL matrix and the old structure as the default; the Reports & Diagnostics rows mention the layout choice and the report details; CLAUDE.md smoke count (21), Layout block (writeup, config_store, report_checks, findings_workbook) and the "Adding a check" checklist (writeup entries, CHECK_SECTIONS, evidence row, workbook, manual). Verified in the browser: Enter on a table row keeps focus with the pin open; the POC list stops at 50 with a message. New tests: `test_writeup` (quoting), `test_report_details` (public roll-up cover / no placeholder, LRQA roll-up numbering, no blank page), `test_mfa_coverage` (LDAP remote-auth admin, FortiManager name, row sanitising). Not changed (owner's call): `config_no_central_auth` still fires alongside the admin and FortiManager findings on a FortiManager-managed box with local admins; `remote_members` also lists unknown group members.

## config_auto_firmware_upgrade 2026-10-19

Owner (after asking what the app makes of `config system fortiguard`): "Judge auto-firmware-upgrade: enable on an OT firewall is a change-control concern (firmware changes outside a maintenance window) and would fit a low finding under SR 7.6." New check `config_auto_firmware_upgrade` (SR 7.6, IEC 62443-2-1 SPE 2; row 9.12; **low**; config_review): raised when `config system fortiguard` sets `auto-firmware-upgrade enable` - the firewall replaces its own firmware when FortiGuard publishes a release, outside the site's change control; `auto-firmware-upgrade-day` / `-start-hour` / `-end-hour` become the `upgrade_window` and `-delay` the `delay` of the evidence (the reason names the window). Only an explicit `enable` is judged (older FortiOS has no such key; a backup with the key at its default prints it, so a 7.2+ device with the default `enable` is reported). Registered everywhere (`CHECK_TYPE_REFERENCE`, `FORTIGATE_CONFIG_CHECKS`, explain, write-up concern / CIS topic / FortiOS example `set auto-firmware-upgrade disable`, `CHECK_SECTIONS`, evidence-page row "Automatic firmware upgrade", check reference 181 + 1 rows, README table 181, workbook regenerated). Test `tests/test_auto_firmware.py` (enable with a window -> low with the window in the evidence; disable / absent -> nothing; registration).

## NTP sources placed against the interface networks 2026-10-19

Owner: "can the app confirm that the NTP is local based on the subnets on interfaces, so in this case is `set server 10.168.1.5` local?" -> "build it". `_config_findings` now places every custom NTP server: **local** when the address is inside a network of this firewall's interfaces (the evidence names the interface and the operator's Purdue level), **internal** when a static route reaches it or it is a private address with no interface / route, **external** when it is a public address or a name (pool names cannot be resolved offline). The context carries `local_nets` (from the interface addresses, with the labels from the database), `routed_nets` (from `config router static`) and `iface_levels`, built at ingest. `config_ntp_fortiguard` keeps its name and now covers: `type fortiguard` (info with a WAN leg, low without, one step higher when `server-mode` serves OT interfaces) and `type custom` with at least one external server (medium with a WAN leg, low without; **high** when no local / internal source exists and the firewall serves time to OT interfaces - the plant's clocks then depend entirely on the Internet). The evidence lists `servers` with kind and place, `external_servers`, `local_servers`, `server_mode`, `served_interfaces`, `served_ot_interfaces`; the title names the external sources and the local ones. `_extract_dns_ntp` returns `ntp_server_mode` and `ntp_server_interfaces`. `config_ntp_unauthenticated` is unchanged. Explain / write-up (FortiOS example keeps the internal source, adds authentication and server-mode), check reference, README limitation 9 rewritten, CLAUDE.md. Test `tests/test_ntp_source.py` (local + pools + routed -> medium with the interface and route named; internal only -> silent; pools only with server-mode on an L1 interface -> high; FortiGuard with OT server-mode -> low; the readiness tests keep FortiGuard info / low).

## Uplink evidence, link-monitor note, remote-site placement 2026-10-19

Owner (tabletop of `config system link-monitor` and `config router static`): build the three suggestions. (1) Evidence page, Availability section: "Uplink monitoring (system link-monitor)" (each monitor's source interface and probe targets) and "Default routes (router static)" (device, distance, dynamic gateway) - the other half of the resilience picture next to HA monitoring. (2) New **informational** check `config_link_monitor_external` (SR 7.1, SR 7.6; row 7.4): raised when every enabled link monitor probes a public third-party host (address or name); silent as soon as one probe targets an internal or ISP address; evidence lists the monitors; the FortiOS example adds an ISP-side target. (3) NTP placement: static routes now carry their device, blackhole routes are ignored, and a destination reached through an IPsec phase1-interface (or an interface of type tunnel) is placed as **remote** - "remote site via ipsecgw (static route 10.250.1.0/24)" - which counts as an inside source for the severity but no longer reads as a site-internal server; the title says "also inside" instead of "also local". The Firewall Devices rule list already labels tunnel sides ("VPN · site-to-site IPsec in"), so nothing changed there. Registered everywhere (catalogue 182, FortiGate check set, explain, write-up, snippet sections, check reference 182 + 1 rows, README 182, workbook). Tests `tests/test_uplinks.py` (evidence rows; public-only probes -> info; an internal probe silences it; NTP server behind the tunnel -> remote).

## Wireless in OT: placement, PSK, WEP 2026-10-19

Owner: "wireless in the OT environment needs to be flagged and confirm no vlans are using weak passphrases or WEP" (with the `wireless-controller setting / arrp-profile / wids-profile / ble-profile` sections, which carry no SSID and are evidence only). Before: `config_wireless_weak_security` judged the SSID's security mode alone (open / WEP / WPA1 / captive portal -> medium) and `config_wireless_not_isolated` looked for interface types that FortiOS never uses for SSIDs (`vap-switch` was missing), so where an SSID landed was never known. Now `_hardening_findings` receives the config context and places every VAP: tunnel mode -> its own `vap-switch` interface (and that interface's label); bridge mode (`local-bridging enable`) -> `vlanid` -> the VLAN sub-interface with that tag and its label; an SSID is **in OT** when that interface is labelled L0-L2. New check **`config_wireless_in_ot`** (SR 1.6, SR 5.1; row 2.5; **high**): the SSID lands in an OT network - evidence `mode`, `lands_on`, `interface`, `vlan_id`, `purdue_level`, `pmf`, `passphrase_set`, `managed_aps`. `config_wireless_weak_security`: open / WEP / WPA1 / captive portal stay medium and become **high** in OT; a WPA2 / WPA3 pre-shared key (`wpa2-only-personal`, `wpa3-sae`, …) on an OT SSID is a new **low** finding - the passphrase itself is stored `ENC` so its strength cannot be read, which the finding says; enterprise (802.1X) modes raise nothing. `config_wireless_not_isolated` now also matches `vap-switch` interfaces. Evidence page gains a "Wireless (row 2.5)" section: SSIDs with security and landing VLAN, the number of managed APs (`wireless-controller wtp`), and the WIDS profile assigned per AP profile (the pasted default WIDS profile - AP scan, weak-WEP-IV, deauth / flood detection - is reported there; it is a detection control, not judged). Registered everywhere (catalogue 183, FortiGate check set, explain for both checks, write-up concern / CIS topic / FortiOS examples - 802.1X with PMF, re-bridging to a wireless VLAN -, snippet sections, check reference 183 + 1 rows, README 183, workbook). Owner: "does the wifi check that WiFi is enabled?" -> "build it": each SSID gets an `active` state - the wireless controller is on (`system global wireless-controller`), the SSID is assigned to a non-disabled radio of a `wtp-profile` (`vaps` list or `vap-all`), and at least one managed AP (`wireless-controller wtp` with admin enable) exists or a FortiWiFi profile (name `FWF…`) stands in for the built-in radio; an inactive SSID is reported as **info** "defined but not broadcast (<reason>)" under the in-OT check when it would land in OT, else under the security check, so dead configuration is still cleaned up; the evidence page shows the controller state. Owner's note recorded in the explain text of both wireless checks and in the manual (4.2): the in-OT judgement needs the wireless VLAN / SSID interfaces labelled on the FW Topology Builder, as every placement check does. Test `tests/test_wireless_ot.py` (bridged SSID on an L1 VLAN -> in-OT high + PSK low; WEP on it -> high; the same SSID on an unlabelled VLAN -> medium only, no in-OT; tunnel-mode SSID on a labelled vap-switch; evidence section; registration).

## FortiLink-managed FortiSwitches 2026-10-19

Owner (after the `switch-controller security-policy 802-1X` tabletop and "how would a fortinet switch parser look?"): "build it, FortiLink-managed first". New `app/parsers/fortiswitch_parser.py`, run as a pass of the FortiGate ingest (never fails it; `stats["fortilink"]`): every `switch-controller managed-switch` entry becomes a switch of the site (`name`, else the serial), its `ports` become `SwitchInterface` rows - mode access / trunk (allowed VLANs, `allowed-vlans-all`, trunk type, FortiLink uplink), native VLAN id and name resolved through the FortiGate's VLAN interfaces, allowed list, description, the new `port_security_policy`, `admin_status` and `uplink` columns (migration `b4c5d6e7f8a9`), the uplink's neighbour = the FortiGate and its FortiLink interface - with `source_file` `fortilink:<firewall>` so a re-upload replaces them; the VLANs become `SwitchVlan` rows (port counts) carrying the Purdue label of the FortiGate's VLAN interface (`purdue_level_source` `fortilink_label`, never over a manual level). L2 only (owner's rule: Cisco only for L3). Two periodic reviews: **`switch_port_no_nac`** (SR 1.2, SR 2.5; row 2.4; low, medium when a port sits on a VLAN labelled L0-L2; one finding per switch; enabled access ports with no port-security policy, uplinks / trunks / ports set down excluded; the 802.1X policies are read for the evidence) and **`switch_unused_port_enabled`** (SR 7.7; row 5.7; low; enabled access ports with no description, no neighbour and no MAC in the uploaded MAC tables). Firewall Ports: `fortiswitches` on the FortiLink port (card line "FortiSwitch: …"). Registered everywhere (catalogue 185, explain, write-up with FortiOS examples - a NAC policy bound to the port, `set status down` -, `_CHECK_INPUTS`, auto-resolve map, review list, coverage map, check reference 185 + 1 rows, README ingest row + table, manual 4.5, workbook). Tests: `tests/test_fortiswitch.py` (rows, uplink, trunk, label flow, replacement; reviews through `/reviews/run`; the Firewall Ports chip; registration), `test_cov_alert_engine.test_review_fortiswitch_ports`. Same day, owner: "build the standalone FortiSwitch configuration upload (vendor_hint=fortinet)": `switch_parser` detects FortiSwitchOS (`config switch interface / physical-port / vlan / trunk`, `#config-version=FS`) as vendor `fortinet` / config type `fortinet_running_config`; `parse_fortinet_switch_config` reads the hostname, `switch vlan` names, `switch interface` (native VLAN, allowed VLANs as lists / `20,30` / ranges, `security-mode` -> port-security policy, FortiLink port), `switch physical-port` (admin state, description) and `switch trunk` (LAG and member ports = uplinks); rows carry `source_file` `fortiswitch:<file>`; the hardening checks gain a FortiOS-style branch (`config system snmp community` names - read-only communities - and `allowaccess … telnet` on an interface); the two port reviews match both `fortilink:` and `fortiswitch:` rows. Console upload vendor *FortiSwitch (standalone)*; README, manual. Tests in `tests/test_fortiswitch.py` (parser; API ingest with the hint and with auto-detection; NAC / unused reviews; public community and telnet hardening findings; a Cisco hint on a FortiSwitch file is rejected).

## IPsec phase 2 selectors on the Access Review; VPN resilience evidence 2026-10-19

Owner (tabletop of `vpn ipsec phase2-interface` / `phase1-interface`): "tighten the Access review page with the selectors; add the resilience evidence". `forti_config_parser.extract_tunnels` reads every phase1-interface (peer `remote-gw`, WAN `interface`, `monitor` = backup of, dynamic, IKE version) and its phase 2 entries (`src-subnet` = local side, `dst-subnet` = remote side; a missing side is 0.0.0.0/0) and every `system ipsec-aggregate` (members' selectors unioned, peers / WAN legs listed); the result is stored on the tunnel's `FirewallInterface.tunnel_info` (JSON column added to the unreleased migration `b4c5d6e7f8a9`). **Access Review**: `tunnel_selector_map` builds one remote-side address spec per (firewall, tunnel interface) - tunnels with an any-selector are left unbounded - and `_narrow_by_selectors` intersects a rule's source (when `srcintf` names the tunnel) or destination (when `dstintf` does) with it before the chain walk, so a policy "all from ipsecgw" shows as "10.250.1.0/24", the wider text stays as `policy_scope`, the row carries the new flag `tunnel_selector`, and a policy that permits nothing inside the selectors makes no row (the chain walk drops the empty intersection). Console flag list / help and the design doc's flag table extended. **Evidence page** (Availability): "Site-to-site IPsec tunnels" (name, WAN leg, peer, selectors) and "Redundant VPN tunnels" (a tunnel that `monitor`s another, two tunnels to the same peer over different WAN legs, aggregates with their members). On the owner's config: `sec-ipsec backs up pri-ipsec (wan2 / wan1)`, `aggregate ipsecgw = pri-ipsec + sec-ipsec`, selectors `172.17.201.95/32 <-> 10.250.1.0/24`. Test `tests/test_tunnel_selectors.py` (extraction incl. the aggregate; the Access Review row narrowed with the flag and policy scope; a policy outside the selectors makes no row; the evidence rows).

## Address objects tighten and clarify the checks 2026-10-19

Owner (tabletop of `config firewall addrgrp` and the user / token blocks): "to confirm the app is using this data to tighten and clarify checks" and the clarified fix for the dial-up VPN groups. Four changes. (1) **Local-in**: `config_local_in_permits_all` resolves the `srcaddr` objects and groups (recursively, as the policy expansion does) and records `sources_resolved`, `source_count`, `external_sources` (public addresses, FQDN / Internet-service tokens) and `wide_sources` (/16 or wider); the title says "(n address(es), m outside the site)" and the severity is high when an outside interface accepts any source or a source outside the site. (2) **Dial-up tunnels as a bounded conduit**: `extract_tunnels` gives a dynamic tunnel one selector - remote = the client pool (`ipv4-start-ip`-`ipv4-end-ip`), local = the resolved `ipv4-split-include` networks (the only destinations the clients route into the tunnel); `tunnel_selector_map` now returns (remote, local) per tunnel and the Access Review narrows the other end by the local side for dynamic tunnels only (a site-to-site local selector may be a pre-NAT VIP address and must not narrow a post-NAT policy); such rows carry `tunnel_selector`. (3) **VPN MFA**: policies whose source interface is a dial-up IPsec tunnel (IKEv2 EAP binds the groups on the policy, not on the phase 1) contribute their `groups` / `users` to the remote-access set (`ctx.dialup_tunnels`); a new evidence section "Remote access and user groups (row 4.3)" says per group who authenticates its members - local, local + token, RADIUS / TACACS+ server, or "LDAP / SAML / FSSO server (MFA not visible)" - instead of silently treating a server member as covered. (4) **Object names as hints**: a host created from a /32 address object takes the object's name as its hostname when none is known, and `device_classification.classify_object_name` suggests a device type (plc / controller / ppc / dcs, rtu, ied, hmi, scada, historian, mes, ntp, dns, jumphost, engineering) or a role (switch, ap) from the name tokens, recorded with source `object_name`, ranked just above the interface label and never over a manual or stronger classification. Docs: Access Review flag table, CLAUDE.md. Tests `tests/test_object_data.py` (name classification and the hosts; the resolved local-in sources and severity; dial-up groups in the MFA finding and the evidence row; the dial-up selector and the bounded Access Review row).

## FortiOS address-object types: interface-subnet, star FQDNs, EMS tags, public names 2026-10-19

Owner (tabletop of `config firewall address`, then a full real configuration run through the application to reflect on it). Four corrections. (1) **`type interface-subnet`** objects ("vlan_20 address", "dmz", "bond0 address" - FortiOS creates one per interface) resolved to nothing: the resolver only knew `ipmask`, so every policy using one carried an unresolved token (uncertain in the reviews, fail-open). `_addr_objects(parsed)` now completes them - a static subnet is used as written, the empty form (`0.0.0.0 255.255.255.255` = "whatever the interface has") takes the network of the named `system interface`; an interface without an address stays unresolved. All three registry builders go through it. (2) A **`*.name` in a plain `fqdn` object** (FortiOS accepts it; `wildcard-fqdn` is the newer type) was judged a plain FQDN (medium) - now a wildcard (high) in `fqdn_in_policy`, token `wildcard-fqdn:*.name`. (3) **`type dynamic` / `sub-type ems-tag`** objects (EMS_ALL_UNKNOWN_CLIENTS, FCTEMS_ALL_FORTICLOUD_SERVERS: membership decided by FortiClient EMS at run time) resolve to `dynamic:<name>` instead of `unresolved:<name>` - still uncertain for the reviews, but no longer reported as a missing object. (4) **Object names never classify a public address** - "Google_DNS1" (8.8.8.8) came out as the site's DNS server and a cloud jump host as a plant jump host; a global address keeps the object name as hostname and gets no device type / role from it. On the owner's configuration (not kept in the repository): 1982 rule rows with no uncertain address token, the local-in policies name their five outside sources, the wildcard objects give three high FQDN findings. Tests `tests/test_address_types.py` (resolver tokens incl. the interface network and the untouched parsed tree; rule rows and the high wildcard finding through the API), `tests/test_object_data.py` extended (public address unclassified).

## Tunnel-reached sources are remote; FortiTrust ID is MFA 2026-10-19

Owner: "build the two proposed items. IP address reached through tunnels (ipsec, vpn, etc) should be considered remote". (1) **Source placement** - a new `_place_source(token, ctx)` places every address of a policy against this firewall: `local` (an interface network), `internal` (a static route through a non-tunnel interface), `wide` (an object that contains one of the firewall's networks, e.g. 10.0.0.0/8 over a 10.168.x site), `remote` (behind a tunnel: inside a site-to-site phase 2 remote selector, a dial-up client pool, an SSL-VPN tunnel pool, or routed through a tunnel interface - `ctx.tunnel_nets` built by `_tunnel_nets(parsed)`), `external` (public address / FQDN / Internet service) or `unplaced` (a private address the firewall has no interface or route for). Remote, external and unplaced are *outside the site*. `config_local_in_permits_all` uses it: the title counts every outside source, the evidence carries `remote_sources`, `unplaced_sources` and `source_places` (token -> where), the reason says how many are on the Internet / behind a VPN tunnel / unroutable, and the severity is high when an outside interface accepts any of them (was: public addresses only). On the owner's configuration the two WAN local-in policies now read "6 address(es), 6 outside the site" - the VPC network behind the IPsec aggregate counted. The NTP placement keeps its own helper (same rules, test-pinned wording). (2) **FortiTrust ID** - `_saml_idps(parsed)` reads `config user saml` (IdP host from `idp-single-sign-on-url` / `idp-entity-id`, the phase 1 tunnels that use it through `ike-saml-server`); an IdP on `fortitrustid.forticloud.com` is Fortinet's cloud identity service with MFA by design. The user-group evidence row says "SAML (FortiTrust ID, cloud MFA)" for such a member (other SAML / FSSO providers stay "server (MFA not visible)"), a new row "SAML identity providers (user saml)" lists each provider with its host, MFA status and the tunnels it authenticates, and the VPN MFA finding names SAML members in `remote_sources` (`saml (FortiTrust ID, cloud MFA)` / `saml server (MFA not visible)`); the check's verdict is unchanged - SAML members were never counted as password-only. Explain texts and check-reference rows of both checks updated. Tests in `tests/test_object_data.py`: the local-in title / `remote_sources` / `source_places` with a site-to-site selector in the group, `_place_source` for every kind and `_tunnel_nets` (selector + dial-up pool), FortiTrust SAML group (only the local user without a token is weak; the IdP table; both evidence rows).

## Audit of the .36 additions 2026-10-19

Owner: "audit the code". Four read-only reviewers over everything changed since .35 (FortiGate parser; FortiSwitch and switch reviews; Access Review selectors and classification; UI, docs and registration), every claim verified before the fix. Fixed, with tests in `tests/test_audit_36.py` unless noted:
- **FortiGate parser.** A local-in source group that contains `all` is now `any` (high on an outside interface; was medium, "0 outside"). The "Default routes" evidence row reads the full-backup form `set dst 0.0.0.0 0.0.0.0` (was always "not set"). `config_wireless_not_isolated` reads zone membership from `config system zone` (the interface never carries `set zone`; the check fired for every tunnel-mode SSID even inside a Wireless zone). `_place_source` tests the firewall's own networks before the tunnel selectors (a /8 hub selector no longer makes the site "remote") and names EMS-tag / cycle tokens instead of "unresolved". The NTP placement also looks through the tunnel selectors and pools (a server behind a selector with no static route is remote, not "private, no route"). Phase 2 selectors in the `*-addr-type name` (address object / group) and `range` forms are read; a dial-up tunnel's mandatory all-zero phase 2 no longer undoes its pool bound (the Access Review lost the dial-up narrowing on every real full backup). A bridged SSID whose VLAN tag exists on two parent interfaces takes the labelled one. Site names (`.local`, `.lan`, `.internal`, `.corp`, `.home`) are not third-party probes for the link-monitor note. `extract_tunnels` runs once per ingest (`ctx.tunnels`), the address registry once per local-in pass and once per tunnel extraction; the NTP server-mode interface list accepts the nested-table form.
- **Access Review.** The tunnel-selector ContextVar is bound for the enumeration and reset afterwards (the scheduler thread kept the last site's map); the narrowing is computed once per candidate; a side is left alone when it shares no address family with the selector (an IPv6 policy on a tunnel with IPv4 selectors produced no row); the `tunnel_selector` flag is set only when a side actually changed.
- **Classification.** The object-name table matches whole tokens with veto tokens ("Domain_Controller", "Wireless-Controller", "SMTP_Relay", "Fire_Panel" give nothing) and drops the bare "sw", "ap", "eng", "jump" tokens; `object_name` is a weak source for Subnets & Zones declarations; a host whose device type already agrees is not re-sourced on every upload (the interface-label / object-name ping-pong inflated `hosts_labelled`). Object names never classify a public address (fixed earlier the same day).
- **FortiSwitch.** ISL ports to another FortiSwitch (`isl-peer-*` keys, neighbour recorded with protocol `isl`), user LAGs and their member ports are uplinks (they were judged as access ports by both reviews). Switch VLAN names and labels come from the VLAN names the ports reference, not from the first FortiGate interface with that tag. A re-upload replaces everything the firewall's pass wrote before, including switches that left the config (their findings now auto-resolve), and a same-named switch written by another firewall's pass (one row per site / switch / port is the schema; this used to fail the whole upload with a UNIQUE error). The VLAN level source is `firewall_propagated` (the source the level machinery knows; `fortilink_label` was overwritten on the next classify anyway). Trunk rows without VLANs store NULL; the reviews match `fortilink:` / `fortiswitch:` exactly; malformed VLAN ranges (`20-`) are skipped instead of raising on `/ingest/switch`; the NAC review's docstring no longer promises open-auth inspection.
- **Report / registration.** `config_wireless_in_ot` is in `_LEVEL_CHECKS` (needs labels: "no data", not PASS); `switch_port_no_nac` cites SR 1.2, SR 2.1 (SR 2.5 is session lock) in the catalogue, README, reference and workbook; the two switch reviews declare a `switch_ports` report input; the firmware FortiOS example prints "was enable (window sunday 2:00-4:00)"; the switch examples fill `{port}` from `first_port`; explain / reference / report-group texts say "FortiLink-managed or standalone FortiSwitch"; the Access Review flag help covers dial-up tunnels; README Web UI rows mention the FortiSwitch picker and the Firewall Ports line; CLAUDE.md line counts.
- **UI smoke.** `tests/ui_demo_server.py` seeds a second Plant A firewall (FW-LINK: a FortiLink-managed FortiSwitch, an L2 FortiLink VLAN, an L4 site-to-site tunnel with phase 2 selectors); `ui_smoke_index.py` checks the FortiSwitch vendor option, the "FortiSwitch:" line on Firewall Ports and the `tunnel_selector` filter and row. Written, not run (Playwright not installed); the seed verified by API.

Full suite after the audit: 2844 passed, 11 skipped (14 min). Not yet packaged: .36 shipped before the audit; the fixes above are in the source tree for the next package.

Noted for the owner, not changed: a VLAN tag reused on two parent interfaces of one FortiGate is one VLAN for the pre-existing topology pass (level by id); a policy naming a FortiOS zone that contains a tunnel, and intra-zone rows, are not narrowed by the selectors; a dial-up spoke without a pool uses its phase 2 local side as the bound; the NAC review treats any bound policy as protection (open-auth / MAC-auth-bypass content not stored); the unused-port review has no FortiSwitch MAC-table input; multi-VDOM local-in ids collide (pre-existing); "ppc" stays a controller token (the owner's plants). *2026-11-09: the zone-to-tunnel narrowing (zones made only of tunnels), the open-auth content and the multi-VDOM local-in ids were built in the owner batch below; intra-zone rows, the spoke bound, the MAC-table input, the reused tag and "ppc" stay as noted.*

## Audit checklist report: the Master Audit Checklist workbook -> PDF + CSV 2026-10-24

Owner: "Using this pdf report as a template I'm looking to create a program that takes the ISA62443_master_audit_check_list.xlsx file as an import and creates a report. The report should have an CSV export besides a pdf. The report finding should have the Check title as the finding title, audit check as the finding details, domain, the auditor fill in sections for Finding observation, Evidence reviewed need to be in the finding, the Recommendations should be the findings recommendations."

- **`app/audit_checklist.py`** reads the workbook (`parse_workbook`: the *Master Checklist* sheet, else the first sheet whose header row has Check title / Audit check / Status; columns matched by header text, missing ones noted; status and severity normalised case- and hyphen-tolerantly, dates to ISO, control characters dropped, 4000 characters per cell, 2000 rows), decides which rows are findings (`is_finding`: Non-compliant / Partially compliant, or a recorded finding / severity / recommendation on a row not rated Compliant / Not applicable - reported as Not assessed with a note), `select_rows` (scope findings / assessed / all), `order_rows` (severity then checklist, or checklist), `domain_summary` (the Summary sheet recomputed: counts, (C + 0.5 P) / (C + P + N), high findings), `report_data`, `to_csv` (one line per finding, UTF-8 BOM, B-18 formula guard), `summary_json`.
- **`generate_audit_report.py`** (repo root) renders the PDF in the LRQA layout, reusing `generate_report.py`'s cover drawer (now takes `title_lines`; the compliance report's title is unchanged), header / footer, `_ReportDoc` contents, distribution list and revision history builders, styles and tables: 3 Engagement Particulars (background, scope, constraints, finding summary tiles by severity, Compliance by Domain), 4 Findings (ID, title, domain, status, severity, section), 5 Analysis (one page per finding: meta table, *Audit Check* = details, *Finding / Observation*, *Evidence Reviewed* + the checklist's suggested evidence, *Recommendation* as numbered items (lines, else sentences), *Further Reading* = Covers / source standards / NIST reference; "No ... was recorded by the auditor" when a yellow cell is empty), 6 Appendix (every check by domain with status / maturity / severity, the severity and status scale, the standards + the workbook's NIST references sheet). CLI with `--csv`, `--scope`, `--order` and the cover fields; `report_bytes(parsed, fmt, ...)` for the route. Renders under `_RENDER_LOCK`.
- **Route** `POST /reports/audit-checklist` (multipart `file`; `format` pdf / csv / json; `scope`; `order`; optional `site` / `site_id` -> the site's Report details on the cover, distribution list, revision history and constraints). The upload is read through `_read_limited` (10 MB) and never stored; a non-checklist upload is 422.
- **Console** Reports & Diagnostics card *Audit checklist report (xlsx -> PDF / CSV)*: file input, *Findings* scope, *Order*, *Check workbook* (preview: counts per status / severity, workbook notes, the finding list), *Download report (PDF)*, *Download findings (CSV)*; `auditChecklist` / `renderAuditPreview` (`el()` / `textContent` only). Smoke `audit_checklist` in `s_system` (builds a two-row workbook with XSS payloads, previews, downloads CSV and PDF).
- Owner 2026-10-24 (second ask): "the program needs to ask for client POC to add into the Distribution list, can have several Client POC; LRQA consultant name, also can have several LRQA persons; ask for author; ask for Prepared for; ask for date of report". CLI: `prompt_details` asks on a terminal for Prepared for, Author (default IACS_Monitor), Date of the report (default today), then the client POCs and the LRQA consultants one person at a time (name / role / email, empty name ends the list) - only for what the flags `--client`, `--author`, `--date`, `--poc`, `--consultant` (repeatable, `'NAME; ROLE; EMAIL'`) did not give; `--no-prompt` / no TTY = flags and defaults only. Route: multipart form field `details` (JSON; `audit_checklist.clean_details` sanitises - scalars, 50 people per side, `pocs` / `consultants` shorthands - and `merge_details` lays it over the site's Report details; a non-object is 422). Console card: Prepared for, Author, Date of the report, Client POC rows and LRQA consultant rows (the `rdRow` widget with the `ac` test-id prefix), pre-filled from the site's Report details (`acPrefill`) and sent as `details`; nothing saved. Report: own `build_distribution` ("<client> - points of contact", "LRQA consultants") and `build_revisions` (default row dated with the report date); the cover date and the constraints default text use `report_date`.
- Owner 2026-10-25 ("use the image for the report's cover page logo"): `ui/lrqa_logo.png` (the LRQA logo the owner supplied) is drawn on the audit report's cover in place of the wordmark box (`_draw_cover` takes `logo` in the cover dict; `generate_audit_report.LOGO`, falling back to the wordmark when the file is missing). The compliance report's cover and every page footer keep the IACS Monitor wordmark. `make-dist.sh` now ships `generate_audit_report.py` (it was missing from INCLUDE_FILES).
- Tests `tests/test_audit_checklist.py` (parsing, header tolerance, refusals, selection and order, summary, CSV, PDF text incl. the distribution list, report date and the cover logo, details cleaning / merging, CLI flags and prompts, route incl. report details, the `details` form field and the 422s), `test_cov_routes.test_audit_checklist`. Docs: README (route table, Web UI row, *Audit checklist report* CLI section, layout), user manual §8, this tracker. Full suite 2026-10-24: 2854 passed, 11 skipped (14.5 min); the console card checked by hand in the browser (preview, CSV and PDF all 200, no console errors; Playwright still not installed, so the smoke section is written but not run).

## Release 2026.10.09.38 - 2026-11-09

Owner: "Housekeeping I would suggest: do this" (merge the audit-checklist branch, build .38 from the git checkout). Built from the git checkout `IACS_Monitor-github` (branch `main`, the `audit-checklist-report` work merged). Contents on top of .37: the Audit checklist report (Master Audit Checklist workbook -> LRQA-style PDF + CSV, route, console card, tests, docs), the three vendor syslog samples, `generate_audit_report.py` in the install tarball, and the generic labels in the test fixtures and notes. Found while verifying the first .38 image: the Dockerfile did not copy `generate_audit_report.py`, so `POST /reports/audit-checklist` and four of its tests failed inside the images (the install tarball already had the file); fixed and rebuilt. 185 checks, no new migration (head `b4c5d6e7f8a9`). Full suite 2855 passed, 11 skipped (19 min); `test_5k_new_hosts_fast_path_timing` failed under load and passed alone (timing budget, as noted in CLAUDE.md). Playwright smoke not run (not installed). Tarballs built in a Linux container from the git checkout (`.git` excluded, top-level folder renamed to `iasc_monitor`); images from the extracted full tarball, `--target runtime` / `--target dev`. Install and full tarballs with sha256 (`sha256sum -c` clean); images `iasc-monitor:2026.10.09.38` / `latest` and `:2026.10.09.38-dev` / `dev`. Verified on a fresh volume: `/health` ok, `/ui` 200, no site on first start, runs as `iasc`, `alembic current` = `b4c5d6e7f8a9`, no pytest in the runtime image, 185 checks; the Plant T config loads, both PDF layouts render, the site survives a restart, no traceback in the log; the dev image runs `test_audit_checklist`, `test_audit_36`, `test_fortiswitch`.

## Owner batch 2026-11-09: address-group exclusions, VPN-sourced ALL, AV by destination, NAC open-auth, tunnel zones, local-in per VDOM

Owner (after the status report listing the open decisions): "build 1,3 and AV a with the plus items". Six changes, one migration (`c5d6e7f8a9b0`, head), no new check. 185 checks.

1. **Address-group `exclude-member`** (`forti_config_parser._resolve_address`, `_group_exclusions`, `_subtract_addresses`, `_groups_with_exclusions`): a group with `set exclude enable` resolves to its members MINUS the resolved `exclude-member` objects (concrete networks only; `all` counts as 0.0.0.0/0 in an IPv4 group; FQDN / range / unresolved / dynamic tokens on either side are left alone). The cut is inherited by every group that contains the excluding group. `exclude-member` without `exclude enable` is inert, as on FortiOS. Rows expanded from such a policy carry the note "srcaddr|dstaddr group X excludes Y" in the stored rule text; `stats["addrgrp_exclusions"]` counts the groups cut. Before, "All_OT minus Historian" resolved to all of OT and every check and Access Review row on it was wider than the firewall itself.
2. **VPN-sourced all-service permits are high** (`_config_findings`, `config_permit_any_any_any`): when the service is `ALL` and the policy's source interface is a remote-access tunnel - a dial-up IPsec phase 1 (`ctx.dialup_tunnels`) or an SSL-VPN interface (`ssl`, `ssl.<vdom>`) - the finding is high whatever the destination (critical when all three positions are `all` as before). Evidence `from_remote_access`, `remote_access_interfaces`, `identity` (the policy's groups / users), `severity_reason` "every service from the remote-access tunnel X". `dialup_ipsec_names` also accepts a dynamic phase 1 with `set usrgrp` (IKEv2 user group) as remote access - it authenticates users like `authusrgrp` / EAP / mode-cfg do.
3. **AV on file-transfer policies, option (a)** (`_hardening_findings`, `config_av_missing_on_file_policy`): judged by the DESTINATION. When every destination interface of the policy (zone members resolved) carries an operator label of L0-L3 the finding is informational ("inline AV inside OT is informational" in the title, `destination_in_ot`, `dst_level`, `severity_reason`); into the DMZ / enterprise (L3.5 and above) or unlabelled it stays medium. Catalogue default unchanged (medium).
4. **NAC open-auth content** (`SwitchInterface.port_security_mode`, migration `c5d6e7f8a9b0`; `fortiswitch_parser.policy_mode`; `switch_parser.parse_fortinet_switch_config`; `alert_engine.review_switch_port_nac`): the bound 802.1X policy's content is stored as `802.1x`, `802.1x-mab` (mac-auth-bypass) or `open-auth` (FortiLink: `switch-controller security-policy 802-1X` `open-auth` / `mac-auth-bypass`; FortiSwitchOS: `security-mode` plus the `config port-security` sub-block). A port whose policy has open-auth enabled is unprotected (unauthenticated devices are admitted) and counts like a port with no policy; MAC authentication bypass alone still counts as protection and is listed as `mab_ports`. Evidence per port `policy`, `mode`, `why`; `open_auth_ports`, `no_policy_ports`; the title says "(n no policy, m open-auth)". A policy name the config does not define leaves the mode NULL and the policy counts (unknown is not open).
5. **Zone-to-tunnel narrowing** (`access_paths.tunnel_selector_map`): a FortiOS zone (`FirewallZoneDef`, kind zone, members resolved) whose members are ALL tunnel interfaces with usable selectors is keyed like a tunnel - the union of the members' remote selectors, no local bound - so a policy naming the zone instead of the tunnel is narrowed and flagged `tunnel_selector` the same way. A zone that mixes a tunnel with a LAN interface is not keyed (traffic may enter on the LAN member). An interface of the same name as a zone wins.
6. **Multi-VDOM local-in ids** (`_hardening_findings`, `config_local_in_permits_all`; ctx `vdom_views`): each VDOM's `firewall local-in-policy` table is judged on its own with that VDOM's address registry; `ref_key` `local-in:<vdom>/<id>`, title "Local-in policy 1 (vdom ot)", evidence `vdom`. Before, the flattened view kept the last VDOM's policy 1 only. `config_store._locate` passes the VDOM to the snippet lookup.

Docs: explain texts of the four checks, `docs/62443-check-reference.md` rows, README (addrgrp ingest row, inline checks line), `docs/access-path-review.md` and the console tooltip for `tunnel_selector`, the workbook regenerated, CLAUDE.md. Not changed: the Access Review has no `group_exclusion` flag (the rule rows carry the note in their stored text instead; FirewallRule has no notes column and the flag would have needed one), the spoke bound, FortiSwitch MAC tables and the VLAN-tag-per-parent keying stay open.

Tests: `tests/test_owner_batch_1109.py` (11: exclusions resolve / inherit / inert / `all` minus / FQDN kept; subtraction leaves unresolvable tokens; policy rows carry the note; VPN-sourced ALL high for dial-up and ssl.root, inside stays medium, single-service VPN policy only "source all"; AV info with L2 destination and medium into the L3.5 DMZ; open-auth ports unprotected with MAB listed, standalone FortiSwitchOS modes, review on stored modes; zone of tunnels narrowed and a mixed zone not, selector map keys; local-in per VDOM with two findings). `test_fortiswitch` expectation gains `port_security_mode`. Full suite 2867 passed, 11 skipped (13:44), 0 failed. Not packaged.

## Upgrade notes for existing installs

* Unversioned databases (created by the app before migrations were tracked) are handled automatically by `install.sh` and the Docker entrypoint: `alembic stamp 0001baseline0 && alembic upgrade head`. Manually, run the same two commands.
* The upgrade closes (never deletes) duplicate open findings that share a `source_ref`, keeping the oldest, with a note on each closed row.
* Disabled rules are now stored (flagged `enabled=false`) and reported as `disabled_rule` findings (3.8 / SR 7.7) after the next config ingest and review run.
* The console is now at `http://<server>:<port>/ui` (the bare host redirects there). Opening `ui/index.html` from disk is no longer supported.
* Tier 3: SQLite databases switch to WAL journal mode with foreign keys enforced on first start. Re-uploading a syslog file no longer duplicates events (only events uploaded after the upgrade carry a dedup hash). Rule-twin findings are re-keyed per logical rule: on the first review run after upgrading, old per-row twin findings are closed as superseded and replaced by one finding per rule. Duplicate sessions are merged by migration f6a7b8c9d0e1.
* Feature round: migrations a8b9c0d1e2f3 (NAT, host history, finding lifecycle, event vendor) and b9c0d1e2f3a4 (Purdue level columns → NUMERIC(2,1) with L3.5; any out-of-set level value is set to NULL with a warning). Re-import firewall configs to populate NAT rows, IPv6 rules and rule identity fields. On the first review run, old-meaning `conduit_matrix_drift` findings and per-row `unused_policy` findings are auto-resolved and replaced. `/purdue-levels` now returns `{levels, device_types}`.
* Multi-site phase 3 (migration f1a2b3c4d5e6): log uploads are routed per device. Lines from devices that are not registered are **held** (Devices page / `GET /site-devices/unmapped`) instead of going to the Default site - re-upload firewall configs (registers their hostnames) or map devices with `POST /site-devices` (replays the held lines), or pass `?site=` on log uploads to map unknown devices. Events stored before the upgrade stay where they are.
* Parser output changed: `dst_port` is now canonical (`any`, `22`, `80,443`, `1000-2000`) and disabled/negated rules are handled differently, so **re-ingest firewall configs** after upgrading to refresh rule rows and findings.
* Management networks (migration d2e3f4a5b6c7): `config_mgmt_not_restricted_to_mgmt_zone` is now one finding per interface - the old per-firewall finding is auto-resolved at the next config upload. Admin trusted hosts are stored from the next upload; until then `config_admin_trusthost_outside_mgmt` has nothing to judge for older firewalls.
* Full project review 2026-10-06 (migration d9e0f1a2b3c4): the two PostgreSQL GIN indexes are dropped; `defusedxml` is a new dependency (`pip install -r requirements.txt`). A FortiGate config re-upload now keeps the rules' owner / conduit / last-matched values. `generate_report.py` on a database with several sites needs `--site-id N` or `--all-sites`. The firewall name of a FortiGate upload without `firewall_name` is now the config's `set hostname` (the file name only when the config has none) - a firewall previously created under its file name gets a second row at the next upload unless the upload names it.
* Real-config review 2026-10-07 (migration e0f1a2b3c4d5): `firewall_interfaces.remote_access`. Re-upload FortiGate configs so FQDN-destination policies get rule rows, dial-up IPsec tunnels are marked, VLAN types are inferred and the new local-in / IPsec / service checks run. Lockout / idle-timeout findings raised for settings left at the FortiOS default are auto-resolved by that upload; `fqdn_in_policy` becomes one finding per policy (the per-object findings are auto-resolved).

* Docker 2026-10-07: dependencies now come from `requirements-lock.txt` (newer than the old pins - rebuild the image). `/data` is a declared volume and also holds `tmp/` (upload spool) and `backups/`. Compose names the volume explicitly `iasc_data` (`name: iasc_data`); a volume created by an older compose file under a project-prefixed name (e.g. `iasc_monitor_iasc_data`) is not picked up automatically - back it up first and copy it over, or set the old name in `docker-compose.yml`.

* Fresh install / review switch 2026-10-07 (migration `5a6b7c8d9e0f`): new table `app_settings`. Existing databases keep their sites (including *Default*); only new databases start without one.

* PCAP capture point 2026-10-08 (migration `6b7c8d9e0f1a`): four nullable columns on `pcap_captures`. Captures uploaded before the upgrade list as unassociated; associate them on the Upload page (or `POST /ingest/pcap/{id}/associate`) to re-attribute their hosts.

* Flat OT fixes 2026-10-08 (migration `7c8d9e0f1a2b`): new table `connection_attempts`, column `pcap_captures.segment`. Re-upload captures to get their segment and attempts; data-less TCP rows from earlier uploads stay sessions.
* Flat OT fixes FO-05 to FO-12 2026-10-08 (migration `8d9e0f1a2b3c`): column `network_sessions.cleartext_auth`; new check `session_remote_access_app`; device type `router` with source `capture_gateway`; `IACS_NTP_MIN_CAPTURE_SECONDS` (3600). Re-upload captures to record cleartext logins; on the first review run after the upgrade, open findings take the title and severity that run gives them (they can go down).
* Isolated OT fixes 2026-10-09 (no migration): new checks `multi_homed_host`, `rogue_dhcp_server`, `dead_default_gateway`. Re-upload captures to get layer-2 domains, DHCP servers, ARP-only hosts and unanswered-UDP attempts; one-way UDP rows from earlier uploads stay sessions. On the next review run, per-address unknown-device findings are replaced by one per MAC.
* Routed-OT group 1 2026-10-09 (no migration): captures now record 802.1Q VLANs (`hosts.vlan_source = "pcap_8021q"`), drop the routed copy of a packet in SPAN captures, and no longer create hosts for addresses that never answered. Re-upload a capture to apply this; hosts and packet counts from earlier uploads are not changed.
* Routed-OT group 2 2026-10-09 (no migration): run the reviews again after upgrading. Internet egress path findings appear at the lowest level that reaches the Internet, and `unused_policy` findings for firewall policies that only Internet traffic through a routing switch used are closed.
* Subnet zones 2026-10-09 (migration `9e0f1a2b3c4d`, new table `subnet_zones`): run `alembic upgrade head` (the installer and the Docker entrypoint do it). Open *Subnets & Zones* for each site, accept or set a level per subnet, then run the reviews.
* External communications 2026-10-09 (migration `1f2a3b4c5d6e`, new tables `observed_names` and `external_flow_reviews`): run `alembic upgrade head` (the installer and the Docker entrypoint do it). Re-upload captures to record the names they show; flows from earlier uploads are listed by address. Review the *External comms* page, approve the documented conduits, then run the reviews. To show organisation names, place an iptoasn file in the data directory (or set `IACS_IP2ASN_FILE`).
* Wording and severity (G4) 2026-10-09 (no migration): run the reviews after upgrading. Level-skipping sessions are re-titled and re-graded (L3 → L1 becomes high); sessions of classified engineering workstations become `engineering_access_unapproved` until the workstation is approved on Mgmt & Engineering; SNMP community findings are replaced by one per source. External-comms approvals of OT hosts' flows no longer close their egress findings.
* Follow-ups 2026-10-10 (no migration): re-upload FortiGate configs so the default-route interface is recorded (role `wan`), then run the reviews: transits to the Internet firewall and its Internet leg get automatic levels and Internet egress through the firewall is reported. Bookmarks to `/ui/topology` keep working (now the console's standalone Host Topology).
* FR / security level 2026-10-10 (no migration): nothing to do. `GET /findings/summary` and `/findings/category-reference` return extra keys (`target_sl`, `fr`, `by_security_level`, `by_fr`; `fr`, `security_level`), and the findings CSV export has two more columns (`fr`, `security_level`) after `isa_sr`.
* Severity order 2026-10-10 (no migration): `GET /findings` and the CSV export now return findings by severity; pass `sort=newest` for the previous newest-first order.
* Tabletop SF 2026-10-10 (migration `2a3b4c5d6e7f`: three nullable columns on `findings`): run `alembic upgrade head` (the installer and the Docker entrypoint do it). `GET /findings` rows and the CSV export carry `false_positive_reason` / `_by` / `_at`; `/findings/summary` `by_status` may contain `false_positive`. Declare the site NTP server on Mgmt & Engineering and run the reviews to close the time-sync findings. A host declared with one role keeps it; add further roles as new declarations.
* Inventory membership 2026-10-10 (no migration): `GET /assets` rows gain `in_inventory` / `inventory_evidence`; the assets CSV export has two more columns after `approved_at`.
* Interface label as host evidence 2026-10-10 (no migration): run `POST /classify` (or upload a capture) once after upgrading - hosts with no evidence of their own behind an operator-labelled interface take its level (`classification_source = "interface_label"`), then run the reviews. Session-level findings for those hosts appear or change severity on that run.
* Zones and conduits 2026-10-11 (migration `3b4c5d6e7f8a`: tables `iec_zones`, `iec_zone_members`, `iec_conduits`): run `alembic upgrade head` (the installer and the Docker entrypoint do it). Nothing else changes; the new page starts empty (Import from Subnets & Zones / FortiGate zones to seed it).
* SL-C and the inventory zone column 2026-10-11 (migration `4c5d6e7f8a9b`: `sl_c`, `sl_c_source` on `hosts` and `firewalls`): run `alembic upgrade head`. Re-upload the Asset Inventory CSV to place its hosts in zones (`zone` column) and record `sl_c`; `GET /assets` rows and the inventory CSV export gain `sl_c` / `sl_c_source`.
* SL-A and zone judgement 2026-10-11 (migration `5d6e7f8a9b0c`: `sl_a`, `sl_a_note` on `iec_zones` and `iec_conduits`): run `alembic upgrade head`. `GET /findings` rows gain `zone_judgement`; the findings CSV export gains three last columns (`iec_zone`, `zone_sl_t`, `zone_verdict`); the PDF gains the Zones and Conduits section when the site has zones (`include_zones=false` / `--no-zones` to leave it out).
* Documentation status and `undocumented_conduit` 2026-10-11 (no migration): run the reviews after upgrading - a site with IEC zones gets one `undocumented_conduit` finding per zone pair that communicates with no documented conduit (document the conduit on Zones & Conduits to close it). `zone_judgement` gains `documentation`; the findings CSV export gains `zone_documentation`.
* Conduit services (option 3) 2026-10-11 (migration `6e7f8a9b0c1d`: `services`, `sources` on `iec_conduits`): run `alembic upgrade head`. Nothing is lowered until a conduit's services are set. `GET /findings` rows gain `shown_severity` / `severity_adjustment`; the findings CSV gains those two last columns; the conduits CSV gains `conduit_services` / `conduit_sources`.
* Assets in several zones 2026-10-11 (migration `7f8a9b0c1d2e`: member uniqueness per zone): run `alembic upgrade head`, then the reviews - a host or network documented in two zones becomes a `host_in_multiple_zones` / `network_in_multiple_zones` finding instead of being refused. `POST /iec-zones/{id}/members` no longer answers 409 for an asset in another zone; the inventory upload stats key `zone_conflicts` is now `zone_multi`; `GET /iec-zones` gains `multi_zone_hosts`.
* SF-5 / SF-6 2026-10-11 (no migration): run the reviews after upgrading. New findings `blocked_cross_zone_attempts`, `conduit_service_blocked` (from stored connection attempts) and `policy_spans_multiple_conduits`; unidentified hosts on L0-L2 networks reaching across the firewall become `unknown_device`; rule-twin titles of multi-interface policies are re-worded on that run ("port1 (L1) + port2 (L3)").
* Asset inventory column mapping 2026-10-11 (migration `8a9b0c1d2e3f`: table `inventory_import_mappings`): run `alembic upgrade head`. The asset inventory upload response gains `mapping_source`, `columns_mapped`, `columns_ignored`, `mapping_problems` and the device-type counters; a device type that names an application type in other case or spacing is now stored as that type. Uploads are otherwise matched as before (field names and aliases; a few more alias spellings such as `IP_Address`, `MAC-Address`, `Asset Type` are recognised).
* Access Review roll-up / light console 2026-10-11 (no migration): nothing to do. `GET /access-paths` rows gain `group_dst` / `group_src`; the review screen rolls similar rows up by default (untick *Roll up similar paths* for one row per path); the PDF permitted-path tables print one line per group with the hosts listed. The console is light-themed.
* Firewall Devices policy lines and hovers 2026-10-11 (no migration, no API change): the rules drill-down shows one line per policy number by default (untick *One line per policy* for the expanded rows); hovering a zone / interface name shows its networks and hosts, hovering a policy line's Source / Destination cell lists its consolidated hosts.
* External DNS 2026-10-12 (no migration): run the reviews after upgrading - every enabled permit to an external DNS server becomes a `rule_dns_external` finding (high from an OT zone). Re-upload FortiGate configs: `config_dns_external` now names every external resolver, including the FortiGuard default when none is configured, so firewalls with an internal primary and an external secondary, or no DNS setting at all, gain the finding.
* Cleartext / management protocols 2026-10-12 (no migration): run the reviews after upgrading. `rule_insecure_protocol` / `insecure_protocol` are now `rule_cleartext_protocol` / `cleartext_protocol`: open findings under the old names are no longer re-derived (close them, or leave them - a fresh install has none). New `rule_mgmt_protocol` / `mgmt_protocol` findings appear for management protocols across zones; Telnet / SNMP give both.
* Access Review bulk note 2026-10-12 (no migration, no API change): nothing to do.
* Firewall rules classify 2026-10-12 (no migration, no API change): nothing to do. Levels set from the rules page are ordinary Subnets & Zones declarations (edit or clear them there).
* Shadow Rules policy cards 2026-10-12 (no migration, no API change): nothing to do; untick *One card per policy pair* for the old view.
* FW Topology Builder disabled interfaces 2026-10-12 (no migration): `/topology/state` interfaces gain `status` / `enabled`. Re-upload a config if an interface's admin state changed since its last upload.
* Interface aliases 2026-10-12 (no migration): `/firewall-zones` rows and `/topology/state` interfaces gain `alias`. Nothing to do.
* Logging / monitoring readiness 2026-10-12 (no migration): re-upload FortiGate configs - `config_no_syslog_forwarding` moves to row 6.3 and closes where FortiAnalyzer / syslog 2-4 / FortiGate Cloud forwarding exists, `config_monitoring_readiness` appears, FortiGuard NTP / DNS findings are re-graded (info / low).
* Report PASS / evidence 2026-10-12 (migration `9b0c1d2e3f4a`: table `check_evaluations`): run `alembic upgrade head`, re-upload FortiGate configs (evidence + config evaluation) and run the reviews; until then checks show NOT EVALUATED where data exists.
* Host VLAN from firewall interfaces 2026-10-12 (no migration): run `POST /classify` (or re-upload a config / capture) once - hosts without a VLAN take their firewall VLAN interface's id (`vlan_source` `fw_interface`).
* Reviews at upload time 2026-10-12 (no migration): uploads now start the site's periodic reviews in the background (`IACS_REVIEW_ON_UPLOAD=false` to keep the schedule-only behaviour). The weekly schedule stays as a safety net.
* Firewall Devices grouped counts / VPN 2026-10-12 (no migration): `/firewall-devices` gains `policy_count` / `disabled_policy_count` / `vpn_policy_count`, `/firewall-zones` gains `tunnel`; the card shows policies instead of expanded rows.
* Checklist rows 2026-10-12 (no migration): re-upload FortiGate configs so `config_ids_ips_missing` moves to 3.12 and the AV / FQDN findings lose their wrong rows.
* Any / temporary rules 2026-10-12 (no migration): re-upload FortiGate configs - policies with 'all' in one or two positions now raise `config_permit_any_any_any` (medium / high), temporary policies without an expiry `config_temporary_rule`.
* Disabled-interface admin access 2026-10-12 (no migration): re-upload FortiGate configs - management-access findings on admin-down interfaces drop to low.
* Access Review flags 2026-10-12 (no migration): `insecure_protocol` is now `cleartext_protocol` / `mgmt_protocol`, computed on the next page load; existing classifications keep their tags.
* Always one site 2026-10-13 (no migration): the console opens on the remembered site, else the default / first one; *All sites* is gone from the pickers (Sites overview and the PDF roll-up remain).
* Multi-homed hosts 2026-10-13 (no migration): addresses a Host Topology drag moved together with their MAC keep that level - drag each red card to its own level, or use Classify.
* FortiOS 7.x admin logins 2026-10-13 (no migration): events already stored are not re-evaluated - re-upload the syslog (duplicates are skipped, so clear the old events first) or wait for new logins.
* SL0 and conduit rules 2026-10-14: migration `ac1d2e3f4a5b` (run `alembic upgrade head` on an existing database) - SL-T checks allow 0, new table `iec_conduit_rules`.
* unknown_device and page approval 2026-10-14 (no migration): hosts already approved on the Inventory page stop being reported as unknown at the next review run.
* Report check selection 2026-10-14: migration `bd2e3f4a5b6c` (`alembic upgrade head`) - new table `report_check_exclusions`; nothing is excluded until a site deselects checks.
* Interface levels 2026-10-14 (no migration): hosts levelled by protocol / VLAN / LLDP on a labelled subnet move to the interface's level at the next classification run or when an interface is (re)labelled; set a host manually to keep a different level.
* Findings workbook 2026-10-14: new dependency openpyxl (in requirements-lock.txt; the Docker image installs it) - a source install needs `pip install -r requirements-lock.txt --no-deps` again.
* Stored firewall config 2026-10-14: migration `ce3f4a5b6c7d` (`alembic upgrade head`) - new table `firewall_config_texts`; configs uploaded before it have no stored text (the *Config* button / snippet say so) until the config is uploaded again.
* Inventory exclusion 2026-10-14: migration `df4a5b6c7d8e` (`alembic upgrade head`) - four columns on `hosts`, default not excluded; nothing changes until a host is excluded.
* Batch A checks 2026-10-15 (no migration): the 14 new `config_*` findings appear at the next FortiGate config upload; `config_monitoring_readiness` findings lose the implicit-deny gap (re-upload updates them) and `config_implicit_deny_not_logged` takes it over.
* Batch B checks 2026-10-15 (no migration): the five new `config_*` findings appear at the next FortiGate upload; `sis_zone_not_isolated` needs hosts marked *Safety function* on the Inventory page (or `is_safety_function` in the inventory CSV) and fires at the next review run.
* Batch C checks 2026-10-15 (no migration): the three reviews run at the next review; the PCAP evidence (default-credential kinds, weak-version rows, banners, identities) appears for captures uploaded after this change - re-upload earlier captures to get it.
* Firewall Ports 2026-10-17 (no migration): aggregate members come from the stored config text - a config uploaded before the config store (2026-10-14) shows its aggregate without members until it is re-uploaded.
* Topology audit 2026-10-17 (no migration): zones on the Network Topology / Firewall Ports pages come from the zone definitions a config upload stores (B-07); a config uploaded before B-07 falls back to the legacy zone rows.
* Report write-ups 2026-10-17 (no migration): the PDF layout changes for every site; nothing stored changes.
* Report template 2026-10-18: migration `f8a9b0c1d2e3` (`alembic upgrade head`) - new table `report_details`; the PDF uses the site name and defaults until the Reports page details are filled in.
* MFA coverage 2026-10-18 (no migration): on the next FortiGate upload every password-only local admin and LDAP-backed VPN user becomes a finding, and FortiManager-managed devices get the informational `config_fortimanager_admin_access`.
* Report details 2026-10-18 (no migration): saving the Report details card from the console was broken in .33 / .34 (`who is not defined`); fixed here - details stored through the API were unaffected.
* Audit 2026-10-18 (no migration): remote-auth admins on LDAP groups become `config_admin_no_two_factor` findings at the next upload; FortiManager findings with a name address drop from low to info.
* config_auto_firmware_upgrade 2026-10-19 (no migration): appears at the next FortiGate upload where `auto-firmware-upgrade enable` is set.
* NTP sources 2026-10-19 (no migration): `config_ntp_fortiguard` now also reports custom external NTP servers at the next upload; existing FortiGuard findings are updated in place.
* Uplinks 2026-10-19 (no migration): `config_link_monitor_external` appears at the next FortiGate upload where every link monitor targets a public host.
* Wireless in OT 2026-10-19 (no migration): `config_wireless_in_ot` and the PSK-in-OT finding appear at the next FortiGate upload once the wireless VLAN interfaces carry Purdue labels.
* FortiLink switches / IPsec selectors 2026-10-19: migration `b4c5d6e7f8a9` (`alembic upgrade head`) - three columns on `switch_interfaces`, `tunnel_info` on `firewall_interfaces`; the switches appear at the next FortiGate upload and the two port reviews at the next review run.
* Audit checklist report 2026-10-24 (no migration): new route `POST /reports/audit-checklist`, `generate_audit_report.py` and the Reports page card; the workbook is the input, nothing is stored.
* Owner batch 2026-11-09: migration `c5d6e7f8a9b0` (`alembic upgrade head`) - `port_security_mode` on `switch_interfaces`; re-upload the FortiGate / FortiSwitch configs so the modes, the address-group exclusions, the per-VDOM local-in findings and the new severities take effect at the next review. The old `local-in:<id>` findings of a multi-VDOM firewall close on the re-upload (their key changed).
